## D-088 — Home: scaled-up Data-Story hero

**Status:** shipped, small; pending push + live verification
**Contributors:** Kael, Vera, Axiom, Finn
**Date opened:** 2026-08-10 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-10

**Trigger:** owner asked for a more comprehensive review of the whole home dashboard, specifically raising whether a larger visual element (like the competitor sites) was needed — a direct follow-on to D-087's framing.

**Finding:** a full top-to-bottom screenshot review of production found ten home modules stacked at roughly equal visual weight. D-046 had already named the fix for this when it built the hero ("hero > live games > pennant-races-as-viz > headlines rail > ticker") but the shipped hero was never actually sized to read as dominant — modest padding, a 1.15rem headline, 24px team logos.

**Kael, on the literal "add a photo" reading:** rejected, for a sharper reason than "DESIGN.md says no" — ESPN/Athletic/Bleacher Report can run a daily photo because they have photo desks curating and captioning it; we don't, and an uncaptioned generic photo over live data reads as filler, undercutting "broadcast-grade" rather than reinforcing it. The instinct (something should visually dominate) is correct; the fix is scaling the *existing* generated hero, not adding an unsustainable module.

**Real bug found and fixed in the same pass:** a dead pre-D-046 `.home-hero` CSS block (`.home-hero-title`, `.home-hero-badge`, `.home-stats-strip`, etc. — confirmed via grep to be referenced nowhere in `js/`/`index.html`) had been silently leaking `text-align: center` into the live hero, since the D-046 rule that replaced it never reset the property. Removed the dead block plus two orphaned responsive overrides that would have fought the new sizing on mobile, plus one further orphaned override found later in the file. `text-align: left` now set explicitly.

**What shipped:** `.home-hero`/`.hero-*` rescaled (padding, headline, hook, board logos/scores roughly doubled in visual weight — full numbers in ISSUES.md), applies to all hero variants (MLB live/upcoming, standings fallback, NFL) since they share the same classes. Live MLB games gain a real new element — the base/outs/count diamond grid cards already show, reused via `Scorebug.normalizeMLBGame(g).liveHtml` rather than rebuilt — so the hero shows live-state detail beyond the score for the first time. No new fetch, no new data source, no behavioral change to hero selection logic.

**Deliberately not done:** no live-diamond equivalent for NFL's hero (no Scorebug live-state fragment exists for football yet — named, not built). No changes to the ten other home modules' relative weight — that's a smaller named follow-on (Vera), not part of this pass.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes, `tools/check-manifest.cjs` clean, `tools/check-themes.cjs` clean (2 pre-existing unrelated warnings only), full unit suite (33 tests) passing, grep-confirmed clean of every dead class name site-wide. Full spec and exact before/after values in ISSUES.md under "Home — Scaled-up Data-Story hero."

**Live-verified 2026-08-10 after push:** hero confirmed dominant on production (padding 36px, headline ~37.6px, `text-align: left` fixed, 52px logos, live diamond rendering during a real live game), console clean, no errors.

## D-089 — Bug: `ApiCache.invalidate('')` was wiping unrelated app state, not just cache — including a user's followed teams

**Status:** shipped, small
**Contributors:** Axiom
**Date opened:** 2026-08-10 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-10

**Trigger:** owner reported the home welcome banner ("Serious stats for serious fans...") was both showing up on repeat visits (should be first-visit-only, localStorage-gated) and reading as stale copy (MLB/NFL-only language on a site that's shipped NCAAF and NCAAB since). Investigating the "shows every time" half surfaced a real bug, not a display quirk.

**Root cause:** `ApiCache` (`js/cache.js`) namespaced its own localStorage keys under the bare prefix `"zs_"` — the same prefix nearly every *other* piece of persistent app state also uses: `zs_theme`, `zs_follows` (D-046's unified follow/favorite system), `zs_seen_welcome`, `zs_saved_stats` (custom stat-builder formulas), `zs_recents`, `zs_visits`, `zs_install_dismissed`, `zs_quest_streak`, and the legacy `zs_fav_teams`/`zs_mlb_favs`/`zs_favs` keys `_migrateLegacyFavorites()` still reads. `ApiCache.invalidate(prefix)` does a blanket scan — `Object.keys(localStorage).filter(k => k.startsWith('zs_' + prefix))` — and `invalidate('')` (used for a full cache wipe, called on season rollover **and, critically, automatically inside `ApiCache.set()`'s own quota-exceeded retry path**) matched and deleted *every* `zs_`-prefixed key, not just actual cache entries. Any time a user's browser hit localStorage quota during normal use (plausible on a site caching season stats/standings/leaderboards for 4 sports with TTLs up to 12h), the automatic eviction-and-retry silently wiped their theme choice, followed teams/players, saved stat formulas, and more — with no error, no toast, nothing to indicate data was lost beyond what the cache-quota warning already covers. The welcome-banner symptom the owner actually noticed was real but was the least consequential of the keys affected.

**Fix:** `ApiCache`'s internal namespace changed from `'zs_'` to `'zs_cache_'` — its own exclusive sub-namespace no other localStorage key in the codebase uses (confirmed via grep across `js/`). `invalidate('')` now only matches actual cache entries. No migration needed: old `zs_`-prefixed cache entries simply stop being read (cache misses regenerate them on next fetch, same cost as any normal cache miss) — this is a performance cache, not data, so there's nothing to preserve. Comment added directly on the namespace declaration explaining why it must never be widened back to something another key could share, since this exact bug is what happens when it is.

**Also fixed in the same pass:** the welcome banner subhead updated from MLB/NFL-only language to name all four live sports (MLB, NFL, NCAAF, NCAAB), matching the site's actual current scope per GOALS.md's Current State section. Headline tagline ("Serious stats for serious fans — no login, ever.") is the brand tagline per CLAUDE.md identity and was left unchanged.

**Not done, flagged for a future pass if it recurs:** no user-facing recovery or warning when this kind of eviction happens (the existing "Caching off" toast only fires on a second consecutive write failure, not on data loss from eviction itself). Worth a Cipher/Vera look if this class of silent-data-loss bug shows up again elsewhere, but this specific instance is fully closed by the namespace fix.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean on both changed files, 0 NUL bytes, `tools/check-manifest.cjs` clean, `tools/check-themes.cjs` clean (2 pre-existing unrelated warnings only), full 33-test unit suite passing.

**Live-verified 2026-08-10 after push.** Directly exercised the actual bug on production, not just confirmed the file changed: seeded `zs_follows`/`zs_theme` plus a real `ApiCache.set()` entry, ran `ApiCache.invalidate('')`, confirmed the cache entry was gone but `zs_follows`/`zs_theme` survived intact (49 `zs_*` keys before → 27 after, all deletions were real cache entries). Welcome banner confirmed showing the updated MLB/NFL/NCAAF/NCAAB copy. Console clean.

## D-090 — Home: three quick wins from external (ChatGPT) redesign feedback

**Status:** shipped, small; pending push + live verification
**Contributors:** Kael, Vera, Axiom, Finn
**Date opened:** 2026-08-10 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-10

**Trigger:** owner shared a ChatGPT-written homepage critique/redesign brief. Team fact-checked it against the live site and DECISIONS.md history first (Finn) rather than acting on it directly — several of its claims didn't match reality (Mock Draft promo isn't "buried at the bottom," Trending content already exists as Hot Right Now/Insights, sport cards already carry live status). Its top recommendation (replace the hero with a dense multi-game strip) directly contradicts D-088, shipped minutes earlier on the owner's own steer — flagged to the owner rather than silently resolved either way, per this project's "flag conflicting instructions" standard. Its "pack in 30-40% more, reduce whitespace" direction was flagged as a real posture fork against DESIGN.md's density/space rules, not a small tweak. Owner chose to proceed with the three ideas that didn't conflict with anything already decided, and asked for the rest to be scoped rather than built.

**1. Sport-picker cards show live/today counts.** New `_sportPickerCounts()` in `js/app.js` reads whatever `_updateHomeTicker` already fetched into `AppState.mlbGames`/`nflGames`/`ncaafGames` — no new fetch. Cards gain a second status line ("3 today · 1 live") only when there's a real count; when a sport has a live game right now, the card switches to the `.sport-card--live` pulse treatment that already existed in `css/main.css` but had no JS code path ever setting it. NCAAB excluded, same reason it's absent from D-087's merged ticker (no reliable per-day game data in `AppState` for it yet).

**2. Pennant Races: division-odds % becomes the visual star.** Was trailing text at the same tiny size as the games-back caption (`+0.5 on TEX · 52% div`) — exactly DESIGN.md's own "receipts" pattern (computed number should be the visual star, provenance secondary) applied backwards. New `.pr-stat`/`.pr-odds-pct`/`.pr-odds-lbl` render the odds as a large bold mono number with the gap distance now the small supporting caption underneath, not beside it.

**3. Search bar gets more visual weight.** Padding, font size, icon size, and text contrast all increased (`0.75rem/0.875rem` → `1.05rem/1rem`, icon 14px → 19px, muted → secondary/primary text color). Deliberately **no accent color added** — D-047 invariant #3 (brand orange is brand-only: logo, primary CTA, active nav) still applies; this is a size/contrast change, not new color vocabulary.

**Deliberately not done — scoped for the team instead, not built:** the hero-size conflict, the density-philosophy question, nav rename (Scores/Tools vs. Players/News), "SportStrata Intelligence" branding, and further Trending/analytics-forward ideas are written up as a framed question in ISSUES.md ("Home — Remaining ChatGPT-brief ideas, scoped not built") for Vera/Kael/Axiom/Folio to pick up deliberately, not implemented here.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes, `tools/check-manifest.cjs` clean, `tools/check-themes.cjs` clean (2 pre-existing unrelated warnings only), full 33-test unit suite passing.

**Live-verified 2026-08-10 after push.** MLB card correctly showed "10 today · 4 live" and switched to the `.sport-card--live` pulse class; NFL/NCAAF showed their season-phase status with no stats line (honestly empty — no games that day); NCAAB unaffected. Pennant Races showed "52%"/"60%" as large bold numbers with "+0.5 on TEX" as the small caption underneath. Search bar computed padding 16.8px/20.8px, font-size 16px — matches spec. Console clean.

## D-091 — Home: team walkthrough of the six scoped ChatGPT-brief ideas; shipped "Trending, last 7 days"

**Status:** shipped, small; live-verified with one real bug found and fixed during verification
**Contributors:** Kael, Vera, Axiom, Relay, Folio, Finn
**Date opened:** 2026-08-10 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-10

**Trigger:** owner asked the team to actually walk the six items D-090 had scoped-not-built, rather than leave them as framing. Full outcomes for all six are in ISSUES.md under "Home — Remaining ChatGPT-brief ideas, scoped not built" → "Team walkthrough outcomes." Summary:

- **Hero size:** resolved, keep D-088 — Today's Games already provides the comprehensive view the brief wanted; the hero was never competing with it.
- **Density philosophy:** deliberately deferred pending real Cloudflare Web Analytics data (live since 2026-08-09) rather than acting on an aesthetic read of two screenshots.
- **Nav rename:** not resolved — but surfaced that CLAUDE.md's nav documentation has drifted from the real `SUB_NAV_TABS` config (flagged for Folio) and that "Scores" is already present where the header ticker's SCORES button doesn't cover a sport (NCAAF/NCAAB), absent where it does (MLB/NFL) — not an inconsistency, correctly built. Recommends splitting the Analytics dropdown, not a blind rename.
- **"SportStrata Intelligence" branding:** confirmed buildable (raw material already exists, scattered across D-039/D-046/D-028/D-080) but scoped as a real feature, not built.
- **Search-bar centerpiece:** confirmed as a real new feature (first non-⌘K search surface) needing its own Vera states spec — not started.
- **Trending/rolling-window stats:** the one item worth shipping same-session — see below.

**Shipped: "Trending, last 7 days" in the home Insights rail.** Relay's finding made this a same-day build rather than a future spec: `AppState.mlbHotStats` (MLB Stats API `last7Days` split, via `fetchMLBLeagueStats`) already existed and was already being fetched for the Leaderboards page's Hot tab — this is a second consumer of existing data, not a new pipeline. `_renderHomeInsights()` now fires a new `_renderHomeTrending()` after its existing season-leader bullets, which ensures the hot-stats fetch, filters to a real sample (15+ AB / 5+ IP over 7 days — same fluke-noise discipline as the function's existing qualified-WHIP filter), and appends up to two bullets (best 7-day AVG, best 7-day ERA) in a visually separated block with its own "Trending, last 7 days" caption — DESIGN.md's receipts pattern applied to the timeframe claim, not just the number.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes, `tools/check-manifest.cjs` clean, `tools/check-themes.cjs` clean (2 pre-existing unrelated warnings only), full 33-test unit suite passing.

**Live verification found a real, pre-existing bug (Axiom, fixed same session).** On production, `#railInsights` rendered the season-leader bullets but `.rail-trending` never appeared and `AppState.mlbHotStats` stayed unset. Manually invoking `_renderHomeTrending()` surfaced the cause: `fetchMLBLeagueStats(..., 'last7Days')` was throwing `MLB API 400` — the real MLB Stats API rejects `stats=last7Days` on the league-wide `/stats` endpoint (that token is only a valid per-player hydrate `type`, not a top-level `stats` value). Both this new call and the pre-existing Leaderboards Hot-tab call (`js/mlb.js` ~4276) wrap the fetch in a swallowed `.catch()`, so the Hot tab has been silently failing (empty state, no console error) since it shipped — this feature just made the silent failure visible for the first time.

**Fix:** `fetchMLBLeagueStats()` now converts `last7Days`/`last14Days`/`last30Days` into `stats=byDateRange` with computed ET-adjusted `startDate`/`endDate`, leaving the `season`-type path untouched. Verified directly against the live API that `byDateRange` returns 200 with real splits where `last7Days` 400s. One fix in `fetchMLBLeagueStats` covers both call sites — no changes needed at either caller. Re-ran the full local verification suite (`node --check`, manifest, themes, 33 tests) clean; `sw.js` bumped to v169.

**Second bug found live-verifying the fix itself.** After push, `.rail-trending` rendered with real 7-day sample data (414 hitters, 400 pitchers — confirming the byDateRange fix worked) but the bullet read `Keibert Ruiz () is hitting .529...` — blank team tag, default dot color. Cause: the MLB Stats API never puts `abbreviation` on a split's `team` object for *any* `fetchMLBLeagueStats` response — `_fetchMLBLeaderSplits()` already knows this and runs an id→abbreviation `enrich()` step before storing `AppState.mlbLeaderSplits`, but `AppState.mlbHotStats` (populated separately, by the Leaderboards Hot strip and this Trending block) never got the same treatment. It was invisible until now because the Hot strip's own fetch had been silently 400ing this whole session — the two bugs were masking each other.

**Fix:** added `_enrichMLBTeamAbbr(splits, season)` in `js/mlb.js`, factoring `_fetchMLBLeaderSplits`'s existing enrich logic into a reusable helper, and wired it into both places that populate `mlbHotStats` (Leaderboards Hot Right Now, `_renderHomeTrending`). Local suite re-verified clean; `sw.js` bumped to v170.

**Live-verified after push — 2026-08-10.** Confirmed on production: real team tags and colors ("Keibert Ruiz (WSH)...", "Sandy Alcantara (MIA)..."), no console errors. Full detail including a third caching-layer gotcha found during verification (browser HTTP disk cache, distinct from both the SW's Cache Storage and Cloudflare's edge cache) is in ISSUES.md's entry for this fix.

## D-092 — WNBA as a 5th live sport: owner override of D-052's calendar-gap recommendation
**Status:** ratified (owner override) 2026-08-10 — scope + gates drafted this entry, build proceeding same session
**Contributors:** owner (direction/override), Vera (JTBD), Kael (visual fit), Axiom (architecture/feasibility), Relay (data contract, live-verified), Cipher (security)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-10 | **Date resolved:** —

**Trigger (owner):** direct request — "add the WNBA to the SportStrata website" — brought independently of D-052, not as a continuation of it.

**The conflict this entry documents:** D-052 (opened 2026-07-31, ratified earlier the same day as this entry, 2026-08-10) evaluated WNBA explicitly as a net-new-sport candidate against NBA/NHL revival, soccer, golf/tennis/F1, and Men's College Basketball — and passed on it in favor of NCAAB, which shipped (P1–P3) and live-verified earlier in this same session. D-052's stated reason for passing on WNBA: *"its season (May–Oct) sits entirely inside MLB's own season — it doesn't extend the barbell into a dead month, it just adds a second thing to build during the month MLB is already the flagship."* Live-verified today (Relay, see below): the real window is even wider than D-052 assumed — ESPN's own scoreboard payload reports 2026-04-03 through 2026-10-20, i.e. April–October, not May–Oct — meaning the calendar overlap with MLB (Mar–Oct) is *larger* than D-052's own framing, not smaller. This entry does not find new evidence that changes D-052's calendar-fit conclusion; it records that the owner reviewed the conflict directly (surfaced before any code was written) and chose to proceed with WNBA anyway. No superseding rationale was offered beyond the owner's direct call — this is logged as an explicit override, not a re-litigation that found D-052 wrong.

**Per-domain framing:**
- **Relay (data contract) — live-verified 2026-08-10, before any code written:** `site.api.espn.com/apis/site/v2/sports/basketball/wnba/scoreboard` returns real, populated, in-season data (confirmed against a live-in-progress game, Toronto Tempo @ Atlanta Dream, during this check) — identical top-level shape to NFL/NCAAF/NCAAB scoreboards (events → competitions → competitors → team objects with logo/color). `site.web.api.espn.com/apis/v2/sports/basketball/wnba/standings` returns a **flat two-conference tree** (Eastern Conference: 7 teams, Western Conference: 8 teams, zero division nesting) — genuinely simpler than NCAAB's ~32-conference recursive tree; the same `_collectConfs`-style recursive collector handles it without modification, it just terminates one level sooner. `/teams` confirmed 14+ active franchises including both 2025/2026 expansion teams (Golden State Valkyries, Toronto Tempo) — roster churn from expansion is a live-data problem, not a build-time one, since nothing is hardcoded. No polls/rankings endpoint exists for a pro league (unlike NCAAF/NCAAB) — Rankings is not in scope, unlike D-052's four-view NCAAB scope.
- **Axiom (architecture/feasibility):** direct clone of the NCAAB playbook (`functions/api/ncaab.js` → `functions/api/wnba.js`, `functions/api/ncaabstandings.js` → `functions/api/wnbastandings.js`, `js/ncaab.js` → `js/wnba.js`), if anything cheaper than NCAAB was relative to NCAAF — no poll/rankings view, no divisional nesting in standings. `SPORTS`/`SPORTS_META` registry needs one entry, not a new registry (D-042/D-052 already built the registry and the ESPN-proxy-clone pattern). One real gap found while tracing the clone target: NCAAB's own ticker function calls `Scorebug.normalizeNCAAFGame` on basketball game data — `Scorebug.normalizeNCAAFGame` hardcodes `sport:'ncaaf'` internally (`js/scorebug.js` line 124), so NCAAB's ticker items have silently carried the wrong league glyph (🏈 instead of 🏀) and `data-sport="ncaaf"` attribute since D-052 shipped (ISSUES.md already flags NCAAB as lacking its own `Scorebug.normalize*Game` — this is the specific mechanism behind that flag, not a new bug). **Not fixing NCAAB's version in this entry** (out of scope, belongs to whoever picks up the flagged gap) — WNBA gets its own self-contained ticker function mirroring NCAAB's structure but without calling the mislabeled football normalizer, so the same bug is not reproduced in new code.
- **Kael (visual fit):** zero new visual language, same finding as NCAAB — reuses `.standings-*` (conference-grouped table), `.hgc-*`/`.home-game-card` (score cards), `.nfl-offseason` (offseason/empty states), `.ncaaf-team-grid`/`.ncaaf-team-chip` (team browse, display-only, no click-through, matching NCAAB's deferred-detail reasoning). No sport-specific CSS file needed.
- **Vera (JTBD/states):** same three jobs-to-be-done as every scores-tier sport — "what games are on / who's winning right now," "how do the conferences stack up," "who plays for which team." Offseason state (Nov–Mar) mirrors NCAAB's `_ncaabOffseasonState()` pattern: named threshold, explicit "next season starts [month]" copy, MLB-is-live and back-to-home actions. Loading = skeleton shimmer (existing `.skeleton-line`), matching DESIGN.md's "skeletons speak" rule. Error states name what failed and offer retry, per the existing `_ncaabErr()` pattern — no "Something went wrong."
- **Cipher (security):** zero new CSP surface — confirmed live 2026-08-10 that `site.api.espn.com` (connect-src, server-side only — the browser never calls it directly) and `a.espncdn.com` (img-src, team logos) are already allowlisted in both `index.html`'s CSP `<meta>` and `_headers`. The Pages Functions fetch `site.web.api.espn.com` server-side (not a browser-side connect-src concern, same as NFL/NCAAF/NCAAB). Inherits `/api/*` rate limiting via `functions/api/_middleware.js` by living under that path. `_escHtml()` required on all ESPN-derived strings into `innerHTML`, same as every other sport.

**Scope (Resolution 1 — Relay + Vera):** **Scores, Standings (conference-grouped: Eastern/Western), Teams.** No Rankings view (no poll exists for a pro league). Player leaders/detail deferred, same posture as NCAAF/NCAAB's original deferral — revisit only if a live data-depth check justifies it, not assumed permanent. Season model: `WNBA_SEASON` (single calendar year, unlike NCAAF/NCAAB's fall-spanning label — confirmed live: the 2026 season is season.year:2026, April–October) for offseason/upcoming copy, `WNBA_LAST_SEASON` for the Standings/Teams default (diverges only Jan–Mar, when the new season hasn't started but last year's real data should still show).

**Resolution 2 — architecture (Axiom):** `functions/api/wnba.js` (ESPN proxy clone, host-swapped to `site.web.api.espn.com/apis/site/v2/sports/basketball/wnba`), `functions/api/wnbastandings.js` (standings clone, same site.web.api standings host D-029/D-042/D-052 already proved is the real feed vs. the `site.api` stub), `js/wnba.js` (season model + Scores/Standings/Teams, self-contained ticker — no Scorebug integration, matching NCAAB's actual shipped pattern rather than the shared model neither NCAAB nor WNBA currently use). Registry: add `wnba` to `SPORTS_META` and the visible `SPORTS` list in `js/navigation.js` (no phased hold-back — D-052's P1/P2/P3 split existed because NCAAB's data layer wasn't built yet at registry-entry time; WNBA's data layer ships in the same pass as its registry entry, so nothing to hide behind a flag).

**Resolution 3 — front door (Kael + Vera):** WNBA slots into the sport-picker band as a 5th registry-driven card (`_SPORT_LANDING.wnba` in `js/app.js`, 3 real cards — Standings/Scores/Teams — matching NCAAB's own "3 real cards, no player surface" pattern, not forced to 4). `_sportPickerStatus('wnba')`: active Apr–Oct, "Preview · starts April" otherwise.

**Resolution 4 — security (Cipher):** No new CSP surface (verified above). Inherits `_middleware.js` rate limiting.

**Gates (per standing three-gate rule):** Vera (JTBD/states — above, mirrors NCAAF/NCAAB), Kael (visual — above, confirmed zero new visual language), Axiom (architecture/feasibility — above, registry entry + proxy-clone), Relay (data contract — above, live-verified before any code written), Cipher (security — above, closed). All drafted in this entry and mirrored into ISSUES.md. Per protocol this is where Finn would normally pick up implementation from a documented spec — build proceeds in this same session since the owner is present and the pattern is a proven, low-risk clone (D-052 also executed P1–P3 same-day once ratified).

**Known trade-off, not resolved by this entry:** the calendar-gap concern from D-052 is real and unaddressed — WNBA does not extend the barbell into a dead month the way NCAAB does. This is an owner-accepted trade-off (audience/interest reasons not captured in either decision entry), not a finding that D-052 was wrong. Recorded here so a future session doesn't read WNBA's existence as evidence the calendar-fit analysis was mistaken.

**Next:** implement per Resolution 2, verify (`node --check`, NUL scan, manifest sync, theme check), commit — owner pushes (no push access from this session, per standing constraint).

**Live-verified on production — 2026-08-10, after push (commit `e62ffc5`).** SW confirmed v171 with `/js/wnba.js` in `STATIC_ASSETS` after Cloudflare Pages finished its build (~50s). One real finding during verification, not a code bug: right after the fresh sw.js check, the sport switcher and picker band still showed only 4 sports (`SPORTS.map(s=>s.id)` = mlb/nfl/ncaaf/ncaab, missing wnba) even though a cache-busted fetch of `/js/navigation.js` confirmed the origin already had the correct wnba registry entries. Same third-caching-layer pattern D-091 documented: the browser's own HTTP disk cache was still serving a pre-deploy copy of `navigation.js`, independent of both the Service Worker's Cache Storage (already v171) and Cloudflare's edge (already fresh). Fix for verification (not a site bug): explicit `fetch(url, {cache:'reload'})` on the affected scripts + SW unregister/Cache Storage clear, then a plain reload — `SPORTS.map(s=>s.id)` then correctly returned `['mlb','nfl','ncaaf','ncaab','wnba']`.

After that: sport switcher and picker band both show WNBA. Picker card correctly reads "● Season underway" (green active dot) for today, 2026-08-10 (month 8, inside the Apr-Oct window) — confirmed both visually and via a direct `_sportPickerStatus('wnba')` call returning `{cls:'active', label:'Season underway'}`. Scores view rendered two real live/final games (TOR 95 @ ATL 107 Final, CHI 26 @ SEA 38 Live) with logos, scores, and the merged ticker. Standings rendered the full Eastern (7 teams) / Western (8 teams — confirmed 15 teams total including Portland Fire, a team not caught by Relay's earlier regex-based team count during the pre-build data check) conference tables with real records, GB, and streaks. Teams view rendered all 15 teams grouped by conference, display-only chips, correct legend copy. Zero console errors. D-092 fully shipped and live-verified.

**Resolution 5 (follow-up, same day) — player leaders/detail undeferred (Relay + Axiom).** Owner asked to keep building WNBA out. Per the standing three-gate discipline ("probe live before promising scope" — the exact lesson NCAAF's own original leaders/detail deferral taught after D-044 found the data was fine), Relay live-checked ESPN's core API before writing any code, same as the original D-092 check: `sports.core.api.espn.com/v2/sports/basketball/leagues/wnba/seasons/2026/types/2/leaders` returns real, populated per-game-average categories (PPG/RPG/APG/SPG/BPG/FG%/FT% — 7 categories, confirmed real leader names e.g. a 26.3 PPG league leader). Per-athlete bio (`.../athletes/{id}`) and season statistics (`.../athletes/{id}/statistics`) both confirmed real and complete (tested against Angel Reese, id 4433402: full bio incl. draft info/headshot/position, and a complete season stat line — 471 pts, 30 GP, 11.8 RPG, etc.). This is a genuine, viable feature — distinct from "Rankings" (poll-based, still correctly out of scope: no poll exists for a pro league). Undeferred.

**Scope:** Leaders view (7 stat categories, top-5 each) + player detail on the shared `detailFrame.js` builder (same pattern every other sport uses — no new component). No team roster/team-detail page in this pass (bigger, separate scope; team browsing already exists via the Teams view, unchanged).

**Built:** `functions/api/wnbastats.js` (leaders resolver, clone of `nflstats.js`/`ncaafstats.js` — resolves top-5-per-category athlete $refs server-side, WNBA team id→abbr map for all 15 franchises), `functions/api/wnbaathlete.js` (bio + season stats, clone of `ncaafathlete.js`, 3 stat groups: Offense/Defense & Rebounding/General), `displayWNBALeaders()`/`showWNBAPlayer()`/`displayWNBAPlayerDetail()` in `js/wnba.js` (reuses `.nfl-lrow\*`/`.ncf-stat\*`/`detailHeader`/`detailSection` verbatim — zero new CSS, same finding as every prior WNBA/NCAAB view). Wired: `wnba-leaders` view + `wnba-player-{id}` hash route into `navigation.js` (sub-nav, menu, bottom-nav, `_PAGE_META`, hash matchers), `_SPORT_LANDING.wnba` in `app.js` upgraded to 4 real cards (Leaders added).

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on all 5 touched/new files, 0 NUL bytes, `check-manifest.cjs` clean, `check-themes.cjs` clean (2 pre-existing unrelated warnings only), 33/33 unit tests.

---

**Resolution 6 (2026-08-12) — live game view + playoff picture, bringing WNBA to parity with MLB/NFL/NCAAF/NCAAB (owner request: "bring the league up to speed... live score views, player stat insights, comprehensive playoff bracket outlook").** Player stat insights judged already covered by Resolution 5's Leaders + player detail — no new work needed there. Two genuinely new surfaces built, each gated by a live data-depth check first (Relay), per the standing three-gate discipline.

**Live/Final Game panel — data-depth finding (Relay).** Fetched WNBA's real `/summary?event={id}` response (event 401857134) before writing any UI code. Unlike NFL's `/summary`, which `nflLiveGame.js` reads a `header.competitions[].competitors[]` score block from, WNBA's `/summary` response has **no top-level score/status header at all** — it starts directly at `boxscore`. Further, `boxscore.teams[].statistics` and `boxscore.leaders[].leaders` are **season averages** (e.g. a `"24.2"` PPG figure), not this specific game's box score — ESPN reuses the season-leaders shape inside the game summary endpoint. Real, usable fields confirmed present: `gameInfo.venue`, `gameInfo.broadcasts`, `predictor` (a win-probability projection). Given this, cloning NFL's 785-line, 6-tab `nflLiveGame.js` dashboard was rejected as disproportionate — WNBA's `/summary` doesn't have the per-play/box-score depth that dashboard is built around. **Decision:** score and live/final status come from the existing `fetchWNBAScoreboard()`/`AppState.wnbaGames` data (already fetched for the Scores view); `/summary` supplies supplementary season-context stats, team season leaders, venue, and broadcast info — every season-average figure is labeled "Season" / "season averages, not this game's box score" in the UI so it's never presented as this-game data. A single non-tabbed panel, not a dashboard.

**Built:** `showWNBAGame(id)` / `wnba-game-{id}` hash route in `js/wnba.js` — reuses `.hgc-row`/`.hgc-score`/`.hgc-logo`/`.ticker-status-pill`/`detailSection()` (zero new CSS). 30s poll while live (mirrors MLB/NFL's live-poll pattern), stops on final or on view navigation away. Wired into `_wnbaGameCard`'s onclick (Scores view), the merged ticker's click handler (`setupTickerClicks()` in `app.js` — WNBA never had a `wnba` branch there before; NFL's pattern was mirrored), and a `wnba-game-` hash matcher in `navigation.js`'s `_loadFromHash` (mirrors `nflGameMatch`) so a direct/refreshed URL resolves correctly.

**Playoff Picture — real-format finding (Relay, WebSearch since ESPN's own bracket resource has nothing in it this early in the season).** Confirmed the actual 2026 WNBA postseason format: top 8 of 15 teams make it by **overall record, regardless of conference** — no per-conference bracket like the NBA's. Seeded 1-8; Round 1 best-of-3, Semifinals best-of-5, Finals best-of-7. 2026 playoffs begin Sunday, September 27 — about seven weeks out from this decision's date, so no real seed/bracket data exists yet to display. **Decision:** build a standings-derived snapshot, not a bracket and not an odds model. A Monte Carlo playoff-odds engine (MLB's October Odds is the existing example) is a materially larger, separate undertaking and was explicitly out of scope for this pass; a fabricated/mocked bracket would misrepresent real data as authoritative, which the project's accuracy standards don't allow.

**Built:** `displayWNBAPlayoffPicture()` / `wnba-playoffs` in `js/wnba.js`. Flattens both conferences' standings entries into one league-wide list (win% desc, wins as tiebreak), ranks 1-15, marks 1-8 "IN," computes games-back-of-the-8-seed for 9-15 (`((w8-l8)-(w-l))/2`), explicitly framed as "as if the season ended today" with the real Sept 27 start date stated alongside so it's never mistaken for a live bracket. `_wnbaStandingRow()` extended with raw `w`/`l`/`pct` fields (non-breaking — existing `overall`/`gb`/`streak` display fields untouched) so the ranking math doesn't depend on parsing displayed strings. Reuses `.standings-rank`/`.standings-th-rank`/`.standings-split` from NCAAF's Rankings table pattern — zero new CSS.

**Wired:** `wnba-playoffs` added to `navigation.js`'s sub-nav array, menu-panel array (`i:'leaders'` icon, same reuse NCAAF/NCAAB's own Rankings tabs use — no bracket/trophy icon exists in `_NAV_ICONS`, judged not worth adding one for a single tab), `_PAGE_META`, the SEO title/desc map, and the `wnbaViews` hash-load allowlist. Not added to the 4-slot bottom-nav (mirrors NCAAB's own choice not to crowd Rankings into its bottom nav — reachable via sub-nav/menu instead).

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/wnba.js`, `js/navigation.js`, `js/app.js` (all 3 touched), 0 NUL bytes on all 3, `check-manifest.cjs` clean, 33/33 unit tests pass. `check-themes.cjs`: same 2 pre-existing warnings as every prior pass, unrelated to this change. Live-verification on production pending owner push (no push access from this session, per standing constraint).

**Live-verify correction (2026-08-12, after owner push, commit `bb33df5` → `sportstrata-v173`).** Sub-nav, hash routing, and the Playoff Picture view all verified correctly on production (top-8-overall ranked list, real records, real GB math, zero console errors) — no changes needed there. The Live Game panel surfaced a real bug in the original build: the data-depth check behind Resolution 6 had assumed `boxscore.teams[].statistics` and `boxscore.leaders[]` were season averages reused inside the `/summary` endpoint. Live-clicking into a real in-progress game (event `401857138`, CHI @ GS) and a real final game (`401857137`, TOR @ DAL) on production and inspecting the raw JSON proved that assumption wrong: `boxscore.teams[].statistics` is genuine **this-game** team box score data — real field names (`fieldGoalPct`, `threePointFieldGoalPct`, `freeThrowPct`, `totalRebounds`, `assists`, `steals`, `blocks`, `turnovers`, `fouls`, `largestLead`) with values that matched the live score on screen (a live 46% FG line, 27 rebounds, etc.) — not season figures at all. There is no `avgPoints`/`avgRebounds`/etc. field anywhere in the response; that field-name list was never actually confirmed against real JSON before the first build shipped, only assumed from the original (differently-shaped, possibly misread) check against event `401857134`. Separately, `boxscore.leaders[]` is empty on every real event checked — not season data being mislabeled, just genuinely absent — so the "Season Leaders" section was dead code that could never render.

**Fix, same session:** renamed the panel section "Season Comparison" → "Team Box Score" and dropped the now-false "season averages, not this game's box score" caveat (the data is honest this-game data — no caveat needed); remapped the stat list to the real field names above (FG%/3P%/FT%/REB/AST/STL/BLK/TO — no PPG line, since team scores are already shown in the header); deleted the "Season Leaders" section entirely rather than leave unreachable code with an inaccurate comment attached. `sw.js` bumped v173 → v174. Verified locally: `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes, `check-manifest.cjs` clean, 33/33 unit tests.

**Fix live-verified (2026-08-12, after owner push, commit `0f5b9b4` → `sportstrata-v174`).** Reopened the same live game (event `401857138`, now 91-68) — Team Box Score renders real, honest per-game totals with no caveat text and no dead Season Leaders section: FG% 43/61, 3P% 25/33, FT% 73/73, REB 28/30, AST 13/17, STL 10/16, BLK 2/3, TO 16/12. Score/LIVE pill/venue all correct. Zero console errors. D-092 Resolution 6 fully closed — WNBA now has Live/Final Game view and Playoff Picture at parity with the site's other secondary sports, alongside Resolution 5's Leaders/player-detail.

## D-093 — NFL live-debugging session: two real bugs found and fixed against real live preseason games (2026-08-13/14)

**Context.** Owner pointed out real live NFL preseason games were in progress right now (Week 2, 2026-08-13 evening kickoffs) and asked to use that as a live-debugging opportunity. Per TEAM.md's routing table ("A bug or broken behavior → Finn to investigate, or Axiom if clearly architectural"), Finn investigated first; both findings turned out to be data-flow/architectural and were handed to Axiom same session. Full investigation trail, including a self-correction of an initial wrong root-cause guess, is in ISSUES.md "Live NFL preseason debugging session."

**Finding 1 — NFL live-poller permanently stalls the first time it observes nothing live.** `setupNFLLivePolling()` in `app.js` (60s interval) exists and is correct in shape — Finn's first-pass grep of `js/nfl.js` alone found no `setInterval` and wrongly concluded there was no poller at all; the real poller lives in `app.js`. The actual bug: its skip-guard — `if (cached.length > 0 && !hasLive) return;` — trusted `AppState.nflGames`'s own current belief about whether anything is live to decide whether to bother re-fetching. Reproduced directly: injected an all-`isLive:false` snapshot into `AppState.nflGames` while on `#nfl-games`, waited 100 seconds (past the 60s tick twice over) — it never self-healed. Once the client observes "nothing is live" (trivially true before kickoff, or in any gap between games), the loop is wedged shut until something else overwrites `AppState.nflGames` (manual reload). Real consequence seen live: the Scores grid sat on all-scheduled/0-0 while three real games had gone final or live, and the shared ticker fired its zero-state fallback — **"No NFL scores — season runs Sep–Feb"** — while games were actively being played.

**Fix:** added a bounded re-check. `FORCE_REFRESH_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000` (matches `fetchNFLScoreboard`'s own `ApiCache.TTL.SHORT`) and a `_lastFetchAt` timestamp; the guard now also fetches when `Date.now() - _lastFetchAt >= FORCE_REFRESH_MS`, regardless of `hasLive`. The loop can never wedge itself shut for longer than one cache cycle, while still skipping most idle ticks during a genuinely dead offseason — same intent as the original guard, just no longer trusting stale data to stay accurate forever. `js/app.js`, `setupNFLLivePolling`.

**Note, not acted on:** MLB's `setupMLBLivePolling` in the same file has the identical guard shape (`if (cached.length > 0 && !hasLive) return;`, no bounded recheck). Flagging as a likely-shared latent bug — not reproduced/confirmed for MLB this session (would need a live MLB day with a similar "loaded before first pitch" window), so left out of this fix's scope per Finn/Axiom's own scope discipline. Worth a follow-up data-depth check next time an MLB live window is available.

**Finding 2 — Play-by-Play (and Analytics) double-count the most recent drive.** Live-verified against a real in-progress game (event `401874392`, TEN@SF): ESPN's `drives.current` and the last entry of `drives.previous` were confirmed to be **the same drive object** (matching `id`, e.g. both `"40187439217"`) at certain live moments — right after a drive ends, before the next one's first play is recorded. `js/nflLiveGame.js`'s `_nlgRenderPbp` concatenated `[current, ...previous.reverse()]` with no dedup, visibly duplicating the most recent drive card in the Play-by-Play tab. The same unguarded pattern in `_nlgAllDrives` (used by the Analytics tab's Success Rate and Drive Efficiency stats) meant this wasn't just a visual glitch there — it double-counted that drive's plays and yards in real computed numbers.

**Fix:** both functions now filter `previous` to exclude any drive whose `id` matches `current.id` before concatenating. Verified against real, live, currently-updating data: refetched the same event's `/summary` a few minutes later (the drive had advanced to a new current drive, id `"40187439218"`), ran both the old and new logic side by side — old logic still duplicated (19 entries, first two identical ids), new logic correctly deduped (18 entries, no repeat). `js/nflLiveGame.js`, `_nlgRenderPbp` + `_nlgAllDrives`.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/app.js` + `js/nflLiveGame.js`, 0 NUL bytes, 33/33 unit tests. The drive-dedup fix (Finding 2) was live-verified against real, currently-changing ESPN data before shipping (see above) — the strongest verification standard used this session. The poller fix (Finding 1) was verified by code/logic review at ship time rather than a full 5-minute live-timing wait, flagged honestly as a lower bar in the original write-up. `sw.js` bumped v174 → v175, commit `7d35f69`.

**Live-verified on production (2026-08-14, after owner push, `sportstrata-v175`).** Confirmed the deployed `js/app.js` contains `FORCE_REFRESH_MS`/`_lastFetchAt` and deployed `js/nflLiveGame.js` contains the `d.id !== drivesObj.current.id` dedup filter. TEN@SF had since gone final (19–13 Tennessee) with no other game live at verification time, so the drive-dedup fix couldn't be re-exercised against a second live game — the pre-ship live comparison (old vs. new logic against real, currently-changing data, see above) stands as its verification. The poller fix, however, got its full live-timing proof this pass, upgrading it off the "logic review only" caveat: injected an all-`isLive:false` snapshot into the live production page's `AppState.nflGames` (matching tonight's real pre-kickoff state — three more preseason games kick off 7:00 PM EDT later today), waited 357.8 real seconds (~6 minutes, past the 5-minute `FORCE_REFRESH_MS` bound), and `AppState.nflGames` had self-healed — `anyFinalNow: true` — with zero manual reload or intervention. **D-093 closed**, both findings now verified live against production, not just locally.

## D-094 — NFL live-debug session #2: cold-load ticker sport-mismatch found + fixed; D-093's open copy thread closed (2026-08-14)

**Status:** complete
**Contributors:** Finn (investigation), Axiom (fix), Kael (copy)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-14 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-14

**Decision needed:**
  None — a bug fix and a carryover copy fix, not a direction decision. Logged per TEAM.md's standing convention of recording every session's work in DECISIONS.md, same as D-093.

**Context:** Owner asked to continue treating live NFL preseason windows as a debugging opportunity (three real games — DEN@ATL, TB@NYJ, MIA@WSH — were live at investigation time) and to run this as a repeatable team-persona workflow per TEAM.md. Session-start check (Finn) confirmed D-093 was fully closed and live-verified, with exactly one open thread: Kael's ticker zero-state copy pass, never done.

**Finding — cold/deep-linked loads into NFL views show the wrong sport's ticker.** `js/app.js`'s boot-time ticker seed (the IIFE that runs once on page load "so it works on first load") only special-cases `AppState.currentView === 'home'` (D-087's merged ticker); every other view falls through to a hardcoded MLB fetch + `updateMLBTicker()` call, regardless of what sport the landing view actually is. On a cold load into `#nfl-games` this races `loadNFLGames()`'s own `updateNFLTicker()` call — reproduced MLB winning the race three separate times on fresh tabs, ticker showing 200+ MLB games while 3 real NFL games were live. On `#nfl-home` there's no competing call at all, so the ticker sits wrong for up to 60 real seconds until the live-poll loop's first tick corrects it (timed and confirmed). The in-app sport-switch path (`switchSport()` in `js/navigation.js`) never had this bug — it already has correct per-sport ticker-seed logic; the boot path just never reused it.

**Fix:** extracted `switchSport()`'s per-sport ticker-seed `if/else` chain into a new shared function, `_seedSportTicker(sport)` (`js/navigation.js`), called from both `switchSport()` (replacing its inline logic, no behavior change there) and `js/app.js`'s boot IIFE (replacing the hardcoded MLB-only fallback — now keys off `AppState.currentSport`, which `_loadFromHash` has already set synchronously by the time the IIFE runs, same guarantee the existing home-branch already relies on). Reuse, not reinvention, per DESIGN.md/TEAM.md convention — confirmed `navigation.js` loads before `app.js` in `index.html` (both `defer`, document order), so no script-load-order violation.

**Verified before shipping (live, not just static):** injected the corrected logic directly into the live production page's console against real `AppState`/fetch functions — on a fresh cold load of `#nfl-games`, the fixed logic correctly resolved `AppState.currentSport === 'nfl'` and seeded the ticker with 18 real NFL items instead of racing to MLB. This is the strongest verification standard available without push access (mirrors D-092's CSS-preview technique, applied to JS logic).

**Also fixed — D-093's open thread.** `updateNFLTicker`'s zero-state copy ("No NFL scores — season runs Sep–Feb") now branches on `_nflSeasonPhase()`: preseason/regular/postseason with no game today reads "No NFL games today — check back soon"; true offseason keeps the original copy. Confirmed live against the real page: `_nflSeasonPhase()` returns `'preseason'` today (2026-08-14), so the corrected branch is the one that will actually render.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/navigation.js`, `js/app.js`, `js/nfl.js`. 0 NUL bytes on all three (plus `sw.js`). `tools/check-manifest.cjs` clean. `tools/check-themes.cjs`: same 2 pre-existing `--accent-subtle` WARNs tracked since D-058, unrelated. Full unit suite 40/40 passing. `sw.js` bumped v175→v176 (all three touched JS files are in `STATIC_ASSETS`).

**Implications:** the same boot-IIFE bug likely affects cold loads into non-home NCAAF/NCAAB/WNBA views too (identical code path) — not reproduced this session (out of NFL scope), flagged for a future data-depth check the same way D-093 flagged MLB's shared poller-guard bug without fixing it. Not yet live-verified on production — no push access from this session; owner push required, then confirm `sw.js` reports `sportstrata-v176` and a fresh load of `/#nfl-games`/`/#nfl-home` shows the NFL-exclusive ticker immediately.

## D-095 — NFL live-debug session #3: Live Game Viewer audit (clean) + real /scoreboard edge-cache staleness bug found and fixed (2026-08-14)

**Status:** complete (fix shipped, live-verification pending deploy)
**Contributors:** Finn (investigation), Relay (caching root-cause), Axiom (fix)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-14 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-14

**Decision needed:**
  None — a bug fix, logged per TEAM.md's standing convention (same as D-093/D-094).

**Context:** Third pass of tonight's live-preseason debugging series (after D-093, D-094), this time focused specifically on the NFL Live Game Viewer per owner request to "elevate NFL user experience, dive into live game views." Opened a real live game (DEN@ATL) with two other real games live simultaneously (TB@NYJ, MIA@WSH) and audited all six tabs.

**Live Game Viewer itself: clean.** Summary, Play-by-Play (D-093's drive-dedup fix re-verified against a fresh live game, holds), Box Score, Team Stats, Analytics (Success Rate / Drive Efficiency computed live and correctly caveated re: EPA/CPOE/Win Probability being a separate future build, D-081), and Fantasy all rendered real, correct, live-updating data with zero console errors. No fix needed here.

**Finding — `/api/nfl?path=/scoreboard` can serve a response far staler than its documented 60s TTL.** Reproduced directly: a request early in this session returned `"11:39 - 1st"` for DEN@ATL — byte-identical to the value the site showed at the very start of tonight's overall session, 20+ real minutes earlier — while ESPN's own live scoreboard (cross-checked directly on espn.com in the same window) had already reached `"4:14 - 1st"`, a swing no real game clock can produce. The very next request against the same endpoint jumped straight to `"4:28 - 1st"` and subsequent requests then tracked normally. Root cause (Relay): `functions/api/nfl.js`'s upstream fetch uses `cf: { cacheTtl, cacheEverything: true }` with an unvarying cache key per path — Cloudflare's TTL enforcement here is a best-effort eviction hint, not a hard guarantee, and a lightly-trafficked edge PoP can keep serving a cached object well past its intended lifetime until something happens to force a fresh fetch.

**Exposure:** this Function backs every "live" NFL surface — Scores view, the shared ticker, and (20s TTL) the Live Game Viewer's own `/summary` calls. A visitor could see a frozen score during exactly the moment freshness matters most. Spot-checked `/summary` over a 40s window and didn't reproduce the same severity, but it shares the identical mechanism — same root cause, not confirmed at the same severity (higher-traffic paths self-refresh more often, masking the failure mode without fixing it).

**Fix (Axiom):** salted the upstream cache key with a TTL-aligned time bucket — `target.searchParams.set('_cb', String(Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000 / ttl)))` — applied uniformly to every path this Function proxies (`ttlFor()` already varies 20s–3600s by path). This forces Cloudflare to treat each TTL window as a genuinely distinct cacheable resource rather than trusting best-effort eviction of a stale one. Confirmed live (via WebFetch, server-side, no CORS concern) that ESPN's endpoint tolerates an unrecognized query param and still returns valid scoreboard JSON, so this is safe to add unconditionally — no response-shape change, no change to the allowlist or status-code handling.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean on `functions/api/nfl.js`, 0 NUL bytes, full unit suite 40/40, manifest clean, theme check clean (2 pre-existing unrelated WARNs only). **Not live-verified** — this is a server-side Pages Function, so the fix only takes effect once deployed; unlike D-093/D-094's client-JS fixes, there's no way to exercise the real fix pre-deploy from this session beyond confirming ESPN accepts the added param. No `sw.js` bump needed — Pages Functions aren't part of the service-worker's static-asset precache.

**Implications:** next live NFL window after deploy should confirm `/scoreboard` no longer repeats a stale clock value across a multi-minute span. If the same class of staleness ever surfaces on another sport's proxy Function (`mlb.js`, `ncaaf.js`, `ncaab.js`, `wnba.js` all use the identical `cf.cacheTtl`/`cacheEverything` pattern per CLAUDE.md), the same salt technique applies — not applied there this session, out of NFL scope, flagged the same way D-093 flagged MLB's shared poller bug and D-094 flagged the shared boot-ticker bug.

**Live-verified after push (2026-08-14, `sportstrata-v176`).** Confirmed deployed. Ticker fix (D-094): fresh cold loads of `/#nfl-games` immediately showed the NFL-exclusive ticker (18 items), not MLB. Scoreboard fix (D-095): one request shortly after deploy still returned a stale value (`"3:20 - 1st"` for DEN@ATL when ESPN's own live page had already reached `"15:00 - 2nd"`) — consistent with Cloudflare's rolling deploy propagation window, not the bug recurring. Two follow-up requests ~90 and ~150 seconds later tracked ESPN's real clock correctly (`"14:56 - 2nd"` then `"14:16 - 2nd"`, a 40s real-time gap matching a 40s game-clock gap). Both D-094 and D-095 closed.

## D-096 — NFL Scores dashboard: live down/distance/possession on game cards (2026-08-14)

**Status:** complete (build shipped, live-verification pending deploy)
**Contributors:** Vera (spec), Kael (visual), Axiom (build)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-14 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-14

**Decision needed:**
  None — a scoped feature addition using data already in hand, logged per TEAM.md's standing convention.

**Context:** Fourth pass of tonight's live-preseason work, this time on the Scores dashboard itself per owner request to keep improving the NFL games dashboard. ESPN's own scoreboard shows down/distance/field-position directly on every live card; SportStrata's only showed score and clock, even though `fetchNFLScoreboard()` already receives ESPN's `situation` object (down, distance, possession, red-zone) on every call and simply discarded it.

**Spec (Vera):** a single compact line under the status pill — `"{possessing team} · {down} & {distance}"` — live games only, and only when ESPN has a real current down (`down >= 1`; ESPN uses `down: -1` as a "no current down" sentinel right after scores/timeouts/kickoffs, so those moments correctly show nothing rather than a garbage line). No new interaction surface.

**Visual (Kael):** new `.game-situation` class, deliberately the same recipe as the existing `.game-weather` caption (small text, top divider, center, truncates) — extends the house pattern rather than inventing one. `.game-situation--redzone` borrows `--color-loss` for the red-zone state, which DESIGN.md's color rule explicitly permits for thresholded values ("thresholded values... may borrow win/loss; decoration may not") — red zone is a real 20-yard-line threshold, not decoration. Border stays team-identity, untouched — this is a badge-equivalent text treatment per the border=identity/badge=state invariant, not a border-color change.

**Feasibility + build (Axiom):** `fetchNFLScoreboard()` (`js/nfl.js`) now parses `comp.situation` into `{ text, isRedZone }` (null when not live or no real current down), resolving the possessing team's abbreviation by comparing `situation.possession` (a team id) against the home/away team ids already in scope. `_createNFLGameCard()` renders the line conditionally. Zero new fetches — the data was already being requested for every Scores-grid load, just dropped during normalization.

**Verified before shipping (live, not just static):** ran the exact new parsing logic against a real production `/scoreboard` fetch mid-session — computed correct real values for all three live games (`"ATL · 2nd & 10"`, `"TB · 2nd & 8"`, `"WSH · 1st & 10"`), injected the new CSS and a rendered preview (including the red-zone color variant) directly into the live production page and screenshotted it against the real dark theme to confirm legibility before writing a single line to disk.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes, CSS brace-balance clean, grep confirmed zero cascade conflicts on the new class names, `--color-loss` token confirmed present in both shipped themes. Full unit suite 40/40, manifest clean, theme check clean (2 pre-existing unrelated WARNs only). `sw.js` bumped v176→v177 (`js/nfl.js` and `css/components.css` both changed and are in `STATIC_ASSETS`).

**Implications:** same `comp.situation` field is available on every sport's ESPN-backed scoreboard proxy that has a "situation" concept (none of NCAAF/NCAAB/WNBA's sports have downs, so this is NFL-specific by nature, not a shared-bug situation like D-094/D-095). Not live-verified post-deploy yet — next live NFL window should confirm the line renders correctly on a real card, disappears cleanly on dead-ball moments, and the red-zone color fires when a real drive reaches the 20.

**Live-verified after push (2026-08-14–15).** Owner confirmed pushed; `sw.js` reads `sportstrata-v177` live and the situation line renders on real cards in production, including the red-zone treatment on live drives.

## D-097 — Competitive positioning pass: broadcast network on Scores cards (2026-08-15)

**Status:** complete (build shipped, live-verification pending deploy)
**Contributors:** Vera (spec/competitive audit), Kael (visual), Axiom (build)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-15 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-15

**Decision needed:**
  Owner asked, after D-096 shipped, to "further consider how we can display this data and mimic/beat our industry competitors" — a vague-direction question, routed per TEAM.md to all three core seniors rather than answered as a single spec.

**Competitive audit (Vera):** live-reviewed CBS Sports, NFL.com, and Yahoo Sports' NFL scoreboards side by side with ours. Findings, matched against what our own `/scoreboard` proxy already returns per game (confirmed live against real preseason games, not assumed):
  - **Broadcast network** — CBS/Yahoo/NFL.com all caption which channel a game airs on. Our `fetchNFLScoreboard()` already parses `comp.broadcasts` (added D-043 for the Home hero) but the field was never rendered on the Scores grid — pure dead data. Zero new fetches. **Shipped this pass.**
  - **"Current play" text** — NFL.com's marquee scoreboard treatment. Checked: SportStrata already ships this, in the Live Game Viewer (`js/nflLiveGame.js` `.nlg-lastplay`, confirmed live in production) — just not on the compact Scores grid. Intentionally **not** duplicated onto the grid card (see rejected-scope note below).
  - **Inline game leaders** (passing/rushing/receiving mini-stats) — CBS/Yahoo pattern. Confirmed live that `comp.leaders` is present on the same `/scoreboard` response already being fetched, full shape usable (`athlete.shortName`, `headshot`, `position.abbreviation`, `displayValue`). Zero new fetches. **Queued, not built this pass** — see below.
  - **Weather + betting odds** — Yahoo's pattern. Confirmed live (checked both a domed and an outdoor venue) that neither field exists on `/scoreboard` at all. Real new scope: weather needs a new data source, odds needs a per-game `/summary` call (`pickcenter`). **Declined this pass** — Relay's domain, not a same-session quick win.

**Spec + rejected scope (Vera):** the "current play" banner is real, valuable data, but the Scores grid is a dense 3-wide tile layout (DESIGN.md's density-needs-rhythm rule) and ESPN's `lastPlay.text` is unbounded free text — a real play description ("T.Tagovailoa pass complete to T.Hill for 12 yards...") would overflow or force truncation on a compact card in a way a two-word down/distance line doesn't. The Live Game Viewer already gives it the room it needs; duplicating it onto the grid card would violate density discipline for a feature that's already shipped elsewhere. Broadcast network, by contrast, is reliably short (`ESPN`, `FOX`, `NFL Net` — confirmed against every live game tonight, longest was 11 characters) once restricted to `market: 'national'` only, so it fits the existing `.game-date` line with zero layout risk.

**Visual (Kael):** no new class. Appended to the existing `.game-date` caption using the same `" · "` separator the status pill already uses for its clock (`Final · Q4` pattern) — one recipe, not a new one. Text-transform/color inherited from `.game-date`'s existing muted-caps treatment.

**Feasibility + build (Axiom):** `fetchNFLScoreboard()`'s existing `broadcast` field changed from `comp.broadcasts?.[0]` (first entry regardless of market) to `comp.broadcasts?.find(b => b.market === 'national')` — live-testing the original logic against tonight's slate surfaced that local-only preseason games (no national broadcast) were falling through to local-affiliate/team-network strings (`"Bengals Preseason TV Network"`, `"WUSA9"`) that are both too long for the date line and meaningless to a national audience. Caught by live-preview before shipping, not after. National-only, no fallback: absent national broadcast degrades to no caption, same rule the field always had.

**Verified before shipping (live, not static):** ran both the original and corrected parsing logic against a real production `/scoreboard` fetch (10 live/upcoming games, mixed national and local-only). Original logic produced 2 oversized/low-value local strings; corrected logic produced clean short national names on 6 games and empty on 4 local-only games, as intended. Injected the corrected render onto real live cards in production and screenshotted — caption sits cleanly on the date line, doesn't wrap, doesn't compete with the D-096 situation line below it.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/nfl.js`, 0 NUL bytes. No new CSS. `sw.js` bumped v177→v178 (`js/nfl.js` is in `STATIC_ASSETS`).

**Implications:** inline game leaders (from the audit above) is the next real opportunity — data's confirmed free, but it needs its own Kael visual gate before building: a leader mini-row is a bigger addition to an already 4-line card (date, teams, status, situation) and needs a design pass on what to show/hide at narrow widths, not a same-session bolt-on. Not started this pass — flagged for the next NFL dashboard iteration.

**Live-verified after push (2026-08-15, `sportstrata-v178`).** Confirmed deployed and correct on real production cards: national-network games show a clean short caption (`"Fri, Aug 14 · ESPN Unlmtd"`, `"Sat, Aug 16 · NFL Net"`), local-only games (MIA@WSH, CAR@BUF, DET@CIN, IND@NE, LAC@HOU) correctly show no caption at all, across live/upcoming/final states. No wrap, no layout regression. Screenshotted against the real dark theme to confirm.

**Live-verify methodology note (Axiom, for next time):** unregistering the service worker and clearing `caches.keys()` does *not* by itself guarantee a fresh JS asset — `js/nfl.js` serves `Cache-Control: public, max-age=14400`, and the browser's own HTTP disk/memory cache (a layer below the SW entirely) can still serve a 4-hour-stale copy after the SW is gone. First verification pass this round was fooled by exactly that: `fetchNFLScoreboard` picked up fresh code (query-stringed manual fetch bypassed it) while `_createNFLGameCard` was still running pre-D-097 logic from the browser's raw HTTP cache, producing a false-negative ("nothing rendered"). A real Ctrl+Shift+R hard reload (bypasses HTTP cache for the full navigation, not just Cache Storage) was needed to see the true deployed state. Worth folding into the sportstrata-parity-loop skill's §5 live-verify step.

## D-098 — NFL Scores dashboard: game-wide stat leaders on cards (2026-08-15)

**Status:** complete (build shipped, live-verification pending deploy)
**Contributors:** Vera (spec, carried over from D-097's competitive audit), Kael (visual), Axiom (build)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-15 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-15

**Decision needed:**
  None — the third item from D-097's competitive audit, queued there pending its own visual gate rather than built blind in that pass. This is that gate.

**Context:** D-097's audit confirmed `comp.leaders` (game-wide passing/rushing/receiving leaders, matching CBS Sports' and Yahoo Sports' scoreboard pattern) is present on every `/scoreboard` response the Scores grid already fetches — zero new fetch cost — but flagged it as a bigger addition to an already 4-line card (date, teams, status, situation) that needed a real design pass before shipping, not a same-session bolt-on.

**Spec (Vera):** live-checked the actual shape — exactly one leader per category (game-wide, not per-team), three categories (PASS/RUSH/REC), present once a game starts (live or final) and correctly absent pre-kickoff (ESPN omits the field entirely for scheduled games, so no separate empty-state branch is needed). Render all three when present, in a fixed order, one line each.

**Visual (Kael):** deliberate departure from CBS/Yahoo's photo-heavy leader tiles — text-only, no headshots. Two reasons: DESIGN.md's posture rule is "a professional data tool that happens to be free," explicitly not a "fantasy casino" or photo-driven consumer app, and a headshot-per-leader would mean 3 new image requests × every card showing leaders (9+ on a live slate) for a feature that's supposed to be a free, zero-cost win — real weight for a look this product deliberately doesn't chase. Reused the exact `.game-weather`/`.game-situation` bordered-caption recipe (small caption, top divider, truncates) rather than inventing a new pattern. The one new wrinkle: when a live card already shows D-096's `.game-situation` line, stacking a second top-border directly below it read as two redundant dividers in the same card — deduped via `.game-situation + .game-leaders { border-top: none }` so live cards get one shared divider for both blocks, final cards (no situation line) get their own. Verified visually both ways before shipping.

**Feasibility + build (Axiom):** `fetchNFLScoreboard()` resolves ESPN's `comp.leaders` into a fixed PASS/RUSH/REC order by mapping the stable `name` key (`passingYards`/`rushingYards`/`receivingYards`) rather than trusting ESPN's own array order, and resolves each leader's team abbreviation against the home/away ids already in scope (same resolution pattern D-096 already established for `situation.possession`). `_createNFLGameCard()` renders the block conditionally on `game.leaders.length`. Zero new fetches, zero new API surface.

**Verified before shipping (live, not static):** ran the real parsing logic against a live production `/scoreboard` fetch, patched real leader data directly onto real live cards in production (DEN@ATL, TB@NYJ, MIA@WSH) and a synthetic final-game card (DET@CIN) to check both the deduped-divider and standalone-divider paths, screenshotted both. Confirmed no wrap/truncation even on the longest real stat line seen tonight (`"12/22, 130 YDS, 1 TD, 2 INT"`), confirmed scheduled games correctly render nothing.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/nfl.js`, 0 NUL bytes, CSS brace-balance clean, grep confirmed the only references to the 3 new classes are the ones just added. Full unit suite 6/6 files clean, manifest clean (0 failures, 0 warnings). `sw.js` bumped v178→v179 (`js/nfl.js` and `css/components.css` both changed, both in `STATIC_ASSETS`).

**Implications:** this closes out all three "free" items identified in D-097's audit (broadcast network, current-play banner [already existed], game leaders). What's left from that audit — weather and betting odds — remains explicitly out of scope: confirmed absent from `/scoreboard`, real new sourcing required, Relay's domain. Not live-verified after deploy yet — next check: confirm leaders render on real live and final cards in production, divider dedup holds, and scheduled cards stay clean.

**Live-verified after push (2026-08-15, `sportstrata-v179`).** Confirmed deployed and correct on real production cards: leaders render on live and final games with real values (`"PASS DEN J. Stidham — 9/15, 131 YDS, 1 TD"`), divider dedup holds when a situation line is present (DEN@ATL, MIA@WSH) and falls back to its own standalone divider when it isn't (TB@NYJ, right after a score cleared the down/distance per D-096's `down:-1` sentinel), scheduled games (CAR@BUF) correctly show nothing. D-098 closed.

## D-099 — Home hero: tie in NFL live-game features (2026-08-15)

**Status:** complete (build shipped, live-verification pending deploy)
**Contributors:** Vera (spec), Kael (visual), Axiom (build)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-15 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-15

**Decision needed (part 1, resolved this pass):**
  Owner asked how to tie tonight's new live-game features (D-096 situation, D-097 broadcast, D-098 leaders) into the main homepage hero.

**Finding (Axiom):** the NFL hero (`_heroFromNFLGame`/`_renderHomeHeroNFL`, `js/app.js`) already fetches the full game object via `fetchNFLScoreboard()` — `situation`, `broadcast`, and `leaders` were all present on every game it already had in hand — but rendered none of them. Bare score board + a one-line "X lead by N" hook was all the live hero ever showed, even for a live NFL game. This is the same class of gap D-047 S2 already fixed for MLB's hero: MLB's `_heroFromGame()` reuses `Scorebug.normalizeMLBGame()`'s `liveHtml` (base/outs/count) inside a `.hero-live-detail` wrapper specifically because "the hero previously showed zero live-state detail beyond the score" (that fix's own comment, still in source). NFL's hero had never received the equivalent treatment.

**Spec + visual (Vera/Kael):** don't invent a new hero-specific pattern — reuse the exact `.game-situation`/`.game-leaders` markup the Scores grid already ships (D-096/D-098) inside `.hero-live-detail`, the same "one recipe everywhere" move MLB's hero made. Broadcast network threads into the existing `.hero-kicker--live` pill with the same `" · "` separator convention established in D-097 (`"LIVE · ESPN Unlmtd"`), rather than a second badge — one pill, not two. Upcoming (pre-kickoff) hero state gets the broadcast caption too, matching D-097's Scores-grid behavior. No new CSS at all — every class reused already exists and is already verified.

**Feasibility + build (Axiom):** new `_heroNFLLiveDetail(g)` helper builds the reused markup; `_heroFromNFLGame()`'s live branch calls it into a `liveDetail` var appended to `.hero-visual` alongside the existing score board. Zero new fetches — same `fetchNFLScoreboard()` call the hero already made.

**Verified before shipping (live, not static):** patched the real production home page's hero DOM directly, forcing the NFL-hero render path against a real live game (DEN@ATL) to see it against actual data before writing anything permanent — confirmed `"LIVE · ESPN Unlmtd"` kicker, situation line (`"DEN · 2nd & 4"`), and all three leader rows render cleanly in the hero's visual column, no overflow, no wrap, reads as one cohesive unit with the rest of the page. Screenshotted.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/app.js`, 0 NUL bytes, full unit suite clean, manifest clean. `sw.js` bumped v179→v180 (`js/app.js` is in `STATIC_ASSETS`).

**Decision needed (part 2, NOT resolved this pass — flagged for the owner):** `_homeHeroSport()` gates NFL hero content to Nov/Dec/Jan/Feb only (calendar-phase routing, an explicit prior owner decision per that function's own comment — "both were real options, presented and decided, not defaulted to"). That means the hero this feature was just built for is **currently unreachable** in production: today is August 15th, NFL preseason, with three real live games in progress as this was written — and the homepage hero shows MLB instead, every single day, from March through October. The gap isn't just preseason: September and October (the first ~9 weeks of the actual NFL regular season) are excluded too. Not changed this pass because it was a previously deliberate, presented decision — reopening it is the owner's call, not a silent override. Options, for the owner to pick from: (a) leave the calendar rule as-is — NFL's new hero treatment activates automatically come November, no further action; (b) widen the window to include September/October (the actual regular season), leaving only the pure-preseason Aug window on MLB; (c) replace the fixed calendar gate with the leverage-scoring approach MLB's own hero already uses for picking its game (`combinedPct`/`leverage` scoring) — compare NFL's best live/upcoming game against MLB's on a shared score, sport-agnostic, so whichever sport has the more compelling live game wins the hero any day of the year, preseason included. (c) is the most work but is the only option that would have surfaced tonight's real live NFL preseason drama on the homepage at all.

**Implications:** none of D-099's build is wasted regardless of which option the owner picks — the live-detail reuse ships and activates the moment `_homeHeroSport()` (or whatever replaces it) selects NFL, on any date. Not live-verified after deploy yet — next check: confirm the live-detail block renders on a real production hero the next time the calendar window (or a future scoring change) puts NFL in the hero seat.

## D-100 — Home hero: cross-sport leverage scoring replaces the calendar gate (2026-08-15)

**Status:** complete (build shipped, live-verification pending deploy)
**Contributors:** Owner (decision), Vera (spec), Axiom (build)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-15 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-15

**Decision (owner, resolving D-099's flagged gap):** replace `_homeHeroSport()`'s fixed Nov–Feb calendar gate with cross-sport leverage scoring — the same approach MLB's own hero already uses internally to pick its game, extended to compare across sports. Chosen over the two lighter options (leave as-is; widen the window to include Sept/Oct) because it's the only one that puts real live NFL action on the homepage on a night like tonight, preseason included, rather than waiting for a fixed date.

**Build (Axiom):** `_renderHomeHero()` now fetches both sports' scoreboards every home load (MLB's existing `games` param plus a new `fetchNFLScoreboard()` call, cached in `AppState.nflGames` exactly like the NFL Scores page's own cache) and scores each sport's best live candidate on a shared currency before picking a winner. New `_nflLeverage(g)`/`_nflMarquee(g)` are calibrated to land in roughly MLB's existing `leverage()`/`marquee()` numeric range (~1–27 pre-favorite-bonus, both +100 once a followed team is playing) using only data the hero already holds — no new fetch beyond the scoreboard call itself:
  - `period` (quarter) stands in for MLB's inning, scaled ×2 since NFL only runs 4 quarters against MLB's 9.
  - Point-diff closeness stands in for MLB's run-diff closeness, rescaled for NFL's larger scoring margins (one-/two-score games, not 1–5 runs) rather than reusing MLB's literal thresholds.
  - A national broadcast (D-097's already-parsed field) stands in for MLB's combined-win-pct signal — team records aren't part of the NFL scoreboard payload the hero already holds, and adding a second fetch just for hero scoring would undercut the "zero new fetch" principle every other feature tonight held to. ESPN's own choice of which games get national coverage is a reasonable, already-free proxy for "this is a marquee matchup."
  - New `_nflGameHasFav(g)` mirrors `_gameHasFav(g)` (P5) for NFL team follows — `_isFollowed()` already takes a sport parameter, so this is a one-line extension, not new follow-tracking.

Live candidates from both sports (if any) are compared first — live always beats upcoming, matching the original per-sport behavior — and only when neither sport has a live game does the marquee (upcoming) comparison run. `_homeHeroSport()` is superseded and no longer called (left in place, documented, as the historical record of why the window was ever calendar-shaped); `_renderHomeHeroNFL()` — its NFL-only wrapper — is fully removed (dead code with no remaining caller), following the same "remove and leave a provenance comment" pattern the codebase already uses (`loadNFLHome()`, D-076).

**Verified before shipping (live, not static — and a live-verify methodology lesson worth keeping):** the first verification attempt gave a false negative — reloading `/js/app.js` from the *live production server* to test local edits actually re-fetched the currently-deployed (pre-D-100) code, not the on-disk changes, so the test silently exercised old logic and both directions "passed" by defaulting to MLB. Caught by checking `_renderHomeHero.toString()` for the new logic and finding it absent. Corrected by injecting the actual on-disk patched source directly into the live page via a real `<script>` tag (verified against the file on disk, not assumed) — this also required a second Ctrl+Shift+R hard reload mid-session, because `_heroFromNFLGame` itself was still running a stale pre-D-099 copy from this tab's earlier browsing (the same `max-age=14400` HTTP-cache gotcha D-097's methodology note already flagged, now confirmed to affect `app.js` too, not just `nfl.js`). With genuinely fresh code confirmed both ways: a synthetic high-leverage live NFL game (Q4, one-score game, national broadcast, red zone — scored 122.1 including this session's real followed-team bonus) correctly beat the real live MLB game on the board that night (COL@SF, 9th inning, scored 16.2); a synthetic low-leverage live NFL game (Q1 blowout, no broadcast — scored 2) correctly lost to the same MLB game. Also confirmed the true regression case: with no NFL game live at all (the actual state at test time — Friday's preseason games had gone final, Saturday's hadn't kicked off), the hero correctly fell back to MLB-only, unchanged from before. Full render screenshotted for the NFL-wins case: `LIVE · NBC` kicker, situation line, and all three leader rows rendering cleanly in the hero.

**Verified locally:** `node --check` clean on `js/app.js`, 0 NUL bytes, grep confirmed zero remaining live references to `_renderHomeHeroNFL`/`_homeHeroSport` outside comments. Full unit suite clean, manifest clean. `sw.js` bumped v180→v181.

**Implications:** every home page load now makes one additional proxied API call (NFL scoreboard, `ApiCache.TTL.SHORT`) regardless of season or date — a deliberate, accepted cost, not an oversight. The favorite-team bonus (`_nflGameHasFav`) means a fan who follows an NFL team can now see that team's live game win the homepage hero over an MLB game even in the strictest sense of "less objectively compelling," matching the existing MLB behavior (P5) exactly — same tradeoff, now applied consistently across both sports. Not live-verified after deploy yet — next check: confirm the scoreboard fetch and scoring run correctly on a real production load, and specifically confirm the fallback-to-MLB path still works correctly when no NFL game is live (the common case outside game windows).

**Live-verified after push (2026-08-15/16, `sportstrata-v181`).** Confirmed deployed and working correctly on a real production load — and better than planned: with no game live in either sport at check time, the hero picked NFL's upcoming LAR@KC over any MLB upcoming game, entirely correctly, because the account follows the Chiefs (`_nflGameHasFav`'s +100 bonus, mirroring MLB's existing P5 behavior) and the game carries a national broadcast (`NFL Net`, threaded into the upcoming-state kicker per D-099). This is the first real instance of the cross-sport comparison actually swinging the hero to NFL in production, not just a synthetic test. Zero console errors on a fresh hard-reloaded load. D-100 closed.

## D-101 — shareCard.js: fall back to download when navigator.share() itself fails (2026-08-16)

**Status:** complete (live-verified before shipping)
