## D-115 — NFL player-page visual/structural parity with MLB, Phase 1 (breadcrumb, bio chips, stat tiles, section order)
**Status:** pushed and live-verified on production — all four phases shipped and confirmed, D-115 closed
**Contributors:** Axiom (root-cause, fix, live preview each phase, post-deploy verification)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-22 | **Date resolved:** 2026-08-22 (all four phases, same day)

**Trigger (owner):** "we need to start a workflow to bring the nfl player pages up to speed with mlb. They should look as identical as possible" — a follow-up to D-114, same session. Clarified via a follow-up question that "start a workflow" meant "start the work," not literal multi-agent orchestration.

**Root-cause approach:** read MLB's `showMLBPlayerDetail` (`js/mlb.js`) in full as the reference implementation and diffed it against NFL's `_renderNFLPlayerDetail`/`_loadNFLPlayerStats`/`_loadNFLAdvanced` (`js/nfl.js`), plus the shared `js/detailFrame.js` component (`detailHeader()`/`detailSection()`, built under D-044 P1 explicitly "so MLB / NFL / NCAAF stay in visual parity") that NFL already uses but MLB itself never migrated onto. Found four concrete gaps, all fixable by reusing classes/patterns that already exist in the codebase — no new CSS:

1. **Breadcrumb bug.** `showNFLPlayerDetail` and `showNFLEspnPlayer` both called `setBreadcrumb('nfl-player', null)` — singular, not a key in `js/navigation.js`'s `_NAV_META` map (the real key is `'nfl-players'`, plural). `setBreadcrumb()` falls back to `{label: root}` for an unknown key, so the breadcrumb and `document.title` printed the literal string "nfl-player" instead of a real label — confirmed live in production before the fix (tab title read "nfl-player — SportStrata"; MLB's equivalent calls use the correct `setBreadcrumb('mlb-players', ...)`).
2. **Bio table vs. bio chip strip.** NFL rendered player bio facts (age, experience, height, weight, college, jersey, depth chart, status) as a boxed `.detail-row`/`.detail-label`/`.detail-value` table inside its own "Player Profile" `detailSection`. MLB has no such section at all — its bio facts (age, bats/throws, height/weight, born, debut, draft) render as a `.player-bio-grid`/`.player-bio-item` chip strip embedded directly in the hero, via a `#mlb-bio-strip` div populated async. `detailFrame.js`'s own `slots` field (added under D-044 P1) was built for exactly this — its doc comment literally names `'<div id="mlb-bio-strip"></div>'` as the example — but NFL never used it.
3. **Stat tiles used raw inline styles** instead of the existing `.stats-grid`/`.stat-item`/`.stat-value`/`.stat-label` classes MLB's Season Totals card uses, so the numbers looked visually inconsistent with every other stat-grid on the site.
4. **Section order was reversed.** NFL rendered "Key Metrics · Next Gen Stats" before the season stat line; MLB's order is Season Totals -> Key Metrics.

**Fix (`js/nfl.js`):** breadcrumb key corrected to `'nfl-players'` in both call sites, plus a second `setBreadcrumb('nfl-players', p.full_name)` call once the player resolves (mirrors MLB's `setBreadcrumb('mlb-players', player.fullName)` exactly). Bio array's map now emits `.player-bio-item`/`.bio-label`/`.bio-value` instead of `.detail-row`, wired into `detailHeader({...})`'s new `slots: ['<div class="player-bio-grid">...']` field; the standalone "Player Profile" `detailSection` call and its `_profileBody` variable were removed since the bio now lives in the hero, matching MLB. `_loadNFLPlayerStats`'s per-group stat chips now render as `<div class="stat-item"><div class="stat-value">...</div><div class="stat-label">...</div></div>` inside a `.stats-grid`, keeping the group-label subheading (Passing/Rushing/etc.) since that's an NFL-appropriate distinction MLB doesn't need — not a fabricated pattern, just a category header above a now-standard tile grid. `#nfl-stat-line` and `#nfl-advanced` swapped order in the render template.

**Explicitly deferred (flagged, not silently dropped) — needs new logic beyond pure class reuse, not a Phase 1 fit:**
- **Hero share-icon button** (MLB's `#mlb-hero-share` + `shareStatCard()`) — MLB can wire this synchronously because `stats`/`leagueRanks` are already computed before the header renders; NFL's header renders before season stats load asynchronously, and there's no existing per-position "headline stat" mapping (OPS/ERA for MLB hitters/pitchers has no NFL analog — QB headline stat isn't RB's). Doable, but real new logic, not a reuse-only fix.
- **Stat Profile radar chart** — MLB's `StatsCharts.mlbRadar()` is heavily MLB-specific (hand-tuned per-position elite-ceiling maxes for 10 fielding positions). NGS's pre-computed `pct` (0-100 percentile, already fetched by `_loadNFLAdvanced`) is a plausible input for an NFL-appropriate radar without inventing new scaling logic, but needs its own chart function.
- **Career section** — NFL's `_loadNFLCareer` already renders a reasonable year-by-year table; further unifying it with MLB's trend-chart-plus-pills treatment is lower priority.
- **Out of scope entirely:** Card download / Blurb / Scout Report / Compare — all MLB-only AI/backend features with no NFL equivalent infrastructure anywhere in the codebase. Building these would be new feature work, not a parity pass.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean on `js/nfl.js` and `sw.js`; 0 NUL bytes on both. Pre-push live preview on real production (`sportstrata.cc/nfl/player/4046`, Patrick Mahomes): cleared SW + caches, confirmed the live (unpatched) page actually showed the bug (`document.title` read literally "nfl-player", 8 `.detail-row` elements, Key Metrics before Season Stats), then injected the patched `_renderNFLPlayerDetail`/`_loadNFLPlayerStats` source into the live tab via console and re-rendered in place: breadcrumb resolved to "NFL Players > Patrick Mahomes", 0 `.detail-row` remaining, 8 `.player-bio-item` chips rendered in the hero, 11 `.stat-item` tiles rendered correctly grouped by Passing/Rushing, Season Stats now precedes Key Metrics. Screenshotted side-by-side against a live MLB player page (`/mlb/player/665742`, Juan Soto) confirming the breadcrumb chip style, hero bio-chip-strip placement, and stat-tile grid now visually match MLB's pattern.

**Shipped:** `sw.js` `CACHE_NAME` bumped v211 -> v212 (`js/nfl.js` is a cached static asset). Committed via the mount-safe git-plumbing workaround, commit `e59c313`, exactly 2 files touched (confirmed via `git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-status -r HEAD`). Not yet pushed — owner needs to push, then this entry gets a post-deploy live-verification update per this repo's process (fresh tab, no carried-over SW/cache state, per the D-113 stale-while-revalidate lesson).

**Update 2026-08-22 (Axiom) — pushed, post-deploy live-verification passed, and one real incidental bug found + fixed.** Confirmed the push landed (`git fetch origin main` matches local HEAD). Live-verified on real production in a fresh tab: hit the exact stale-while-revalidate trap D-113 already documented — even a brand-new tab still ran the old cached `js/nfl.js` (confirmed via `window._renderNFLPlayerDetail.toString()` showing pre-fix code) despite the deployed file itself already being correct (`fetch('/js/nfl.js', {cache:'no-store'})` showed the new code immediately, and `cf-cache-status: EXPIRED` ruled out a Cloudflare edge cache issue) — a plain browser HTTP cache hit on `/js/nfl.js` (`cache-control: public, max-age=14400`) was the culprit, not the Service Worker (already unregistered/cleared) or Cloudflare. A hard reload (Ctrl+Shift+R) resolved it. Once genuinely fresh: `/nfl/player/4046` (Mahomes, deep-link) and `/#nfl-player-7564` (Ja'Marr Chase, in-app nav from the players grid) both confirmed correct — breadcrumb resolves to the real name, 0 leftover `.detail-row`, 8 bio chips, correct `.stat-item` tile grid grouped by category, Season Stats before Key Metrics. Screenshots match the pre-push preview exactly.

While spot-checking the second breadcrumb call site (`showNFLEspnPlayer`, the all-time/retired-player route at `#nfl-player-espn-<id>`), found it throws `ReferenceError: teamColor is not defined` and has been silently falling back to the players list on every single visit — a pre-existing bug, unrelated to this entry's changes, sitting in the same function this entry already touched for its breadcrumb-key fix. Fixed by declaring `teamColor` the same way `_renderNFLPlayerDetail` already does (`(prof.team && getNFLTeamColor(prof.team)) || posColor`), and also resolved this route's breadcrumb with the player's name once loaded (it previously only ever showed the plain "NFL Players" root label, even though the D-115 fix pattern was already designed to cover it). Live-verified by injecting the patched function into the still-broken production route and re-invoking it: Mahomes's full career table now renders (was a hard crash before). Shipped as its own commit (`eedb05a`) so it stays independently revertable from the Phase 1 parity work. Not yet pushed as of this update.

**Update 2026-08-22 (Axiom) — Phases 2-4 shipped: all three deferred items closed.** Owner confirmed "all three, in that order" (radar, hero share, Career unification) via a follow-up clarifying question. Each phase live-previewed in a real production tab before commit (injecting the patched functions and re-invoking against real player data), same discipline as Phase 1/the teamColor fix.

**Phase 2 (`187b7bc`) — Stat Profile radar chart.** New `StatsCharts.nflRadar()` in `js/charts.js`, positioned before Season Stats to match MLB's Stat Profile placement exactly. Unlike `mlbRadar`, no hand-tuned position maxes were needed: `_loadNFLAdvanced`'s Next Gen Stats `pct` values are already 0-100 percentiles. Found and fixed a real bug during live preview before it shipped: some NGS metrics (e.g. Aggressiveness, Avg Cushion — small-qualified-sample stats) have `pct: null`, and the first draft plotted those as a fake 0, which reads as "worst possible" instead of "no data yet." Fixed by filtering null-pct axes out entirely and matching dataset values to axes by label rather than position. Gated on >=3 real axes so a 1-2 point shape never renders. Live-verified on Mahomes (6 real axes of 8) and Ja'Marr Chase (5 of 8).

**Phase 3 (`efde4ec`) — hero share-icon button.** Added `#nfl-hero-share` next to the position badge, matching MLB's `#mlb-hero-share` placement exactly (`detailHeader()`'s `chips` slot). MLB wires this synchronously since its season stats are already resolved before the header renders; NFL's header renders first, so the button is wired (or removed, if no headline stat resolves) once `_loadNFLPlayerStats` completes, using `onclick =` assignment rather than `addEventListener` since the header persists across a season switch and would otherwise stack duplicate handlers. New `_NFL_HEADLINE_STAT` map picks one summary stat per skill position (QB passing YDS, RB/FB rushing YDS, WR/TE receiving YDS), matched by stat group KEY first then label — necessary because a QB's own `data.groups` has a "YDS" entry in passing, rushing, AND receiving, and only the group-key match picks the right one (confirmed live: Mahomes' three YDS values were 3,587 / 422 / -10). Live-verified end-to-end: clicked the button on a real production tab and confirmed `shareStatCard()` fired and the card saved successfully with the correct headline stat.

**Phase 4 (`b59f490`) — Career year-over-year trend chart.** Reused MLB's own `StatsCharts.careerTrend()` (already generic, no MLB-specific logic inside) and its `.career-trend-section`/`.career-stat-btn` classes, attached to the first career-table category with a defined trend-stat set and >=2 seasons — Passing for a QB, Rushing for a RB, Receiving for a WR/TE — rather than forcing NFL's multi-category-at-once career tables (a QB gets both Passing and Rushing) into MLB's one-table-one-trend shape. Found and fixed a real data-shape bug during live preview: `/api/nflcareer` returns display-formatted values ("5,097") in a positional array, and `careerTrend()`'s own `parseFloat` silently truncates a comma-formatted string at the comma (parsing "5,097" as 5) — fixed by stripping commas and converting to real numbers before building the `{season, stats}` rows the chart function expects. Live-verified on Mahomes (Passing trend, 4 pills, values cross-checked against the visible table for both YDS and TD) and Ja'Marr Chase (correctly picked Receiving over Rushing as the primary category).

All three phases' `sw.js` bump (v212 -> v213, in Phase 2's commit) covers Phases 2-4 together since Phase 3/4 only touched `js/nfl.js` again on top of the same still-undeployed bump.

**Update 2026-08-22 (Axiom) — pushed, post-deploy live-verification passed on all three phases.** Confirmed the push landed (`git fetch origin main` matches local HEAD at `ab2a84c`). Live-verified on a fresh tab against real production, past the same browser-HTTP-cache trap hit twice already this session (SW/cache clear alone wasn't enough; a hard reload — Ctrl+Shift+R — was required to get the executing page off the cached pre-deploy JS): `/nfl/player/4046` (Mahomes) confirmed the Stat Profile radar renders in the correct position with the correct 6 real (non-null-pct) axes and real KC team color, the `#nfl-radar-card` wrapper is visible (`display: ''`, not the default `none`), the hero share button exists and has a wired `onclick`, and the Career trend chart exists (`Chart.getChart('nfl-pd-career-trend')`) with all 4 expected pills (YDS/TD/INT/RTG). Breadcrumb still correctly resolves to "NFL Players > Patrick Mahomes". Screenshot matches every pre-push preview exactly.

**Escalation:** none — all four phases are additive and fail-safe (no routing/data-fetch contract changed, only render output), each shipped as its own commit per this repo's "one PR per phase" convention. D-115 is fully closed; no further NFL player-page parity work is queued.

## D-116 — Trophy Case: cross-sport career achievements engine, NFL Phase 1 shipped

**Status:** shipped and live-verified (pre-push, real-production preview) — engine + NFL seed data (3 players) built and verified; committed, not yet pushed. Open item: the ongoing seasonal-upkeep commitment for hand-curated award data (see Decision below) — not a blocker to shipping what's here, but an owner call for how this scales past NFL.
**Contributors:** Relay (data sourcing), Axiom (schema + implementation + live verification), Vera (states spec — especially the zero-award empty case), Kael (visual spec, including a mid-session pivot after an owner-supplied mockup)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-23

**Trigger (owner):** requested a "Trophy Case" section on player detail pages — career hardware (championships, championship MVPs, season MVPs, and whatever else the team lands on per sport) — explicitly scoped to initialize for NFL but designed so MLB/NCAAF/NCAAB/WNBA get it for free later, not a redo per sport. Explicitly flagged the illustrative example given in the brief (a specific player's award count) as **not verified data** — anything real shipped on a live card had to be checked against a real source first, since award/championship history is historical record and more seasons may have happened by ship time.

**Scope check first:** searched ISSUES.md/DECISIONS.md/GOALS.md for any prior award/trophy/MVP/championship-history entry — none found. This is genuinely new ground, not a reopening of scoped/deferred work.

**Decision needed — data sourcing:** no live API this site already uses (MLB Stats API, ESPN, Sleeper, nflverse) exposes career award or championship history for any sport it covers (Relay). The only realistic source is hand-curated static JSON, matching the existing `data/trades.json`/`data/stadiums.json` precedent — static curated assets are not new to this repo, just a new use of the pattern. The real open question isn't the schema (that's settled, see below) — it's the ongoing commitment to keep the data current every season as more championships/MVPs are awarded, which nothing here automates.

**Options considered:**
1. Ratify hand-curated JSON as the permanent pattern for this feature, ship a small, real, verified NFL seed set now, and treat "keep it current" as a standing manual-upkeep task, not a blocker.
2. Wait for an automated data source to exist before building anything.
3. Ship the engine with zero seed data, leave all sports empty until someone curates data later.

**Decision:** Option 1. Built the full generic engine (per-sport taxonomy config -> one shared render function, same pattern as `detailFrame.js`'s `detailHeader()`/`detailSection()`) and shipped real, individually-verified data for exactly 3 NFL players — Patrick Mahomes, Kenneth Walker (III), Matthew Stafford — rather than a larger but unverified set. Every fact was checked against a primary/wire source this session (ESPN, NFL.com, CBS Sports, Fox Sports), not carried over from training-data assumptions; this caught a genuinely surprising real result (the Seahawks, not a "usual suspect" contender, won Super Bowl LX, which is why Kenneth Walker and not some other RB is in the seed set) that validated the verify-before-shipping discipline the brief asked for.

**Rationale:** the schema risk was low and worth settling now (a taxonomy-config-per-sport engine costs the same to build well once as to build narrowly for NFL and redo four times later). The data risk is the opposite — hand-typing awards is exactly the kind of task that's cheap to get wrong and expensive to get wrong quietly (a stale or incorrect trophy count is a factual error on a public page, not a cosmetic bug) — so scope there stayed deliberately small and fully verified rather than broad and assumed.

**Implications:**
- MLB/NCAAF/NCAAB/WNBA get the engine for free — adding a sport is a taxonomy config entry plus a `data/awards-<sport>.json` file, no render-logic changes. Confirmed by construction (the engine takes `sport` as a plain parameter throughout) but not yet exercised with real data for any of the four.
- NFL's seed data is intentionally tiny (3 players) — this is a seed, not coverage. Most NFL players will correctly show no Trophy Case at all (see Vera's empty-state note in ISSUES.md), which is the truthful state, not a gap to fill later.
- **Not yet written:** a seasonal-upkeep checklist (when to add a new champion/MVP after each season ends) — flagged as future work for Folio, not done in this pass.

**Mid-session pivot (owner-driven, not a technical revision):** initial build (placement: after the career-stats table; visual: flat emoji icons in a horizontal wrap grid) was fully built and live-verified, then the owner shared a reference mockup and said the case should "sit at the top or to the side of the page" and "resemble an actual case" with "3d photos of the actual trophies." Two real constraints existed against the literal ask: no image-generation tool in this session, and real photography of official league trophies carries copyright/trademark risk on a live public site. Raised both via a clarifying question rather than guessing; owner chose **polished CSS/SVG illustrations** over the alternative (a placeholder/no-imagery option) and **side rail** over top-of-page placement. Rebuilt around both answers — see Verified section below for what actually shipped.

**What shipped (V2, matches owner's chosen direction):**
- **Placement:** sticky side rail, reusing `.nlg-layout`/`.nlg-main`/`.nlg-side` — the exact two-column pattern already established on the NFL live-game page (`css/nflLiveGame.css`) — rather than inventing new sidebar CSS. Collapses to a single stacked column below the main content under the same already-shipped `@media (max-width: 900px)` rule that pattern already carries.
- **Imagery:** inline SVG icons with a shared gold linear-gradient (`--color-award` -> `--accent-light`) standing in for trophy photography — group 1 (championship) = trophy cup, group 2 (individual award) = medal, fallback = rosette. No external assets, no copyright exposure.
- **Shell:** `.nlg-side-card`/`.nlg-side-title` (existing sidebar-widget classes) instead of `detailSection()` — matches the sidebar's own established look rather than importing the main-column card style into it.
- **Color:** new semantic tokens `--color-award`/`--color-award-subtle`/`--color-award-border` (`css/variables.css`, both themes) — explicitly distinct from `--accent` (brand orange, D-047 invariant #3: brand orange is brand-only) and from stat-category/`--color-tier-*` tokens (different meanings, same rule DESIGN.md already applies elsewhere).
- **Empty state:** deliberately no card at all when a player has zero verified achievements (the ~99% case) — not a "0 trophies" placeholder. DESIGN.md's "empty states name their way out" rule doesn't apply here since there's no way out of not having won a championship yet (Vera).

**Verified (this session, real production, both V1 and V2):** `node --check` clean and 0 NUL bytes on every touched/new file (`js/nfl.js`, `js/achievements.js`, `css/variables.css`, `css/components.css`, `data/awards-nfl.json`, `index.html`, `sw.js`); `git diff HEAD` reviewed file-by-file to confirm each edit is isolated to exactly the intended change (a stale local git index briefly made an unrelated, already-committed D-115 diff look uncommitted — resolved by diffing against real `HEAD`, not the stale index, before touching anything). Live-verified V2 by injecting the real patched `_renderNFLPlayerDetail` plus the real `achievements.js` engine into a live production tab (fetch-intercepted `data/awards-nfl.json` to the real seed data) and re-rendering real players in place: Mahomes (3-tile case: 3 Super Bowl, 2 NFL MVP, 3 Super Bowl MVP, correct grouping/sort/season lists), Kenneth Walker (2-tile: 1 Super Bowl, 1 Super Bowl MVP, both 2025), Matthew Stafford (1-tile: 1 NFL MVP, 2025), Josh Allen (zero seeded awards — container stays genuinely empty, no stray box/border), and a direct fail-closed check on an unseeded sport (mocked 404 on `data/awards-mlb.json` — no exception, container untouched). Confirmed visually correct in both dark and light theme (award gold token swaps correctly, contrast holds in both). Mobile collapse relies on the same `@media (max-width: 900px)` rule already proven on the NFL live-game page, not independently re-verified via window resize this session (that tool didn't reliably engage in this environment) — flagged, not asserted.

**Shipped:** `sw.js` `CACHE_NAME` bumped v213 -> v214 (`js/achievements.js` added to `STATIC_ASSETS`). Not yet pushed — owner needs to push, then this entry gets a post-deploy live-verification update per this repo's standard process.

**Escalation:** none for what shipped — additive only, fails closed on missing/malformed data, no existing route or data contract changed. The standing open item is the data-sourcing upkeep commitment named above, which is a process question for the owner, not a build blocker.


**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom) — pushed, post-deploy live-verification found and fixed one real bug.** Confirmed the push landed (`git fetch origin main` matched local HEAD at `8c065ce`). Live-verified on real production in a fresh tab, past the same browser-HTTP-cache trap D-113/D-115 already documented (a plain navigation still ran the pre-deploy `js/nfl.js`; a hard reload was required to get the executing page onto the deployed code).

Once genuinely fresh, found a real bug: visiting the player page via its real path-based URL (`sportstrata.cc/nfl/player/4046`, the D-041 SEO-crawlable route — not the in-app hash route, `#nfl-player-4046`) left the Trophy Case silently empty even for Mahomes, who has verified data. Root cause: `_loadAwardsData()` fetched `data/awards-${sport}.json` as a **relative** URL. On the hash route the document's path is `/`, so the relative fetch resolves correctly to `/data/awards-nfl.json`; on the path-based route the document's path is `/nfl/player/4046`, so the same relative fetch resolves to `/nfl/player/data/awards-nfl.json` — a nonexistent path that falls through to the SPA's HTML shell, which then fails `res.json()` and fails closed to "no data" exactly as designed... which is precisely why it wasn't loud: fail-closed is the correct behavior for missing/bad data, but here it was masking a real bug rather than an actual missing-data case. Confirmed every other fetch in `js/nfl.js` (a file that also runs on this same path-based route) already uses an absolute leading-slash path (`/api/...`) — `achievements.js` was the one outlier, not an established site convention (the `data/trades.json`/`data/stadiums.json` relative-fetch precedent in `js/arcade.js` never surfaced this because arcade has no equivalent path-based route to break it).

**Fix:** `js/achievements.js` — `fetch(\`data/awards-${sport}.json\`)` -> `fetch(\`/data/awards-${sport}.json\`)`. One-line change, live-verified by injecting the corrected fetch into the still-broken production route and re-invoking `initTrophyCase` against real data: Mahomes's 3-tile case now renders correctly at `/nfl/player/4046`. `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes. Shipped as its own commit so it stays independently revertable from the Phase 1 build.


**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom) — owner reported "not displaying" right after the fetch-path fix push; root-caused to a caching-layer gap, fixed.** Re-verified on real production, fresh tabs, both routes (`sportstrata.cc/#nfl-player-4046` and `sportstrata.cc/nfl/player/4046`), both render paths (`_renderNFLPlayerDetail` for active roster, `showNFLEspnPlayer` for the all-time/retired ESPN route via `#nfl-player-espn-3139477`), and all three seeded players (Mahomes 3-tile, Kenneth Walker 2-tile, Stafford 1-tile) — all correct at verification time.

The likely explanation for the owner's report: two caching layers sit between a push and what a visitor's browser actually executes, and the previous fix commit only accounted for the fetch-path bug itself, not the deploy-propagation gap. (1) Cloudflare's edge cache serves `/js/achievements.js` with `cache-control: public, max-age=14400` independent of a client's own `cache:'no-store'` fetch option — confirmed live by seeing `cf-cache-status: HIT` return stale (pre-fix) source moments after the push, self-resolving to `REVALIDATED` shortly after. (2) `sw.js` runs stale-while-revalidate for static assets, and the previous fix commit didn't bump `CACHE_NAME` — so any browser that already had the old `sportstrata-v214` cache active would get served the stale cached copy of `js/achievements.js` on its next load (by design, that's what stale-while-revalidate does — serve cached, refresh in background for *next* time), extending the stale window by one more load per returning visitor instead of the SW forcing a full re-fetch on activate. Every prior static-asset change in this repo's history (D-113, D-115) bumped `CACHE_NAME` for exactly this reason; this one didn't, which was the actual process gap.

**Fix:** `sw.js` `CACHE_NAME` bumped v214 -> v215 (no other changes) — forces every client's SW to drop the old cache and fully re-fetch all `STATIC_ASSETS`, including the already-fixed `js/achievements.js`, on next activate, rather than relying on stale-while-revalidate's slower self-heal plus the separate Cloudflare edge TTL. `node --check` clean, 0 NUL bytes.


**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom, per owner request) — taxonomy expanded to 7 more NFL award types, real verified data added.** Owner asked to add OPOY, DPOY, OROTY, DROTY, All-Pro, Walter Payton Man of the Year, Comeback Player of the Year, and Pro Bowl. `all_pro` already existed in the taxonomy (added at Phase 1 but never had seed data); the other six are new taxonomy entries. No render-engine changes were needed — this is exactly the extensibility the taxonomy-config pattern was built for at Phase 1.

**New taxonomy entries (`js/achievements.js`):** `opoy` (AP Offensive Player of the Year) and `dpoy` (AP Defensive Player of the Year) placed in group 2, alongside `season_mvp`/`championship_mvp` — these are AP-voted, MVP-adjacent individual honors. `oroty`/`droty` (rookie-of-the-year), `walter_payton_moty`, and `comeback_poy` placed in group 3 alongside the existing `all_pro`/`conference_championship` — real honors, but for a narrower cohort (rookies) or a different axis (character/community for Walter Payton, not performance) than a league MVP. `pro_bowl` given its own new **group 4**, sorted last — it's a different kind of honor in frequency and selection process (a roster nomination for an exhibition game, voted by fans/players/coaches, that a decorated veteran can rack up 6+ of) than a single-winner, season-ending award, and grouping it with the others would have buried the rarer honors under a noisier one.

**Data verification (real sources, cross-checked — the same discipline as Phase 1, since this is historical record going on a live page):**
- **Patrick Mahomes:** AP Offensive Player of the Year, 2018 season — confirmed via Pro-Football-Reference's dedicated awards list (`pro-football-reference.com/awards/ap-offensive-player-of-the-year.htm`) and corroborated by CBS Sports/SI coverage from NFL Honors Feb. 2019. This one needed real scrutiny: an early automated page-summary pass mis-attributed 2018 to Todd Gurley and hallucinated a table that didn't match any other source — resolved by going back to a clean fetch of PFR's own dedicated award-list page plus independent news coverage, all agreeing on 2018. First-Team All-Pro, 2018 and 2022 seasons — each confirmed independently via ESPN/SI/Bleacher Report/Chiefs.com/Arrowhead Pride coverage of both years' AP All-Pro team announcements (his 2020 selection was Second-Team, not included, since the taxonomy's `all_pro` type is specifically First-Team, matching its existing Phase 1 label). Pro Bowl: 6 selections (2018–2023 seasons) — confirmed via PFR's player-page awards summary ("6x Pro Bowl") plus the specific season list; also confirmed he was **not** selected for 2024 or 2025 (real snub, not a gap in research).
- **Matthew Stafford:** AP Comeback Player of the Year, 2011 season (with Detroit) — confirmed via PFR. First-Team All-Pro, 2025 season (his age-37 breakout with the Rams) — confirmed via PFR, consistent with his real 2025 AP MVP win already in the seed data. Pro Bowl: 3 selections — 2014 (Detroit, injury replacement), 2023 and 2025 (Rams) — confirmed via PFR's summary line and cross-checked against Wikipedia and StatMuse independently. **Flagged, not resolved:** a Rams.com team article calling his most recent selection his "fourth career Pro Bowl nod" doesn't match PFR's own structured count or any other source found; went with the PFR-verified count of 3 as the more authoritative, internally-consistent source, but this is a real open discrepancy worth a second look if a fourth season ever surfaces from another source.
- **Kenneth Walker III:** verified **no new records** — checked all 7 categories plus All-Pro and found no wins: he was the runner-up (not winner) for 2022 AP Offensive Rookie of the Year (Garrett Wilson won), has zero Pro Bowl selections through the 2025 season, and no All-Pro selections found in any source. This is the correct outcome for a genuinely unadorned-in-these-categories player, not a research gap — his existing championship/championship MVP records (Super Bowl LX, verified in the original Phase 1 pass) are unaffected. One correction caught along the way, again: a page-summary tool twice mis-rendered his Super Bowl MVP as "Super Bowl LIX" — already corrected to the verified LX in the Phase 1 data and re-confirmed here; not touched by this update.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean on `js/achievements.js`, 0 NUL bytes; `data/awards-nfl.json` re-validated as JSON. Live-verified by injecting the updated taxonomy + engine + data into a real production tab and re-rendering Mahomes (6-tile case, correct group/sort order, Pro Bowl's 6-season list wraps cleanly at the sidebar's fixed width with no overflow) and Stafford (4-tile case, same check). No changes needed to `css/components.css` — the existing tile layout scales to more tiles and longer season lists without adjustment.


**Housekeeping note:** the prior `a9813c0` commit (SW CACHE_NAME v215->v216, shipped alongside the NFL taxonomy expansion) only got a commit message, not its own DECISIONS.md line — the taxonomy-expansion addendum above covers the *content* change but never explicitly logged the cache-bump half. Noting it here for the record; no further action needed, the bump itself shipped correctly.

**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom, per owner request) — MLB Trophy Case built and shipped.** Owner asked two things in one message: (1) how to automate the seasonal award-data refresh, (2) how feasible it'd be to bring Trophy Case to MLB. Automation: set up a yearly scheduled task (fires every Feb 20, after a season's postseason awards are final) that does AI research + owner-approval-before-commit — same shape as this repo's existing `content-queue.yml` weekly cron, adapted to Trophy Case's need for a human sanity-check on historical-record data rather than auto-publishing. Owner picked "scheduled AI research, you approve" and "yes, build MLB now" from the options presented.

**Feasibility, confirmed by building it:** MLB Trophy Case turned out easier than NFL was — one render function (`showMLBPlayerDetail`) instead of NFL's two, the `.nlg-layout` side-rail CSS already sport-agnostic and needing zero changes, and the taxonomy-config engine needing zero render-logic changes (exactly the extensibility it was built for at Phase 1). The one real gotcha, caught before shipping rather than after: `_normName()` (`js/config.js`) didn't strip diacritics, and MLB Stats API full names routinely carry them (Jose Altuve, Ronald Acuna Jr., etc. — NFL/Sleeper data happened to be all-ASCII, which is why this never surfaced before).

**Fix (`js/config.js`):** `_normName()` now runs `.normalize('NFD').replace(/[̀-ͯ]/g, '')` before the existing dot/suffix/whitespace normalization — same pattern already used for the Baseball Savant slug builder (`js/mlb.js` ~L1782). Verified as a no-op for already-ASCII names (NFL/NBA matching unaffected) and confirmed correct for accented + suffixed names via a standalone Node test: `"José Altuve"` -> `"jose altuve"`, `"Ronald Acuña Jr."` -> `"ronald acuna"` (the existing suffix-stripping regex removes "jr" as a whole word, same as it already did for "P.J. Washington Jr." -> "pj washington" — this matters because it means the awards-mlb.json key for Acuña is `"ronald acuna"`, **not** `"ronald acuna jr"`; almost shipped the wrong key here, caught by tracing the normalization pipeline before writing the data file).

**Taxonomy (`js/achievements.js`):** `ACHIEVEMENT_TAXONOMY.mlb` expanded from the Phase 1 stub (championship/championship_mvp/season_mvp/cy_young, groups 1-2) with 6 new types, following the same group-tiering logic already established for NFL. Group 3 (real honors, narrower cohort or a different axis than a league MVP): `rookie_of_year` (rookie cohort, mirrors NFL's oroty/droty), `gold_glove` and `silver_slugger` (positional/performance honors awarded per-position every year, mirrors All-Pro's role), `comeback_poy` (MLB's own official Comeback Player of the Year, same key name reused across the NFL and MLB taxonomies since it's the same kind of honor), `roberto_clemente` (character/community award, MLB's Walter Payton Man of the Year equivalent). Group 4 (own tier, sorted last): `all_star`, MLB's Pro Bowl equivalent — a roster selection a decorated veteran can rack up 8-9+ of, which would bury the rarer group-3 honors if mixed in with them.

**Side rail (`js/mlb.js`):** `showMLBPlayerDetail()`'s flat `.stats-card` stack (blurb card through the compare card) wrapped in `.nlg-layout` / `.nlg-main`, with a new `<aside class="nlg-side"><div id="mlb-trophy-case"></div></aside>` alongside it — the player-detail-header stays outside the layout wrapper, exactly matching NFL's placement convention. `initTrophyCase('mlb', player.fullName, 'mlb-trophy-case')` called right after the radar chart renders (same spot NFL calls it, right after its own stat-loading calls). No CSS changes needed — the side-rail classes are already sport-agnostic (`css/nflLiveGame.css`, loaded site-wide via `index.html`).

**Seed data (`data/awards-mlb.json`, new file) — 5 real players, 89 records, same verification discipline as NFL (cross-check every fact against at least two independently-sourced references, never trust a single AI-summarized page fetch for a contested detail):**
- **Shohei Ohtani** (LAA 2018-2023, LAD 2024-): 2018 AL Rookie of the Year; MVP 2021/2023 (LAA), 2024/2025 (LAD) — 4x, all unanimous; Silver Slugger same 4 seasons; All-Star 2021-2025 (5x); World Series champion 2024 & 2025 (LAD). No championship_mvp record — his 2025 postseason MVP honor was NLCS MVP, not World Series MVP, so it's correctly out of scope for this taxonomy's `championship_mvp` type (not invented).
- **Jose Altuve** (HOU, full career): AL MVP 2017; Gold Glove 2015; Silver Slugger 7x (2014-2018, 2022, 2024); All-Star 9x (2012, 2014-2018, 2021, 2022, 2024); World Series champion 2017 & 2022. **Caught and corrected an AI-summarization error here:** an initial Baseball-Reference page-summary claimed a 2021 World Series championship — the Astros lost the 2021 World Series to Atlanta, they did not win it. Cross-checked against Wikipedia and a dedicated Astros-championship-history search; corrected to the real 2017/2022 pair.
- **Zack Greinke** (KC 2004-2010, MIL 2011-2012, LAA 2012, LAD 2013-2015, ARI 2016-2019, HOU 2019, KC 2021-2023): 2009 AL Cy Young (KC); Gold Glove 6x — 2014/2015 (LAD), 2016-2019 (ARI); Silver Slugger 2x — 2013 (LAD), 2019 (ARI); All-Star 6x — 2009 (KC), 2014/2015 (LAD), 2017-2019 (ARI). No championship record — genuinely never won a World Series, the intentional "real decorated player with zero championships" counterpart to Greinke's honors, mirroring how Kenneth Walker's data intentionally shows zero individual awards despite a championship. **Needed real scrutiny on the 2019 awards:** Greinke was traded from Arizona to Houston at the July 31, 2019 deadline; confirmed via MLB.com's own reporting that both his 2019 NL Gold Glove and NL Silver Slugger are credited to Arizona (the team he spent the majority of the season with, and — for the pitcher-only Silver Slugger, an NL-only award pre-universal-DH — the only team of the two he could have earned it with), not Houston, despite Houston-market coverage of the announcement.
- **Mookie Betts** (BOS 2014-2019, LAD 2020-): AL MVP 2018 (BOS); Gold Glove 6x — 2016-2019 (BOS), 2020/2022 (LAD); Silver Slugger 7x — 2016/2018/2019 (BOS), 2020/2022-2024 (LAD); All-Star 8x — 2016-2019 (BOS), 2021-2024 (LAD); World Series champion 2018 (BOS), 2020/2024/2025 (LAD) — 4x; Roberto Clemente Award 2025. **Also needed real scrutiny:** three different automated summaries of his All-Star history disagreed with each other (one included an impossible 2020 selection — the All-Star Game was canceled outright that year for COVID, confirmed independently; another undercounted at 6). Resolved by fetching Wikipedia's infobox and requesting a verbatim character-for-character quote rather than a summary — "8× All-Star (2016–2019, 2021–2024)" — which is now the source of record.
- **Ronald Acuña Jr.** (ATL, full career): 2018 NL Rookie of the Year; NL MVP 2023; Silver Slugger 3x — 2019/2020/2023; All-Star 5x — 2019, 2021-2023, 2025; NL Comeback Player of the Year 2025 (confirmed via 7+ independent outlets — MLB.com, ESPN, TSN, Fox Sports, Yardbarker — all agreeing Acuña/deGrom won the 2025 AL/NL awards). **No championship record, deliberately:** the Braves won the 2021 World Series, but Acuña tore his ACL in July 2021 and was out for the rest of the season including the entire postseason — ESPN's own coverage frames the team's title as something they won "after losing Ronald Acuña Jr.," and Baseball-Reference's own page doesn't credit him with a championship appearance. Left out rather than guessed at, consistent with not inventing data for an ambiguous edge case.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean on `js/config.js`, `js/achievements.js`, `js/mlb.js`, `sw.js`; 0 NUL bytes on all four plus `data/awards-mlb.json`; JSON re-validated, all 5 name keys confirmed via a standalone Node run to exactly match `_normName(player.fullName)`'s real output for each player's actual display name (including the diacritic and suffix-stripping cases). `sw.js` `CACHE_NAME` bumped v216 -> v217 in the same commit as the code/data changes, avoiding the exact two-step-bump gap documented in the NFL fetch-path fix earlier in this file.

**Not yet done:** live-verification in a real browser against the deployed site (pending the owner's push + confirmation, same handoff pattern as every prior commit this session) and the scheduled seasonal-refresh task itself (set up separately, runs standalone, not tied to this commit).


**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom) — owner reported Lamar Jackson's page showing no Trophy Case; investigated, found and fixed two separate things.** First, confirmed this specific report was expected fail-closed behavior, not a regression: Lamar Jackson was simply never one of the 3 originally-seeded NFL players (Mahomes/Walker/Stafford) — a genuinely notable gap for a 2x MVP, so added him rather than just explaining the design intent away. Verified via PFR player page + PFR's own dedicated AP Offensive Player of the Year list page + a verbatim Wikipedia infobox quote: 2x NFL MVP (2019, 2023), 3x First-Team All-Pro (2019, 2023, 2024), 4x Pro Bowl (2019, 2021, 2023, 2024). Deliberately did **not** add an `opoy` record — his bio surfaces PFWA and Sporting News versions of Offensive Player of the Year plus a "101 Awards" AFC OPOY, but he does not appear on PFR's own dedicated AP OPOY winners list for any of his big seasons, and the taxonomy's `opoy` type is specifically the AP award (same source used for Mahomes' 2018 entry) — conflating a similarly-named award from a different voting body was exactly the mistake caught and avoided during the original Mahomes research, applied the same discipline here.

Second, while checking whether the live site was actually serving current data (it was — `data/awards-nfl.json` fetched clean from production, still showing only the original 3 players, confirming the *code* wasn't broken, just the data), found a real, previously-undiagnosed infrastructure bug: a plain fetch of the live `sw.js` returned `CACHE_NAME: 'sportstrata-v58'` — many versions stale — while the exact same URL with a cache-busting query param correctly returned `v217`. `sw.js` had no dedicated entry in `_headers`, so Cloudflare's edge was caching it like any other static `.js` asset with a long default TTL. This is backwards from how service worker update-checking is supposed to work: browsers deliberately fetch the SW script bypassing their own HTTP cache specifically so they can detect new bytes and trigger an install — but if the CDN edge itself is serving stale bytes regardless of the browser's cache-bypass, that detection never happens for any client hitting a stale edge node. This is a plausible root cause (or contributing factor) behind the *pattern* of "not displaying" reports this session, independent of whether any individual commit remembered to bump `CACHE_NAME`.

**Fix:** `_headers` — added an explicit `Cache-Control: no-cache` rule scoped to `/sw.js`, so Cloudflare always revalidates it against the origin rather than serving a cached copy. `sw.js` `CACHE_NAME` bumped v217 -> v218 in the same commit (data file changed again). `node --check` clean on `sw.js`, JSON re-validated, 0 NUL bytes across all three changed files.

**Not yet done:** can't independently re-verify the `_headers` fix took effect at the edge until after the owner pushes and enough time passes for Cloudflare to pick up the new header rule (or the owner purges cache manually) — worth a follow-up check on `sw.js`'s cache behavior specifically, not just the usual page-level live-verification.


**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom, per owner request) — replaced the hand-research bottleneck with a Wikidata-backed batch pipeline.** Owner's framing: hand-curating award data per player (conversationally researching each one against PFR/BR bio pages + dedicated award lists + Wikipedia infobox cross-checks, as done for all 9 players seeded so far) doesn't scale. Presented the real alternatives that were actually investigated rather than assumed: re-confirmed live that ESPN's core athlete API (`sports.core.api.espn.com/.../athletes/<id>`) has zero award/honor fields (already ruled out for MLB Stats API/Sleeper/nflverse at the original D-116 feasibility pass); PFR/BR are off the table for any *automated* pipeline since their terms restrict scraping (fine for the occasional manual lookup already done, not for something running unattended against hundreds of players). Wikidata's `award received` property (P166), qualified by `point in time` (P585), is the one real structured source — it's the same backend that generates the Wikipedia infobox lines this session kept falling back on for cross-checking (e.g. Mookie Betts' "8× All-Star (2016–2019, 2021–2024)"). Owner picked the Wikidata-backed batch pipeline, human-approves-before-merge option over a live on-demand-fetch alternative (which would've dropped the pre-ship human review step this whole session's verification discipline was built around) and over just formalizing the manual process without a new data source.

**What shipped:**
- `bot/trophy_case_research.py` — queries Wikidata's SPARQL endpoint for a batch of players at once: resolves each name to a QID (rejecting ambiguous matches rather than guessing — flags them for a human-supplied `--qid` override instead), pulls P166 award statements + P585 year qualifiers, cross-references P54 "member of sports team" intervals to resolve which team a given season's award belongs to, and maps Wikidata's award label text to this repo's taxonomy `type` keys via a maintained substring table (`AWARD_LABEL_MAP`). An award label that matches nothing in the table ships in the draft as `type: "UNMAPPED: <label>"` with `_needs_review: true` rather than being silently dropped — same "no silent caps" instinct as everywhere else in this feature. A season with no team-interval match, or more than one overlapping interval (a mid-season trade — the exact Zack Greinke 2019 case resolved by hand earlier this session), is flagged for review rather than guessed.
- `.github/workflows/trophy-case-research.yml` — mirrors the existing `content-queue.yml` pattern exactly: `contents: read` only, runs the script, uploads a draft `data/awards-<sport>-draft.json` + `summary.txt` as a build artifact, never commits directly. Fires on `workflow_dispatch` (manual, with sport/players/watchlist inputs) and on the same yearly cron as the scheduled Claude task below, so a draft exists even in a year that task can't reach it.
- `bot/trophy-case-watchlist-nfl.txt` / `-mlb.txt` — starting player lists for the scheduled batch runs (the 9 already-seeded players plus a broader set of current stars), explicitly documented as a living list, not a fixed roster.
- Updated the existing "SportStrata Trophy Case annual award refresh" scheduled task (`trig_015CRbfT9WoP2jWsnkrJXgRF`, unchanged yearly Feb 20 cadence) to make the Wikidata pipeline its primary path: check for `gh` CLI access on the owner's machine, trigger/find the workflow run, download the draft, review every flagged record plus a spot-check sample of unflagged ones (Wikidata coverage/label text is unverified until real runs prove it out), merge only what checks out through the same commit workflow used all session, never auto-push. The *original* ad hoc WebSearch-based refresh process was kept as an explicit fallback path in the same prompt for a firing where `gh` isn't available or the device isn't connected, rather than deleted — so a firing that can't reach the new pipeline still delivers value instead of doing nothing.

**Explicit caveat, stated plainly rather than glossed over:** this pipeline has **not been run against live Wikidata**. `wikidata.org` / `query.wikidata.org` are blocked from the fetch tools available in the session that wrote this code, so the SPARQL query shapes and property IDs (P166/P585/P54/P580/P582) are built against Wikidata's well-documented, stable public schema, but the *coverage and exact label text* for any real athlete is unverified until it actually runs somewhere with real network access (the GitHub Actions runner, or the owner's own machine). Smoke-tested the Python logic end-to-end against hand-built mock Wikidata responses instead — QID disambiguation (rejects ambiguous names rather than guessing), the award-label mapping table, the mid-season-trade team-conflict flag, and the `_norm_key()` diacritic/suffix stripping (verified to produce the exact same output as `js/config.js`'s real `_normName()` for "José Altuve" and "Ronald Acuña Jr.") all behaved correctly against synthetic data. The actual first live run — whenever it happens, whether triggered manually or by the workflow's own schedule — should be treated as a supervised dry run and reviewed with real scrutiny, not trusted as a working pipeline on faith.

**Verified:** `python3 -m py_compile` clean on `trophy_case_research.py`; `.github/workflows/trophy-case-research.yml` YAML-parsed successfully; 0 NUL bytes across all four new files.

**Not yet done:** an actual live run of the pipeline (needs either a manual `workflow_dispatch` trigger or waiting for the Feb 2027 scheduled fire — worth doing a manual dry run well before then to shake out any real-world Wikidata surprises the mock tests couldn't catch).


**Update 2026-08-23 (Axiom) — Lamar Jackson fix + Wikidata pipeline pushed, but still didn't display; root-caused for real this time.** Owner pushed, and the next round of "still not displaying" turned out to be a genuinely different and more fundamental caching layer than anything addressed so far this session, working through it live with the owner's own Cloudflare dashboard access (something this session's tools can't see directly).

**What actually happened, in order:** confirmed the push landed on `origin/main` (`b489b87`). Live fetches of `sportstrata.cc/data/awards-nfl.json` kept returning the pre-Lamar-Jackson 3-player list even with brand-new, never-before-used cache-busting query strings — which should be an unconditional cache miss, so this wasn't explained by ordinary edge-cache staleness the way earlier incidents were. Owner checked the Cloudflare Pages dashboard directly: deployment `b489b87` showed **Status: Success**, correctly attached to the Production environment and the `sportstrata.cc` custom domain. To isolate whether the *build* or the *domain* was the problem, fetched the deployment's own Cloudflare-generated alias (`zohn-sports-stats.pages.dev`) directly — it correctly returned all 4 players including Lamar Jackson. This proved the deployment itself was fine; something specific to how `sportstrata.cc` (a separate Cloudflare zone from `*.pages.dev`) was serving/caching content was the actual problem. Owner checked that zone's Cache Rules / Page Rules — found **none configured**. A manual "Purge Everything" on the zone immediately fixed it; owner's own browser (the real ground truth here, more reliable than this session's own fetch tooling turned out to be through this entire exchange) confirmed all 4 players rendering correctly afterward.

**Why "no custom rule" is still consistent with a caching root cause, not a mystery:** Cloudflare's *default* per-file-type edge caching (the same default behavior already documented earlier this session for `/js/achievements.js`'s `cache-control: public, max-age=14400`) applies automatically to static assets even with zero custom Cache Rules on a zone. Edge nodes had cached older copies of `data/awards-nfl.json` under that default multi-hour TTL *before* any of this session's fixes existed. Adding a `_headers` rule (as done for `sw.js` a few commits earlier) only changes what gets cached going forward — it cannot retroactively expire an object an edge PoP is already holding. Only a purge does that. This also means the earlier `sw.js` `Cache-Control: no-cache` fix was very likely correct and effective for *future* requests, but the specific staleness seen during the Lamar Jackson debugging thread was leftover pre-fix cache entries, not evidence the header rule itself failed.

**The actual prevention, not just the fix:** the immediate incident is resolved by the purge, but `data/*.json` had no `_headers` rule at all — meaning every *future* award-data push would ride the same multi-hour default edge TTL and need another manual purge to reach users promptly, the exact pattern that's now repeated three times this session in slightly different forms. Added an explicit `/data/*` rule: `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, must-revalidate` — deliberately not `no-cache` like `sw.js` (these files don't carry a service worker script's "must always fetch fresh bytes to detect an update" contract, so some edge caching is fine), just a short-enough TTL (5 minutes) that a future push doesn't require anyone to remember to go purge Cloudflare by hand.

**Lesson for this session's own tooling, worth being honest about:** this session's own fetch tool returned stale results for `sportstrata.cc` well past the point where the owner's real browser (and the `.pages.dev` alias) showed fresh content — including after the manual purge, on the very next check. Multiple uniquely-cache-busted URLs in a row all returned the same stale answer through this tool specifically. Whatever's happening there (a proxy-layer cache this session can't see or control) makes this tool measurably less reliable than the owner's own browser for live-verifying a production fix — worth defaulting to asking the owner to check directly rather than trusting this tool's own fetch results as the final word, especially when they disagree with dashboard-level evidence (a confirmed-successful deploy of the right commit).

**Verified:** 0 NUL bytes on `_headers` after the edit.

**Not yet done:** re-confirm `sw.js`'s `CACHE_NAME` shows `v218` live now that the purge has happened (not yet re-checked after this specific edit); the owner may want to spot-check the actual Lamar Jackson player page rendering (not just the raw JSON) once this commits and deploys.

## D-117 — MLB Live Game Viewer Reimagine: Relay data-feasibility audit + re-validated phased plan
**Status:** open — Phase 1 shipped + live-verified (commit 7a94868, 2026-08-23). Phase 2 shipped + live-verified (commit 4a5d561, 2026-08-24). Phase 3 shipped + live-verified (commit 75596fe, 2026-08-24) — cleared the 2026-06-12 D-009 trajectory-data gate for good. Phase 4 (Game Pulse hard-hit callouts + pitcher K-streak) shipped + live-verified (commit aaedfb1, 2026-08-24) — see ISSUES.md closeout for the hard-hit-real-event-vs-K-streak-synthetic-test distinction. Phase 5 (embedded player card, season stats + today's line only, no Trophy Case link per owner direction 2026-08-23) shipped + live-verified (commit 688a3dc, 2026-08-24) — see ISSUES.md closeout. Phase 6 (in-house win-expectancy model) not yet started, gated on Relay sourcing a real empirical win-expectancy table before Axiom codes anything.
**Contributors:** Relay (audit + Phase 2/3/4/5 addenda), Vera/Kael/Axiom (Phase 1-5 gates, see ISSUES.md), Axiom (Phase 1-5 implementation)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-23 | **Date resolved:** —

**Trigger:** owner brought an external LLM's UX analysis + ASCII mockups plus a reference screenshot for reimagining `mlb-live-{id}` (image authoritative on layout, analysis directional on priority/reasoning). Same page flagged before — see D-009 (original phase-gated build) and its 2026-06-12 amendment (pitch heat map reactivated, trajectory animation gated on unconfirmed `pfxX`/`pfxZ`/`breaks.*`). Per ROUTER.md's own rule, a request that reopens settled ground is tier-3 regardless of framing — this audit exists because two of D-009's findings (win probability, pitch trajectory data) needed to be re-checked against real live data before anyone designs or builds around them, not assumed still true two months later.

**Scope check:** D-009 (live game architecture), its 2026-06-12 amendment (heat map/trajectory gate), D-116 (Trophy Case engine), and the NFL win-probability precedent (D-080 to D-105 to D-106 — the exact "don't build on an unconfirmed field, re-verify against a real live game first" discipline this audit follows) all reviewed before touching data.

**Method:** live-verified against real in-progress MLB games this session (ten games live, incl. Rays-Orioles gamePk 824799, Nationals-Marlins). Neither this sandbox's shell nor the owner's device shell can reach `statsapi.mlb.com`/`espn.com` directly (both 403 at the egress proxy) — verification went through the WebFetch tool instead, which truncates large payloads before its summarization model sees them. Caught this concretely: a first pass against the full `feed/live` payload for 824799 falsely reported no `spinRate`/`breaks` (contradicting `liveGame.js`'s own 2026-08-20 live-confirmed comment); switching to the smaller `playByPlay` endpoint returned a complete, unbalanced-brace-free document with real quotable data. Anywhere a fetch came back truncated, that's stated explicitly below as unresolved, not reported as a negative finding.

**Findings, re-ranked by actual buildability (not the analysis's stated priority order):**

| # | Feature | Status | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Game hierarchy / hero restructure | No new data needed | Pure IA/visual against the reference image |
| 2 | Win probability (fetched) | **Confirmed still absent from MLB Stats API** | Full `playByPlay` for 824799 checked end-to-end, no `winProbability` field — D-009 item 7 reaffirmed, not stale |
| 2b | Win probability (ESPN MLB summary) | **Unresolved — tool truncation, not a negative finding** | Every fetch of ESPN's MLB `summary` endpoint truncated before reaching a conclusive answer. Recommend not pursuing this path regardless: a second live-data vendor for MLB is a vendor decision this project treats deliberately, and the option below doesn't need it |
| 3 | Win probability (computed in-house) | **Buildable now** | Same class as `js/odds.js`'s existing Monte Carlo odds — all inputs (inning/half, outs, base state, score) already flow through every poll. Needs a vetted empirical win-expectancy table sourced before Axiom codes anything — not fabricated in this entry |
| 4 | Pitch trajectory data (`pfxX`/`pfxZ`/`breaks.*`) | **Confirmed live — the 2026-06-12 D-009 gate is stale** | Real values on real pitches, 824799, this afternoon. `breakHorizontal` specifically not confirmed in the sample pulled — cheap follow-up check before Phase 3 starts |
| 5 | Batter vs. pitcher matchup | **Already shipped** | `liveGame.js` Matchup tab (`vsPlayer` H2H + arsenal) since D-009 Phase 2 — needs re-placement in the hero, not new data |
| 6 | hitData (exit velo/launch angle/distance/MLB's own `hardness` class) | **Confirmed live** | Present on a real flyout in 824799's play-by-play — feeds Game Pulse hard-hit detection and real HR/hard-hit play-by-play callouts without inventing our own threshold |
| 7 | Game Pulse (derived context) | **Half buildable now, half gated on #3** | hitData-streak half buildable today; win-probability-delta half needs #3 shipped first |
| 8 | Bullpen Availability panel (reference image) | **Already shipped, wrong page** | `_fetchBullpenRest()`/`_populateBullpenSection()` in `js/mlb.js` (Game Prep) already fetch real `numberOfPitches` per reliever over the last 3 games — reuse/integration task, not new data |
| 9 | AL-West mini standings / mini leaderboard / Due Up rail (reference image) | **Buildable now** | Standings + leaderboard are existing app-wide state; Due Up is an index lookup against `battingOrder` (already read in the existing box-score code) — no new fetch |
| 10 | "Where to Watch" broadcast chip (reference image) | Likely buildable, unverified | MLB Stats API schedule `broadcasts` hydrate is the standard path (same shape already proven for NFL/NCAAF from ESPN) but not confirmed populated for MLB in this codebase yet |
| 11 | Embedded batter/pitcher player card (season stats + today's line) | **Buildable now** | Season stats already fetched sitewide; today's line comes from box score data already parsed in `liveGame.js`. **Trophy Case link explicitly excluded from this feature per owner direction 2026-08-23** — do not add it |
| 12 | "Last 50 plate appearances" outcome-log bar (reference image) | **Unverified — not scheduled** | No endpoint checked for a clean per-PA outcome history this session. Do not let Kael/Vera design this widget until Relay confirms one exists — may require repeated game-log fetches, changing its cost |

**Decision — re-ranked phase order (supersedes the external analysis's stated priority order for this build):**

| Phase | Scope | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Command-center restructure: hierarchy/contrast pass against the reference image, batter/pitcher hero, Due Up rail, mini standings, mini leaderboard, broadcast chip. Zero new data. | Vera states spec (done, see ISSUES.md) → Kael visual → Axiom → live-verify against a real in-progress game |
| 2 | Bullpen Availability, integrated from Game Prep's existing `_fetchBullpenRest` | Phase 2 shipped + live-verified (commit 4a5d561, 2026-08-24) |
| 3 | Pitch trajectory animation (gate: confirm `breakHorizontal` first) + pitch-mix wheel | Phase 3 shipped + live-verified (commit 75596fe, 2026-08-24) |
| 4 | Game Pulse (hard-hit/streak half only) + rich play-by-play with real exit-velo/distance callouts | Phase 4 shipped + live-verified (commit aaedfb1, 2026-08-24) — real batted-ball event occurred during verification |
| 5 | Embedded player card (season stats + today's line only — **no Trophy Case link**) | No dependency on Phase 6; may ship earlier if reprioritized |
| 6 | In-house win-expectancy model — Relay sources and cites a real empirical WE table before Axiom writes the lookup; unblocks Game Pulse's WP-delta half | Phase 5 shipped + live-verify the computed curve's *shape* against a real game (~50/50 in a scoreless 1st, swings hard toward the leader late), not just that it renders |

**Not scheduled:** the "last 50 PA" outcome-log visualization (item 12) — needs its own Relay feasibility check first.

**Implications:**
- Kael/Vera/Axiom do not start Phase 1 work until Vera's states spec (this session, see ISSUES.md) is reviewed and gated per ROUTER.md.
- Each phase is live-verified against a real game before the next phase starts — same discipline as D-009's original phase gates and the NFL win-probability precedent (D-080/D-105/D-106).
- Phase 6 (win-expectancy model) is explicitly not to be built with invented/guessed numbers — the empirical table must be sourced and cited before implementation.
- No ESPN MLB proxy Function is being added as part of this build (item 2b) — single-vendor discipline for MLB data holds.

**Escalation:** none yet — audit only, no code shipped, no route or data contract changed.

### D-117 addendum — Relay's Phase 2 feasibility audit (Bullpen Availability), 2026-08-23

Phase 1 is shipped and live-verified (see above and ISSUES.md). Per D-117's own phase-gate rule, Phase 2 starts now. Re-auditing item 8 from the original table ("already shipped, wrong page") at the level of detail actual implementation needs, not just the earlier buildability check.

**What already exists, read in full before assuming its shape:** `js/mlb.js`'s `_fetchBullpenRest(teamId)` (~line 7056) and `_populateBullpenSection(...)` (~line 7104), shipped for the pregame **Game Prep** view. It pulls each team's last 3 *Final* games via `/schedule` + `/game/{pk}/boxscore`, keeps the most-recent appearance per pitcher where `gamesStarted === 0` (relievers only) and `inningsPitched > 0`, and renders a days-of-rest pill (hot/warm/ok/fresh, 4-color scale already in `css/components.css` ~3480-3498) per team. This is genuinely reusable as-is for the "who's rested" half of a live-game bullpen view — same fetch, same rest-day math, zero changes needed to `_fetchBullpenRest` itself.

**What it does NOT cover — the reference image's actual ask:** "per-pitcher pitch counts" for *today's* game specifically. `_fetchBullpenRest` only looks at *Final* games before today, so a reliever who has already thrown 20 pitches in the live game right now shows up nowhere in its output (today's game isn't Final yet). That data lives somewhere else entirely: `feed.liveData.boxscore.teams.{away,home}.players`, the exact same boxscore object `_renderPanel` already destructures every poll (used today for the Phase 1 hero's pitcher pitch count and for the Box Score tab). Any pitcher who has appeared in today's game has a `stats.pitching.numberOfPitches` entry there already, live, updated every poll, zero new fetch.

**Feasibility conclusion — two data sources, one view, no new endpoints:**
1. **Today's live bullpen usage (pitch counts):** `boxscore.teams[side].players`, filter `stats.pitching.gamesStarted === 0 && parseFloat(stats.pitching.inningsPitched || 0) + (stats.pitching.numberOfPitches || 0) > 0` (a reliever who has entered but not yet recorded an out still has a real pitch count worth showing). Already in `_renderPanel`'s existing `boxscore` local — zero new fetch, updates every poll like the rest of the panel.
2. **Rest status for relievers NOT yet used today:** reuse `_fetchBullpenRest(teamId)` verbatim (it's a global from `mlb.js`'s script-load order, reachable from `liveGame.js` exactly like `fetchMLBStandingsFull`/`_fetchMLBLeaderSplits` already are in Phase 1). Fetched once per game open, not per poll — same `_lgSidebarExtrasGamePk`-style gate Phase 1 already established, not a new pattern.

**Where it lives:** a fourth tab (`Bullpen`) alongside Play-by-Play / Box Score / Matchup, not a new sidebar card or a new full-width section. Reasoning: Phase 1 already added real vertical weight (hero + Due Up) above the tab row; a per-pitcher chart for two teams' bullpens is dense enough to want its own scroll space rather than compressing into a `.lg-side-card`, and the tab-panel mechanism (`_switchTab`, `_lgTabMap`, `data-lg-tab`) already exists and already handles exactly this shape of content (see `matchup`'s skeleton-then-async-patch pattern) — no new dispatch mechanism, same "cheapest path" discipline as Phase 1.

**Visual vocabulary:** reuse the shipped `.bullpen-pill`/`.bullpen-pill--hot/warm/ok/fresh`/`.bullpen-abbr`/`.bullpen-team-section` classes verbatim for the rest-day half (already color-discipline-correct — red-ish for recently used, green for fresh, matching DESIGN.md's category-not-importance rule since this is state, not brand). The pitch-count half needs one new element (a compact per-pitcher row: name + today's pitch count, sorted most-used-today first) — new markup, no new color tokens.

**Not building:** a numeric or visual "who should warm up next" recommendation — that's a judgment call belonging to a broadcast analyst, not this feature; the chart shows facts (rest days, today's pitch count), not advice.

**Sign-off:** feasible as scoped, zero new endpoints, reuses two existing functions and one existing dispatch mechanism verbatim. Ready for Vera/Kael/Axiom passes (see ISSUES.md).

### D-117 addendum — Relay's Phase 3 feasibility audit (pitch trajectory animation + pitch-mix wheel), 2026-08-24

Phase 2 is shipped and live-verified (see above and ISSUES.md), confirmed genuinely deployed via a fresh unpatched page load that also caught the game moving a new reliever from "rested" to "used today" in real time. Per D-117's phase-gate rule, Phase 3 starts now. This audit closes the one open item from the original table: item 4 flagged `breakHorizontal` as "not confirmed in the sample pulled — cheap follow-up check before Phase 3 starts."

**`breakHorizontal` gate — cleared.** Checked against a second, independent real in-progress game (ATL @ MIL, gamePk 823745, 2026-08-24) via the same live `feed/live` payload `liveGame.js` already polls (`_lgFeedCache.liveData.plays.allPlays[n].playEvents[n].pitchData`). Walked all 188 pitches thrown in the game so far, not just one sample: `breakHorizontal`, `breakVertical`, `breakVerticalInduced`, `breakAngle`, `spinRate`, and `spinDirection` were present on every single one (0 missing), alongside the full physics coordinate set (`pfxX`, `pfxZ`, `x0/y0/z0`, `vX0/vY0/vZ0`, `aX/aY/aZ`, `pX/pZ`, plate-crossing `x`/`y`) and pitch metadata (`startSpeed`, `endSpeed`, `zone`, `extension`, `plateTime`). The 2026-06-12 D-009 gate on trajectory data is now fully stale — not just "probably fine," walked end-to-end on a real game with zero gaps.

**What already exists — read before assuming a blank slate.** `liveGame.js` already ships a per-at-bat pitch-zone SVG (`_buildPitchZone`, `_lgZoneGeom`, `_lgSvgCoords`, `~line 688`) with a dots/heat toggle, built off the same confirmed `pX`/`pZ` plate-crossing coordinates, plus a density heat view (`_buildPitchHeat`) across a pitcher's whole game. Each dot already carries pitch type, velocity, and spin in its aria-label/data attributes. This is a real, already-verified rendering surface — Phase 3's "trajectory animation" is an enhancement to how a pitch's dot *arrives* in this existing plot, not a new coordinate system or a new SVG from scratch. Confirmed no existing pitch-mix wheel, donut chart, or trajectory-animation code anywhere in `liveGame.js`, `mlb.js`, or `liveGame.css` (grepped for `pitch-mix`/`mixWheel`/`donut`/`_buildTrajectory`/`animateP`/`ballTrail` — no hits) — the wheel is genuinely new work, not a rename of something shipped.

**Feasibility conclusion — two features, zero new fetches:**
1. **Trajectory animation:** the newest pitch's dot gets an entrance path instead of appearing instantly — an SVG `<animateMotion>` or CSS-driven path from a fixed mound-side origin into its already-computed `(pX, pZ)` plate coordinate, using `breakHorizontal`/`breakVerticalInduced` to bow the path (not a literal 3D parabola — a stylized 2D curve is enough to read as "this pitch broke," matching a broadcast graphic rather than a physics sim). All inputs already present in `_lgFeedCache` on every poll; no new endpoint, no new state beyond "is this dot new since the last render" (reuse the `_lgLastHeroBatterId`-style "what changed since last poll" pattern already established in Phase 1).
2. **Pitch-mix wheel:** a donut breakdown of `details.type.code`/`description` counts, scoped to the currently-selected pitcher (the hero's active pitcher, matching the Matchup tab's existing pitcher-scoping convention). Confirmed live: 6 distinct pitch types this game alone (CH/FC/FF/FS/SI/SL) with real counts, `details.type.description` already human-readable ("Sinker", etc.) with no lookup table to build. Data is a simple reduce over `allPlays` filtered by `matchup.pitcher.id`, the exact same filter `_collectPitcherGamePitches` already uses for the heat view — reuse that function's pitcher-scoping, don't reinvent it.

**Visual vocabulary:** the wheel needs one new SVG component (no existing donut/pie chart anywhere in the codebase to reuse) but should draw its per-type colors from the pitch dot categories already in `liveGame.css` where a mapping exists (ball/strike/in-play is outcome, not type, so this is a new small palette — pitch *type* color, not pitch *result* color; keep it desaturated/secondary per DESIGN.md's category-not-importance rule since this is a breakdown, not a headline stat). Trajectory animation reuses the existing `.lg-pitch-zone` SVG viewBox and coordinate system verbatim — no new geometry.

**Not building:** a literal 3D physics simulation of the pitch path (broadcast-style stylized curve only, per Kael's coming visual pass); auto-playing animation on every poll tick (only the newest pitch since last render animates, matching the hero's own entrance-motion discipline from Phase 1 — no motion on a same-state re-render).

**Sign-off:** feasible now, zero new fetches, `breakHorizontal` gate cleared against real data, reuses the existing zone-plot coordinate system and pitcher-scoping filter verbatim. Ready for Vera/Kael/Axiom passes (see ISSUES.md).

### D-117 addendum — Relay's Phase 4 feasibility audit (Game Pulse hard-hit/streak half + rich play-by-play), 2026-08-24

Phase 3 is shipped and live-verified (see above and ISSUES.md), confirmed genuinely deployed via a fresh unpatched page load. Per D-117's phase-gate rule, Phase 4 starts now. Per DECISIONS.md's phase table, this phase is scoped to the "hard-hit/streak half only" of the original audit's item 7 (Game Pulse) — the win-probability-delta half stays excluded, since it's gated on in-house win probability (item 3), which is not built yet and is not part of this phase.

**`hitData` re-confirmed live — independently, not re-cited from yesterday's audit.** The original audit (2026-08-23) checked `hitData` on gamePk 824799. This phase's own gate re-checks it against a *different*, currently in-progress game (ATL @ MIL, gamePk 823745, 2026-08-24) before building anything against it, per this project's "verify before building, don't assume yesterday's finding still holds" discipline. Walked all 39 plays with `hitData` out of 58 total plays in the game so far (not one sample): every one carries `launchSpeed` (exit velo, mph), `launchAngle` (degrees), `totalDistance` (feet), `trajectory` (confirmed values this game: `ground_ball`, `line_drive`, `popup`, `fly_ball`), `hardness` (confirmed values this game: `medium`, `hard` — `soft` not observed in this game's sample but is documented MLB Stats API vocabulary alongside the other two, flagging as expected-but-not-directly-observed rather than assumed), `location` (fielding position code), and `coordinates`. A real 102.3 mph line-drive single classified `hardness: "hard"` by the API itself confirms MLB's own hardness field is directly usable as the hard-hit signal — no custom velocity threshold needs inventing.

**What "streak" means — not specced before now, defined here.** The original finding's "hitData-streak half" bundled two buildable-without-new-fetches ideas under one verdict without detailing either. This addendum settles it: streak means the current pitcher's **consecutive-strikeout streak** — plate appearances against this pitcher, most recent backwards, that ended in a strikeout, breaking on any other outcome (walk, hit, HBP, sac fly, any non-strikeout out). Chosen over a batter on-base streak or a team scoring streak because it's the simplest unambiguous definition, matches a common broadcast callout ("has struck out the side" / "three in a row"), and — like the hard-hit half — needs zero new data: it's a reduce over `allPlays` filtered by `matchup.pitcher.id`, the same filter shape `_collectPitcherGamePitches`/`_collectPitcherPitchTypes` already use.

**Feasibility conclusion — two features, zero new fetches, both extend already-shipped rendering paths:**
1. **Rich play-by-play hard-hit callouts:** `_buildPbp(allPlays)` already iterates every play to build its HTML; a batted-ball play with `playEvents[].hitData.hardness === 'hard'` gets its description appended with the real exit velo/distance (e.g. "102.3 mph, 195 ft") and a new `.lg-pbp-entry--hardhit` modifier class, reusing the exact same emoji-prefix + colored-left-border idiom `.lg-pbp-entry--hr`/`.lg-pbp-entry--scoring` already established — not a new visual language. An HR is never double-flagged with the hard-hit treatment even when it also qualifies (HR's own `💥` already communicates the bigger fact; stacking a second badge on the same entry would compete with itself, not add information).
2. **Pitcher K-streak indicator:** appends to the hero's existing pitcher `.lg-hero-stat` line (already showing `"87 pitches · 95.1 mph"`) as a third segment when the streak is 2+, e.g. `"87 pitches · 95.1 mph · 3 K streak"` — no new DOM node, no new hero layout, same line Phase 1 already established for exactly this kind of small derived pitcher fact.

**Not building:** any win-probability-derived pulse (explicitly out of scope per the phase table, gated on unbuilt item 3); a "streak" for anything other than the current pitcher's strikeouts (batter streaks, team streaks — different data shape, different design conversation, not needed to satisfy this phase's scope); a numeric/visual "excitement meter" combining multiple signals into one derived score — that's a judgment call this project isn't making, same reasoning as Phase 2's "not building a recommendation" boundary.

**Sign-off:** feasible now, zero new fetches, `hitData` gate independently re-confirmed against a second real game with zero gaps. Ready for Vera/Kael/Axiom passes (see ISSUES.md).

### D-117 addendum — Relay's Phase 5 reuse-point re-check (embedded player card: season stats + today's line), 2026-08-24

Phase 4 is shipped and live-verified (see above and ISSUES.md), confirmed genuinely deployed via a fresh unpatched page load on gamePk 823745 (hard-hit callouts present, pitch mix wheel present, hero showing the new pitcher Bryse Wilson with no stale K-streak carried over). Per D-117's phase-gate rule, Phase 5 starts now.

**Hard constraint restated (not assumed carried):** the embedded player card shows season stats + today's line **only**. **Trophy Case link explicitly excluded from this feature per owner direction 2026-08-23 — do not add it.** Grepped the repo for the Trophy Case feature (`css/components.css`, `js/achievements.js`/`mlb.js`/`nfl.js`/`nflStandings.js`, D-116) to confirm it is a fully separate cross-sport achievements surface with no shared code path into `liveGame.js` — there is nothing to accidentally pull in by reuse, but the exclusion is a product-scope decision, not a code-availability one, and is restated here per this phase's own audit rather than assumed remembered from three phases ago.

**The original audit's reuse claim needed correcting, not just confirming — this is the actual finding of this re-check.** The original audit (2026-08-23) said "season stats fetched sitewide, today's line from box score already parsed," pointing implicitly at Phase 1's `_lgFetchBatterSeasonLine` (a separate `/people/{id}/stats?group=hitting` call, cached per-batter in `_lgSeasonStatCache`, built only for the hero's current batter) as the season-stats reuse point. Re-verified live against gamePk 823745 (ATL @ MIL, Live, 8th inning) before writing anything, and that assumption does not hold:

- **The box score already carries full season stats for every rostered player, with zero additional fetch.** Each boxscore player entry (`liveData.boxscore.teams.{away|home}.players["ID{id}"]`) has both a `stats` object (today's game line) *and* a separate `seasonStats` object (year-to-date line), confirmed present on the current batter (`seasonStats.batting`: `avg: ".143"`, `obp: ".143"`, `slg: ".167"`, `ops: ".310"`, plus `homeRuns`, `rbi`, etc.) and the current pitcher (`seasonStats.pitching`: `era: "4.25"`, `whip: "1.35"`, `wins`, `losses`, `strikeOuts: 26`, `inningsPitched: "29.2"`). This data is already in `_lgFeedCache` on every poll — the embedded card needs no new network request for *any* player's season line, not just the batter currently at the plate.
