This is strictly better than the original plan: `_lgFetchBatterSeasonLine`'s per-player fetch-and-cache machinery is unnecessary for this feature and should not be extended to cover pitchers/due-up batters/PBP participants — the box score already has all of it, for everyone, for free.
- **The today's-line key name in the original audit's phrasing is wrong and would have caused a silent bug if copied by analogy.** "Today's line from box score" is directionally correct, but the box score's own per-player stat-category keys are `stats.batting` / `stats.pitching` / `stats.fielding` — **not** `stats.hitting`. `.hitting` is only the `group` query-param value used by the unrelated `/people/{id}/stats` endpoint (Phase 1's separate fetch, a different namespace). Confirmed live: querying `bp.stats.hitting` on the current batter returns `undefined`; the real data lives at `bp.stats.batting` (confirmed keys include `summary: "1-4 | K, RBI"`, `hits`, `atBats`, `doubles`, `triples`, `homeRuns`, `strikeOuts`, `baseOnBalls`, `rbi`, `totalBases`, `plateAppearances`). This is exactly the "never assume a data shape by analogy" trap this project's discipline exists to catch — caught here before any code was written against it, not after. `stats.pitching` for today's line is already correctly used elsewhere in this file (Phase 1's `pStats.numberOfPitches`, Phase 4's K-streak filter), so no correction needed on the pitching side.

**No existing modal/popover component to reuse.** Grepped `css/components.css` and `js/app.js` for a sitewide modal/card-overlay pattern — none exists; players are shown inline elsewhere in the site (`js/playerDetail.js`, a separate full-page navigation target, not an embeddable overlay, and built around basketball-specific fields like per-36 and shooting charts that don't apply to MLB). The closest reusable *interaction* shape already in this file is the pitch-zone tooltip (`_lgShowTooltip`/`_lgHideTooltip`, anchored-near-click, single-instance, dismiss-on-outside-click/Escape) — reuse that interaction pattern rather than inventing a new overlay system. The closest reusable *visual* shell is `.lg-side-card` (declared in Phase 1's CSS as the deliberate shell language for card-shaped content on this page). Recommend Kael's visual pass build the card as a tooltip-pattern overlay dressed in the side-card shell, not a new full-screen modal.

**Feasibility conclusion:** buildable now, zero new fetches (an improvement on the original audit, which implied at least one), sourced entirely from `_lgFeedCache.liveData.boxscore.teams.{side}.players["ID{id}"]` — `stats.batting`/`stats.pitching` for today's line, `seasonStats.batting`/`seasonStats.pitching` for the season line — for any player already present in the box score (batters, pitchers, due-up hitters, PBP participants alike). No Trophy Case link, per standing owner direction.

**Sign-off:** feasible now, zero new fetches, reuse points independently re-verified against live data and corrected where the original audit's shorthand would have misled implementation. Ready for Vera/Kael/Axiom passes (see ISSUES.md).

### D-117 addendum — js/css edge-cache gap found during Phase 5 live-verification (2026-08-24)

Attempting to re-verify Phase 5 (embedded player card) on a genuinely fresh production tab, clicking a player name didn't open the card even though the deployed `js/liveGame.js` demonstrably had the new functions and correct `data-player-id`/`side`/`role` attributes on the trigger buttons (confirmed directly). Root cause: `css/liveGame.css` was still serving pre-Phase-5 content from a stale Cloudflare edge cache entry, so `.lg-player-name-trigger`/`.lg-player-card` never applied — the buttons rendered as unstyled default `<button>` elements (confirmed via a zoomed screenshot) rather than the intended plain-text triggers, and any card that did get created by a real click would have inserted unstyled/unposition ed rather than visibly anchoring near the name.

This is the exact same failure mode D-116 already root-caused for `/data/*.json` and fixed for `/sw.js`: `js/*.js` and `css/*.css` had no dedicated `_headers` cache rule, so both ride Cloudflare's default long per-file-type edge TTL, and a deploy can leave different edge nodes serving different (old vs. new) copies of the same push for a while — explaining why JS and CSS looked freshly-deployed and stale, respectively, from the same tab at the same moment. Not a Phase 5 code defect — the committed code (`688a3dc`) is correct, this is a delivery/caching gap that predates D-117 and just happened to be caught by it.

**Fix:** added `/js/*` and `/css/*` rules to `_headers`, matching the `/data/*` precedent (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, must-revalidate`) — short edge TTL so a future push doesn't need a manual Cloudflare purge to reach every user within a reasonable window. Owner approved this as a standalone infra fix, separate from D-117's own scope.

**Not yet re-verified live:** this session hit a browser-automation rate limit immediately after finding this, so Phase 5's actual on-screen behavior post-fix has not been re-confirmed yet, and Phase 6 has not started. Both are next once the rate limit clears.

**Re-verified post-fix, 2026-08-24 (commit fa2e0fe live).** No MLB game is currently in progress (just past midnight, today's slate is all Preview) — the ATL @ MIL game used throughout D-117's verification has since gone Final. Rather than wait, used the same secondary-verification technique established in Phase 3/4 (synthetic data over real values, clearly distinguished from a genuinely-observed live event): fetched the real final feed for gamePk 823745 and force-rendered it through the actual shipped `_buildHero`/`_buildDueUp`/`_buildPlayerCard` functions with `abstractGameState` overridden to `'Live'` for rendering purposes only, on a fresh, completely unpatched production tab.

Confirmed the `_headers` fix actually closed the gap: `.lg-player-name-trigger` now computes `text-decoration-line: underline` and `.lg-panel` computes `position: relative` (both from the deployed CSS, not browser defaults) — matching what failed before the fix. A real click on the pitcher's name (dispatched via the trigger element itself, not screen coordinates — the `computer` tool's screenshot/click coordinate spaces turned out to be scaled differently from the actual page viewport in this session, which is what made the very first post-fix attempt look like a renewed failure until `getBoundingClientRect()` exposed the mismatch) opened a correctly styled, correctly positioned card via the real shipped listener end to end, showing real season/today data pulled from the boxscore. All four dismissal paths (toggle same trigger, Escape, click outside, a second trigger) worked. This closes out the open item from the previous addendum — Phase 5 is confirmed working in production, not just correct in the committed source.

**Gate status: Phase 5 shipped, live-verified, and re-verified post-cache-fix.** Phase 6 (in-house win-expectancy model) can proceed — its Relay data-sourcing step doesn't require a live game, but its own live-verification step will need one, same as every prior phase.

### D-117 addendum — Relay's Phase 6 data-sourcing audit (in-house win-expectancy model), 2026-08-24

Per the phase table, Phase 6 is gated on Relay sourcing/citing a real empirical win-expectancy table before Axiom codes anything — must not be built with invented/guessed numbers. This addendum is that sourcing.

**Methodology source (why this shape of table is the right one, not an invention):** Baseball-Reference's own published WE/WPA methodology (`baseball-reference.com/about/wpa.shtml`, credited to Tom Tango's charts) keys win expectancy on four dimensions — inning and half ("extras are just repeats of the ninth inning" is BR's own documented simplification for capping table size), all 24 base-out states, score differential (BR caps at ±11, "any lead of more than 11 is treated as 11"), and run environment. FanGraphs' Sabermetrics Library page on Win Expectancy (`library.fangraphs.com/misc/we/`) independently corroborates the same Tango-derived methodology: "identify all similar situations in the last ten years or so... and then find the winning percentage of teams who found themselves in those situations" — i.e. win expectancy is an empirical frequency, not a formula, which settles the "in-house model" framing: this has to be a real lookup table built from real games, not a curve someone made up.

**Concrete data source (the actual numbers, not just the methodology):** `github.com/gregstoll/baseballstats`, an open-source Retrosheet-derived win-expectancy calculator (the same family of tool as the live "Win Expectancy Finder" at `gregstoll.com/~gregstoll/baseball/stats.html`, built on "Phil Birnbaum's description of the data file" — Birnbaum being a recognized SABR statistical-methods author). Its `probs.txt` output (fetched directly, 308,867 bytes, 16,112 rows) is a real per-state tally: `"H"|"V", inning, outs, baseState, scoreDiff, totalCount, homeWinCount`, derived from real Retrosheet play-by-play games. Verified the schema empirically rather than assuming it from the filename:
- `"H"`/`"V"` = bottom-half (home batting) / top-half (visitor batting) — confirmed because `"H"` rows never carry a positive `scoreDiff` (home team already leading skips the bottom half entirely, exactly matching real baseball rules), while `"V"` rows range the full ±27.
- `baseState` 1-8 = standard 3-bit runner encoding + 1 (`bit0`=1B, `bit1`=2B, `bit2`=3B occupied), confirmed by count ordering at inning 1/0 outs: state 1 (empty) = 401,342 occurrences, state 2 (1B only) = 114,910, descending exactly as expected down to the rarer multi-runner states — not assumed from the number alone.
- Win probability for the home team at any state = `homeWinCount / totalCount`. Spot-checked for sanity, not just schema: bottom 9th/2 outs/bases empty/tied = 5,236 of 9,460 = 55.3% (home batting last, home-field edge); the same state trailing by 1 = 387 of 10,600 = 3.65% (needing a real rally with a man due up); top 9th/2 outs/bases empty/home leading by 1 = 9,282 of 11,151 = 83.2% (still not certain — the visitor still has that at-bat). All three numbers land exactly where baseball intuition says they should, which is what "real data" looks like as opposed to a guessed curve.
- Extra innings are real per-inning data up to inning 25 in this file (not simplified to "repeats of the 9th" the way BR's own public methodology says it does at their scale) — better granularity than the methodology source strictly requires, though the tail is thin: 4,002 of 16,112 rows (~25%) have fewer than 5 sample occurrences, concentrated at the extreme score-differential ends. Flagging this now as an implementation concern for Axiom's pass, not resolving it here — some capping/smoothing at the sparse tails (mirroring BR's own ±11 cap, which is a defensible, citable precedent) will be needed so a rare state doesn't render a win probability computed from a sample of 1.

**Feasibility — every input this table is keyed on is already in hand, most already parsed elsewhere in this file:** inning (`linescore.currentInning`, used since Phase 1), half (`linescore.isTopInning`, used since Phase 1), outs (`linescore.outs`, used since Phase 1), score differential (`homeScore - awayScore`, already computed in `_renderPanel`), and base state — the exact same 3-bit encoding this table uses can be derived from `currentPlay.runners[].movement.end` in `{'1B','2B','3B'}`, the identical field Phase 2's `_buildBaseDiagram` already reads to draw the diamond diagram. No new fetch, no new field to verify live — every dimension is a reuse point already proven live across Phases 1-2.

**Not yet done (Axiom's job, not Relay's):** deciding the final table size/pruning strategy (full 16k rows vs. a capped-and-smoothed subset sized for a static site), and how to encode/ship it (a JS object literal, a compact array, etc.). This addendum only establishes that real data exists, is accessible, and every input it needs is already available — it does not commit to an implementation shape.

**Sign-off:** a real, cited, spot-checked empirical win-expectancy table exists and is fetchable; every state variable it's keyed on is already available live. Ready for Vera/Kael/Axiom passes (see ISSUES.md).

### D-117 addendum — Phase 6 (in-house win-expectancy model) shipped + synthetically verified, 2026-08-24

**What shipped:** `js/liveGame.js` gained `_lgOccupiedBases` (extracted from `_buildBaseDiagram`, now the single source of truth both the base diagram and the win-expectancy lookup share — Axiom's reuse-over-reinvent requirement from the spec), the 6,551-row `LG_WE_TABLE`/`LG_WE_MAP` lookup table (real Retrosheet-derived data, sourcing and spot-checks documented in the Relay addendum above), `_lgBaseStateIndex`, `_lgWinProbability`, and `_buildWinProb`. `css/liveGame.css` gained `.lg-winprob`/`.lg-winprob-seg`/`.lg-winprob-label` (team-color gradient + white text, same recipe as the hero avatar, per Kael's spec). Wired into `_buildSkeletonPanel`'s template (`.lg-winprob-host`, between the linescore and the poll-timestamp/hero) and `_renderPanel` (rendered fresh every poll, immediately after the linescore update). All four JS patches applied via a `replace_once`-guarded script (no manual edits), CSS inserted the same way. Verified after: `node --check` passes, 0 NUL bytes in both files, brace count balanced in the CSS (191/191), `LG_WE_TABLE` parses to exactly 6,551 rows with first/last row byte-identical to the source table (`[0,1,0,0,0,460]` / `[1,9,2,7,0,682]`).

**Verification technique — synthetic-but-real-data (no live game available):** as anticipated in the Phase 6 spec's gate-status line, no MLB game was in progress at implementation time (2026-08-24, ~01:15 CDT — today's slate still entirely `Preview`/`Scheduled`, checked directly against `statsapi.mlb.com`'s schedule endpoint via a live tab's own `fetch`, not the sandbox's, since the sandbox's egress allowlist blocks that host directly). Per the same synthetic-but-real-data technique used for Phase 5's re-verification, the actual shipped function bodies (`_lgOccupiedBases`, `_lgBaseStateIndex`, `_lgWinProbability`, `_buildWinProb`, copy-pasted verbatim from the on-disk file, not retyped/paraphrased) were injected into a live production tab (the site's already-open ATL@MIL box score, a real completed game) alongside a small **real** subset of `LG_WE_TABLE` (11 of the actual 6,551 rows, pulled directly from the shipped table via `node`, not fabricated) — kept small only to bound the injection payload, not to avoid using genuine data. Four cases were run against real/realistic feed shapes with `abstractGameState` forced to `'Live'` for rendering purposes only (the game is actually Final):

1. Exact-match lookup (bottom 9th, 2 outs, bases empty, home down 2 — the real row `[1,9,2,0,-2,13]`): returned `0.013`, exact match.
2. Fallback-chain lookup (same state, home up 2 — no real row exists for a positive run-differential in this exact bases-empty/2-out/bottom-9 combination, which is itself a genuine data characteristic: a home team already leading in that state has almost always already won and left the sample space, so those cells are naturally sparse-to-absent in the source data): correctly walked `diff` from `+2` down through `+1` to the real `diff=0` row (`556`), returning `0.556`.
3. Not-`Live` guard: `abstractGameState: 'Final'` correctly returned `null` from `_lgWinProbability` and `''` from `_buildWinProb` (an idle/completed game renders no bar, not a stale one).
4. Mid-game state (top 5th, 1 out, bases loaded, tied — the real row `[0,5,1,7,0,611]`): returned `0.611`, exact match.

The rendered HTML from case 1 was injected into the live tab's real DOM (id `d117-p6-test-host`, `position:fixed`, removed afterward) alongside the actual shipped CSS block, and a real `getComputedStyle` check confirmed: away segment width `99%` / home segment width `1%` (matching `homeProb=0.013` rounded), away segment background `linear-gradient(135deg, rgba(206,17,65,0.8), rgba(206,17,65,0.333))` (Atlanta's real primary color, `getMLBTeamColors('ATL').primary = #CE1141`, at the spec'd `cc`/`55` alpha stops), label computed `color: rgb(255,255,255)`, `font-weight: 800`, and `transition-property: width` all present as shipped. A zoomed screenshot of the live tab visually confirms a legible "ATL 99%" red-gradient bar rendered in the real page — not a mockup. Test DOM/style elements were removed from the tab immediately after.

**What this does and doesn't establish:** this confirms the lookup math (exact-match and fallback-chain), the not-Live guard, the HTML/CSS rendering pipeline, and the render-cycle wiring all work correctly against real function code and real (if partial) data, in a real browser DOM. It does not re-verify the full 6,551-row table's values against Retrosheet (already done in the Relay sourcing addendum's spot-checks) or observe the feature against a truly live, in-progress broadcast feed — that remains open, same as Phase 5's own first verification pass was until a live game became available. No regression risk: nothing here modifies data paths any other feature reads from.

**Gate status: Phase 6 shipped and synthetically verified. Live re-verification against a genuinely in-progress game (Phase 5's own precedent) should happen opportunistically next time one is running, but is not a blocker — the phase ships now.**

### D-117 addendum — Phase 6 pushed to production; post-push verification found a NEW cache-layer bug, distinct from the D-116/Phase-5 one, 2026-08-24

**Push confirmed:** `git rev-parse HEAD` on the working repo and `origin/main` both resolve to `3e1f86c` (the Phase 6 docs commit) — the push landed.

**Production content check (passed):** fetched `/js/liveGame.js` and `/css/liveGame.css` from `https://sportstrata.cc` with a cache-busting query string (forces a real origin fetch, `cf-cache-status: MISS`) and confirmed byte-for-byte the deployed files carry Phase 6: `LG_WE_TABLE` present with all 6,551 rows, `_buildWinProb`/`_lgOccupiedBases` present, `.lg-winprob`/`.lg-winprob-label` present in the CSS. The code that shipped is the code that was verified pre-push.

**New finding — a real visitor hitting the canonical URL right now can still get the pre-Phase-6 file.** Fetching the exact same two URLs *without* a cache-buster returned `cf-cache-status: HIT` with `age: ~77-156s` and the **old** content (87,669 bytes / no `LG_WE_TABLE` for the JS; 26,285 bytes / no `.lg-winprob` for the CSS) — a stale Cloudflare edge cache entry, still being served from the POP this session's requests are routed through (`cf-ray` region tag `MCI`, Kansas City). A spot-check of five other static files on the same push (`js/mlb.js`, `css/components.css`, `sw.js`) all came back `cf-cache-status: REVALIDATED` (Cloudflare checked with origin, content matched, no problem) — so this isn't every file, it's specifically the two files this push actually changed, at this one edge node, in the window right after deploy.

**Root cause is different from the D-116 / Phase-5 fix, and that fix did not actually take effect the way it was assumed to.** Every path checked — including files this push never touched — comes back `Cache-Control: public, max-age=14400, must-revalidate` (`sw.js`: `max-age=14400` with no `public`/`must-revalidate`, its own distinct rule). `_headers` on disk (committed, `fa2e0fe`) says `max-age=300` for `/js/*`, `/css/*`, and `/data/*`. **Production has never actually been serving `max-age=300` for these paths — it's been serving `max-age=14400` (4 hours) the whole time since that fix, on every file, not just this push's two.** That means some other Cloudflare-side mechanism outside this repo — almost certainly a zone-level Cache Rule or Page Rule (Cloudflare dashboard → Caching, or Rules → Cache Rules) setting a 4-hour Edge/Browser Cache TTL for static assets — is overriding `_headers`' declared value, and unlike a Cloudflare Pages deployment's own asset cache, a zone-level Cache Rule's cached objects are not automatically purged by a new Pages deployment. The `_headers` fix (`fa2e0fe`) was still correct and necessary (it's *part* of the caching chain, and did fix the specific same-request js/css inconsistency documented in that entry), but it does not control this second, larger-TTL layer, which is what's actually gating how fast a real edge node picks up a new deploy.

**Status: open, not fixed — outside this session's access.** Fixing this requires the Cloudflare dashboard (a zone-level Cache Rule/Page Rule and/or manually purging cache for `sportstrata.cc`), which isn't something this session can reach or should guess at changing unilaterally. Until either that rule is adjusted or the cache is purged, a real visitor's request may get up to ~4 hours of staleness on a changed `/js/*` or `/css/*` file after a deploy, at whichever edge node already had the old file cached — exactly the same *symptom* the D-116/Phase-5 entries describe (fresh JS + stale CSS, or vice versa, from the same tab), but this time the browser-testing technique this project has relied on for live-verification (a single fresh tab, cache-busted or not) can mask it: a cache-busted fetch, or any request that happens to land on a POP that already expired/missed, will show "everything's fine" even while other POPs are still stale. **Recommend:** owner checks the Cloudflare dashboard for a Cache Rule/Page Rule overriding static-asset TTL to 4 hours, and purges cache (or at minimum purges `/js/liveGame.js` and `/css/liveGame.css`) to get this push live everywhere immediately; going forward, treat "live-verified against production" claims in this file as verified-against-whichever-edge-node-answered, not verified-everywhere, until this is resolved.

### D-117 addendum — post-ship debug pass finds the panel was frozen mid-play for most of a half-inning, root cause predates D-117 but all six phases depend on it, 2026-08-30

**Requested:** a full debug/error-handling pass of the live game viewer, since the six D-117 phases shipped without a dedicated bug-hunt session. Reviewed `js/liveGame.js` end to end (owner's own automated multi-agent code-review was rate-limited mid-run — 7 of 8 finder agents failed on a session limit; this pass is a manual read plus live verification, not that tool's output).

**Root-cause finding — `_doPoll`'s state-change gate never included outs, balls, strikes, or the current batter.** The file's original architecture (predates D-117 by many commits — confirmed via `git log -S`) polls the lightweight `/linescore` endpoint every 9s and only fetches the full `feed/live` payload (which every D-117 widget reads: hero, Due Up, win probability, hard-hit callouts, K-streak, pitch-mix wheel, PBP, box score) when a `stateKey` built from `currentInning|inningState|awayRuns|homeRuns` changes. Strikeouts, walks, and groundouts don't move any of those four fields, so a full half-inning of real plate appearances could pass with the panel completely inert. **Live-verified, not theoretical:** polled the real live CIN@CHC game (gamePk 824636) 7 times over ~100 seconds on 2026-08-30 — the old key (`3|Top|0|2`) never changed once across that span, while `/linescore`'s own payload already carries `outs`, `balls`, `strikes`, and `offense.batter.id`, all of which did move. This also explains why Phase 3's per-pitch trajectory-entrance animation could only ever fire in batches (all of an at-bat's pitches appearing at once, on the next score/inning change) instead of one pitch at a time as designed.

**Fix:** `stateKey` now includes `outs`, `balls`, `strikes`, and `offense.batter.id` alongside the original four fields. Re-verified live: rendered the same game (now CHC 2–CIN 0, top 3rd) through a real browser against the fix and confirmed hero, Due Up, win probability (CIN 32%/CHC 88%), pitch zone + mix wheel, hard-hit callout on a Conforto single (106.3 mph, 260 ft), and the sidebar's mini standings/leaders all populated correctly. **Trade-off, flagged not hidden:** this makes `feed/live` refetch on nearly every pitch during live action instead of only on scores/innings — a real increase in upstream request volume for however this endpoint is proxied/cached. Given the alternative was "the live game viewer doesn't show live data most of the time," this is judged the right default; dial back to omitting `balls`/`strikes` (keep only `outs`+batter, still fixes the main staleness bug, loses true pitch-by-pitch dot timing) if request volume becomes a real concern.

**Two more bugs found and fixed in the same pass:**
1. `openLiveGamePanel` (the inline accordion opened from the Scores grid) still armed its *first* poll interval from `_pollInterval(game)` — the stale AppState game stub — while `showMLBLiveGame` (page mode) had already been fixed to use `_lgNextInterval(feed)` (the post-fetch, authoritative interval). The comment documenting that fix only mentioned `showMLBLiveGame`; the inline entry point was missed. Both entry points now use `_lgNextInterval`; the now-dead `_pollInterval` was removed.
2. `stopLiveGamePolling()` reset every other per-game module cache (`_lgMiniStandingsHtml`, `_lgBullpenRestHtml`, etc.) but not `_lgZoneMode`/`_lgZoneLastPitchCount` (both `Map`s) — a session-lifetime memory leak across every live game opened. Both are now `.clear()`'d on close.

**Not fixed, flagged for a future pass:** `js/liveGame.js` is ~2,000 lines with several duplicated markup templates (hero's pregame vs. live sides, win-prob's away/home segments) that a partial automated review (the one finder-agent that completed before the rate limit) flagged as simplification debt, not correctness bugs — see that pass's own findings if picked back up.

## D-118 — NFL Live Game Viewer immersion: sticky header + big-play Highlight Card auto-suggest, all three gates cleared

**Status:** spec + design + feasibility complete (Vera, Kael, Axiom all weighed in) — NOT built, NOT scheduled. Full behavioral spec, visual pass, and feasibility notes live in ISSUES.md ("NFL Live Game Viewer — immersion brainstorm, two ideas taken to Vera"); this entry records the cross-domain consensus per ROUTER.md's tier-3 rule (touches shared state/architecture, spans three domains).
**Contributors:** Vera (behavioral spec), Kael (visual pass), Axiom (feasibility check)
**Date opened:** 2026-08-22 (Vera's spec) — closed to consensus 2026-08-24 (Kael + Axiom)

**Trigger:** owner asked to brainstorm ways to make the NFL live game viewer feel more immersive. Two ideas survived Vera's first gate (full job-to-be-done writeups in ISSUES.md): a sticky score/clock/down-distance header, and a rule-based (no-EPA) big-play Highlight Card auto-suggest prompt.

**Decision:** both ideas are architecturally sound and buildable as specced, with two corrections found during Kael/Axiom's pass (documented in full in ISSUES.md, summarized here):
1. Idea 1's sticky rule can't apply to `.nlg-header` as literally proposed — the field position viewer is currently a DOM sibling inside that same element (`_nlgRenderHeader`, `js/nflLiveGame.js`) and would incorrectly stick along with the score row, contradicting the spec's own requirement. Needs a new wrapper (`.nlg-header-pin`) around just the score + situation rows. Small template change, not a scope change.
2. Idea 2's prompt should reuse the site's existing `ErrorHandler.toast()` system (`js/errorHandler.js`) rather than a bespoke element, extended with one new action-button variant. Everything else in both specs — the play-detection rule, the player-reference handoff, the sticky-positioning math — reuses already-shipped code paths with zero new fetches and zero new ID schemes.

**Rationale:** both ideas are additive, low-risk, and reuse existing patterns almost entirely — sticky-positioning math already proven by `.nlg-side`, toast chrome already proven by the error/warn/success/info system, state-diffing already proven by tonight's own `lastPlayArrowId`/`lastTimeouts` work (the possession-dot-pulse/timeout-flash polish shipped earlier this session). The kind of small-blast-radius feature this file's own "no one expands scope without documenting it" rule is comfortable clearing quickly once properly specced, rather than something that needed a long design cycle.

**Implications:**
- Neither idea is scheduled. Per this file's own D-043 precedent, a gate-cleared spec waits for owner ratification before anyone picks it up to build — this entry is the record that all three gates are now satisfied, not a green light.
- If both ship in the same pass, build Idea 1 first — Idea 2's prompt placement explicitly anchors relative to whether Idea 1's sticky header exists.

## D-119 — NFL Field Position Viewer Phases 1-4: shipped, plus a live-verification methodology gap worth keeping

**Status:** shipped, committed, live-verified in production (see ISSUES.md for the full phase-by-phase writeup). Closing this out as its own decisions entry because two real judgment calls happened along the way that are worth a permanent record, not just the ISSUES.md shipped-summary.
**Contributors:** Axiom
**Date:** 2026-08-22 to 2026-08-24

**Decision 1 — 3D perspective values, tuned live rather than picked from a formula.** Phase 1 needed real depth on the field (`perspective` + `rotateX`) without either looking flat (no perceptible tilt) or making end-zone vertical text illegible. Tested live against a real game at `perspective:900px`/`rotateX(55deg)` (negligible distortion) and `perspective:250px` (strong but illegible), before settling on `perspective:450px`/`rotateX(22deg)` desktop and `350px`/`18deg` mobile as the balance point. Recorded here because "why these exact numbers" has no other home — they're not derivable from a token or a rule, they're a visual-tuning outcome, and the next person changing this CSS should know it was chosen this way, not guessed once and left alone.

**Decision 2 — verification-methodology gap: SW clearing alone is not proof of a live deploy on this site.** Mid-Phase-3, a real transition (a team's timeout count dropping between renders) failed to trigger its flash animation, despite the deployed source being confirmed byte-exact-correct via a direct `fetch(url, {cache:'no-store'})`. Root cause: the page's own `<script src>` tag load — a normal navigation, not an explicit no-store fetch — was still being served from the browser's native HTTP disk cache (`Cache-Control: max-age=14400`), a layer completely separate from and untouched by `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations()` unregister + `caches.delete()`. A hard reload (Ctrl+Shift+R) resolved it immediately. `fa2e0fe` (owner's own fix that same session, found independently while re-verifying D-117 Phase 5) now gives `/js/*`/`/css/*` a short edge-cache TTL, closing the gap for real users going forward — but this file's own live-verification protocol (established over many earlier D-numbers) should be read as: SW clear + normal navigate proves the *deployed source* is correct; a hard reload is the extra step needed to prove the *executing page* is running it. Worth remembering the next time a live-verified function "should" work and doesn't.

**Implications:** no code change from this entry — it's a record of two decisions (one visual, one methodological) that would otherwise only exist in session scrollback. Future sessions tuning `.fv-field`'s perspective or debugging a live-verification mismatch on this site should read this first.

## D-120 — SEO audit: sitemap.xml found stale for weeks, now auto-refreshed daily

**Status:** shipped and committed.
**Contributors:** Axiom (audit + fix)
**Date:** 2026-08-31

**Trigger:** owner asked for a full SEO audit, starting from the already-known NCAAB/WNBA edge-render gap (D-102). Verifying `sitemap.xml` directly against `tools/gen-sitemap.cjs` (rather than trusting CLAUDE.md, which had already drifted once before) surfaced a second, unrelated problem: the committed `sitemap.xml` was generated 2026-08-02 and never regenerated since. It predated the same day's D-057 commit (`f3cacb6`) that added `nfl/leaders`, `ncaaf/standings`, `ncaaf/rankings`, and `ncaaf/player` to the generator — so those four sections had zero sitemap presence for four weeks despite their edge-render templates already being live. The rolling `mlb/game`/`nfl/game` discovery windows had also fully aged out (all entries were from a 14-day window ending 2026-08-02, i.e. already stale relative to any date after ~2026-08-16).

**Decision:** two-part fix, not just a one-time regen (a manual fix would just go stale again the same way):
1. Ran `node tools/gen-sitemap.cjs` once to restore the missing sections immediately (1666 URLs vs. 1553 before — confirmed `nfl/leaders`, `ncaaf/standings`, `ncaaf/rankings`, `ncaaf/player`, and a live `nfl/game` rolling window are all populated in the regenerated file).
2. Added `.github/workflows/sitemap-refresh.yml` — a daily cron (`workflow_dispatch` also available) that runs the generator and auto-commits `sitemap.xml` straight to `main` if it changed. Owner's explicit call to auto-commit rather than open a PR per run or run CI-check-only, since the output is fully deterministic script output from code already reviewed once, not hand-authored content needing per-run review.

**A safety floor was added specifically because of the auto-commit choice.** `gen-sitemap.cjs` deliberately fails open per-section — each upstream fetch is wrapped in its own try/catch that logs and skips rather than throwing, which is the right call for a one-off manual run (partial data beats none). But paired with an unattended cron that pushes straight to `main`, that same design means a transient ESPN outage during a scheduled run would have silently *shrunk* the live sitemap instead of failing anything visibly. The workflow now checks the generated URL count against a hard floor (800 — comfortably below a healthy run's ~1600-1700, comfortably above what's left if ESPN is fully unreachable and only MLB + static pages survive) and fails the job without committing if the count comes in under it.

**Implications:** `sitemap.xml` regeneration is no longer "owner/CI-run" in practice (that framing in CLAUDE.md's SEO section was itself the root cause — it means "runs when someone remembers"); it's automated. CLAUDE.md's Path URLs & Edge Rendering section updated in the same pass to describe the workflow and the floor, per this file's own doc-sync rule. This does not touch the separate, larger NCAAB/WNBA edge-render gap (D-102) — that's still open and unscoped; this entry only covers the sitemap-freshness half of the audit.

## D-121 — NCAAB/WNBA edge-render gap (D-102) closed: landing + standings for both, leaders + player for WNBA

**Status:** shipped, syntax-checked, and smoke-tested locally (`wrangler pages dev` against live upstreams). Not yet deployed/live-verified in production.
**Contributors:** Axiom
**Date:** 2026-08-31

**Trigger:** the second half of the same SEO audit that produced D-120. D-102 (2026-08-15) had already found that NCAAB and WNBA — both live surfaces since D-052/D-092 — had zero crawlable pages, and framed it as "a direct clone of the already-proven `functions/ncaaf/*` pattern — no architectural gate, just not yet built." That framing turned out to be half right: the *landing page* is a direct clone, but team and player pages are not, because the underlying SPA routes they'd need to hydrate into don't all exist.

**Real gap D-102 didn't catch, found by checking `js/navigation.js`'s dispatch and `js/ncaab.js`/`js/wnba.js`'s `_render{NCAAB,WNBA}View` switches directly (not assumed from CLAUDE.md, which is exactly what D-102 itself warns against):** neither NCAAB nor WNBA has a client-side per-team route — `{sport}-teams` renders a grid only, there's no `{sport}-team-{id}` case in either dispatch switch. NCAAB additionally has no player-detail view of any kind (CLAUDE.md already correctly said this was "not built, owner decision pending" — D-102's own gap-finding pass just didn't connect that fact to what it implies for edge-render scope). WNBA *does* have a real player-detail route (`wnba-player-{id}` → `showWNBAPlayer`, D-092 Resolution 5) — confirmed live via `/api/wnbastats` and `/api/wnbaathlete`, both already-shipped, already-working endpoints.

**Decision:** ship exactly what has a real hydration target, not a mechanical full clone of the NCAAF three-template set:
- `functions/ncaab/index.js` (landing) + `functions/ncaab/standings.js` (conference standings) — clones of `functions/ncaaf/index.js` / `functions/ncaaf/standings.js`, same recursive conference/division collector reused verbatim in the standings template since NCAAB's tree shape (mostly flat, occasionally divisioned) is a strict subset of what that collector already handles.
- `functions/wnba/index.js` (landing) + `functions/wnba/standings.js` (conference standings, same collector — WNBA's tree is flatter still, two conferences with no division level) + `functions/wnba/leaders.js` (clone of `functions/nfl/leaders.js` against `/api/wnbastats`) + `functions/wnba/player/[id]/[[slug]].js` (clone of `functions/nfl/player/[id]/[[slug]].js`, simplified since WNBA is ESPN-native end-to-end — no Sleeper-id bridge needed, the route's id is already the ESPN athlete id `/api/wnbaathlete` expects).
- No `/ncaab/team/`, no `/wnba/team/`, no `/ncaab/player/`. Building any of these now would set `window.__SS_ROUTE` to a view `_render{NCAAB,WNBA}View` doesn't handle — the SPA would fall through to nothing, silently. **This is the one new rule worth keeping for the next sport-surface edge-render pass: verify the client-side route exists before writing the template that hydrates into it — a crawlable page with no SPA feature behind it is worse than no page**, since it wastes crawl budget on a dead end instead of just not existing.
- `_routes.json` gained `/ncaab`, `/ncaab/*`, `/wnba`, `/wnba/*`. `tools/gen-sitemap.cjs` gained both landings, both standings pages, `/wnba/leaders`, and a new players section pulling ids/names straight off the already-live `/api/wnbastats` response (mirrors the existing NCAAF-players section's pattern of using the site's own compact leaders endpoint instead of paginating ESPN's core API directly).

**Verification:** all four new page types round-tripped correctly under `wrangler pages dev` against real upstream data before this was called done — not just read-through review. `/ncaab`, `/ncaab/standings`, `/wnba`, `/wnba/standings`, `/wnba/leaders` all returned correct `<title>`/canonical/`__SS_ROUTE`. `/wnba/player/3149391/a-ja-wilson` (a real id pulled live from `/api/wnbastats`) rendered the correct name/canonical/JSON-LD, with the apostrophe correctly HTML-escaped in the title. An invalid player id correctly fell back to the plain, unmodified shell (200, generic title) rather than erroring — confirms the fail-safe path actually works, not just that it's present in the code.

**Implications:** the sitemap URL count rose from 1666 to 1700 with this pass (on top of D-120's 1553→1666). Team and player pages for NCAAB, and team pages for WNBA, remain a real, separate future item — but it's gated behind a client-side feature (a team-detail view for both sports, a player-detail view for NCAAB) shipping first, not behind more edge-render work. That's worth surfacing to the owner as its own decision, not bundled into this one. This entry closes D-102's tracked gap for what's buildable today; D-102 itself should be considered resolved-with-narrower-scope rather than fully resolved.

## D-122 — NFL Week 1 (2026-09-09) readiness pass: one real gap closed, two findings deliberately left alone

**Status:** `/nfl/standings` shipped and smoke-tested. Two other findings investigated and documented, not fixed — both low-severity, low-exposure, and judged not worth the risk of touching shared NFL code this close to real Week 1 traffic.
**Contributors:** Axiom
**Date:** 2026-08-31

**Trigger:** owner asked for an NFL-focused push ahead of Week 1. Confirmed the real 2026 opener date first rather than assuming: ESPN's own `week=1&seasontype=2&year=2026` scoreboard returns Wed Sept 9 (NE @ SEA) as the earliest game, with the bulk of the 16-game slate Sunday Sept 13 and MNF Sept 14 — about 9-13 days out from this session. No games exist anywhere between 2026-08-31 and 2026-09-07 (preseason is fully over; this is the annual pre-Week-1 dead week, roster-cut season).

**Shipped: `functions/nfl/standings.js` → `/nfl/standings`.** NFL had a live, rich standings feature (`js/nflStandings.js` — division tables, playoff bracket, seeding) but was the one sport of MLB/NFL/NCAAF with no crawlable path for it, an inconsistency that predates this session. Clone of `functions/ncaaf/standings.js`'s recursive conference/division collector against `/api/nflstandings` — AFC/NFC → 4 divisions each is a strict subset of the shape that collector already handles, so no new logic was needed, just the template. Covered by the existing `/nfl/*` `_routes.json` entry. `tools/gen-sitemap.cjs` gained the URL; sitemap regenerated (1700 → 1704). Smoke-tested live: correct `<title>`/canonical/`__SS_ROUTE`, all 8 divisions render with real 2025-season data (2026 doesn't exist yet, correctly falls back — see below).

**Verified clean, no action needed:** Draft HQ (needs ~9s to finish its Sleeper ADP + VBD + bye-week fetch chain — an initial 3.5s screenshot wait made it look stuck; it wasn't), the Mock Draft simulator (`#nfl-mock` — 886 players loaded from live Sleeper ADP), the Injury Report (449 real Sleeper-sourced entries; initially looked suspicious — Patrick Mahomes tagged "Questionable" with an ACL-surgery note — but that's genuinely what Sleeper's own feed reports, not a SportStrata parsing bug, and the full status distribution (Questionable/IR/PUP/Sus/NA/DNR/Out) is real and varied once you scroll past the first section), and NFL Standings itself (correctly defaults to the 2025 season since 2026 has no data yet, matching `NFL_STATS_SEASON`'s month≥9 rule).

**Found, documented, deliberately not fixed — both narrow and self-resolving:**
1. **Scores page week-pill nav shows preseason labels ("HOF Game, Wk 1, Wk 2, Wk 3") while the "Today" tab correctly displays real Week 1 regular-season games.** Root cause: `_nflScoresNavDefaults()` (js/nfl.js) derives the pill row's active tab from `_nflSeasonPhase()`'s pure calendar heuristic (still correctly says "preseason" today, since no games have been played), while `loadNFLGames()`'s default "Today" fetch pulls whatever ESPN's own zero-param scoreboard pointer returns — which has already independently rolled forward to Week 1 since there's nothing left in preseason to show. Those two signals only disagree during this specific ~9-day dead-week gap each year. The games shown are correct; only the pill labeling context is stale, and it's inert (no pill claims to be "active"), not a false claim. A proper fix means threading real fetched season/week metadata from `fetchNFLScoreboard()` back into `_renderNFLScoresNav()` instead of trusting the separate calendar heuristic — a real change to a shared, high-traffic data path. Given the exposure window is under two weeks and self-closes the moment `_nflSeasonPhase()` crosses into 'regular' on its own, this was judged not worth the regression risk of touching that path days before the real event. Worth fixing properly in the off-season, not now.
2. **The season-phase model's code comment ("the season opener is always the Thursday after Labor Day") is factually wrong for 2026** — Labor Day 2026 is Monday Sept 7, so "the Thursday after" would be Sept 10, but the real opener is Wednesday Sept 9. The boundary value itself (`day <= 8` in September still counts as preseason) still happens to resolve correctly this year, since day 9 falls through to 'regular' regardless of which weekday it lands on — but that margin is now known to be exactly one day, not the multi-day cushion the comment implies. Not changed this session (the boundary works, the comment doesn't need to block anything) — flagged so whoever next touches `_nflSeasonPhase()` knows the "Thursday after Labor Day" assumption isn't reliable and shouldn't be load-bearing.

**Implications:** no regressions risked in shared NFL code this close to kickoff. The one shipped change is purely additive (a new Function, a new sitemap line) with zero blast radius on existing behavior. The two documented-not-fixed items are both real and both worth a look in the off-season, but neither is a Week 1 blocker.

## D-123 — Team roster pages were counting IR players as active on all 32 NFL teams, fixed post-cutdown

**Status:** shipped, live-verified against real Sleeper data with an exact before/after count match.
**Contributors:** Axiom
**Date:** 2026-08-31

**Trigger:** owner asked for team-roster and injury/waiver-wire functionality checks now that real 53-man rosters are set (confirmed live: ESPN's KC roster split shows 55 active/specialTeam + 12 practice squad + 9 IR, i.e. real post-cutdown shape, not bloated 90-man preseason rosters).

**Found:** `_renderNFLTeamDetail`'s roster (`js/nfl.js`) is built from Sleeper's player pool filtered on `p.active && p.team === sAbbr` — but `p.active` is Sleeper's "still rostered somewhere in the league" flag, not "on this team's active roster." It says nothing about IR status. Sleeper's own `status` field is the real signal, and it's a clean binary league-wide (`Active`: 2502, `Inactive`: 170 — verified across every active-and-rostered player, no other values), with every current `Inactive` case carrying `injury_status: 'IR'`. Without checking it, IR players were counted in the header's Offense/Defense/Special-Teams tallies and mixed unlabeled into position groups on **every one of the 32 team pages** — for KC specifically, 5 of 64 listed "roster" players (Matt Waletzko, Ethan Downs, Jimmy Holiday, John Michael Gyllenborg, Jeff Caldwell) were actually on IR.

**Fix:** added `p.status !== 'Inactive'` to the roster filter. Deliberately scoped to just this one call site — grepped for the same `p.active && p.team` pattern elsewhere in `js/nfl.js` first and found two other uses that **must** keep including IR/Inactive players: the Injury Report's own pool filter (`displayNFLInjuries`, obviously — the whole page is about who's hurt) and the Waiver Wire's teammate lookup (`displayNFLWaivers`'s `byTeamPos`, which powers the "possible opportunity: [teammate] (IR)" trending-add context — confirmed live-working, e.g. "Zavion Thomas... possible opportunity: Ray-Ray McCloud (IR)"). Excluding IR there would have silently broken that feature. Fixing only the genuinely wrong call site, not pattern-matching the fix everywhere the pattern appears.

**Verification — a real methodology finding along the way, not just the fix itself.** The first several re-screenshots after editing `js/nfl.js` kept showing the old, unfixed count (64), even with a direct `curl` confirming the dev server was serving the edited file and with the CDP session's HTTP cache explicitly disabled. Root cause: the same long-running headless Chrome process had been reused across many navigations earlier in the session, and this site's service worker (`sw.js` precaches `/js/*`) had installed and was shadowing every subsequent request for `js/nfl.js` with its own cached copy — `Network.setCacheDisabled` only touches the browser HTTP cache, a layer below where a SW intercepts. This is the exact same class of gotcha D-119 documented for live-site verification ("SW clearing alone is not proof of a live deploy"), now confirmed to apply equally to local headless-Chrome verification, not just production. Fixed by using a fresh, isolated CDP browser context (`Target.createBrowserContext`) per check instead of reusing one tab — folded into `.claude/commands/screenshot.md` as the new default technique. With that, the count dropped from 64 to 59 exactly as predicted (offense 32→28, defense 28→27 — matching the 4 offense-position + 1 defense-position IR players found), confirming the fix against real data, not just code review.

**Also verified, no action needed:** the Injury Report's status distribution is real and varied (Questionable/IR/PUP/Sus/NA/DNR/Out, 449 total) — an earlier same-session read of a partial, unscrolled screenshot had looked like everything was uniformly "Questionable," which was a viewing artifact, not a bug (see D-122's session log). The Waiver Wire's trending-add list and its "possible opportunity" teammate-injury context both render correctly against live Sleeper data.

**Follow-up, same session:** the blank-name DST row flagged above was fixed once the pattern showed up a second time (Trending's own top-12 list, not just Waiver Wire) — confirmed it was systemic rather than a one-off before spending time on it. Root cause: Sleeper's 32 team-DEF entries have no `full_name` field at all, only `first_name`/`last_name` (e.g. `"Houston"`/`"Texans"`) — verified directly against the raw pool data, not guessed. Added `_nflPlayerName(p)` (`js/nfl.js`), a small helper falling back to `first_name + last_name` then `team`, and applied it at the two confirmed call sites (`displayNFLTrending`, `displayNFLWaivers`) rather than every `p.full_name` reference in the file — grepped first and confirmed the other 13+ occurrences are all in individual-skill-position contexts (player detail, Players page's top-120-by-ADP, etc.) that never actually receive a DEF-position object, so patching them would have been unnecessary surface area. Live-verified: Trending's row 12 now reads "Jacksonville Jaguars, JAX · DEF" instead of a blank name.

`sw.js` CACHE_NAME bumped v224 → v225 (js/nfl.js is a precached static asset).

**Implications:** this was a real, live-user-facing accuracy bug on the single highest-traffic NFL page type (every team's roster) at the exact moment (post-cutdown, pre-Week-1) people most need it to be right for fantasy and depth-chart decisions. The isolated-browser-context verification technique is now the documented default for any future local check of a JS/CSS edit, not just this one.

## D-124 — MLB live game viewer: ground-up information-architecture redesign, not a re-skin

**Status:** shipped (`js/liveGame.js`, `css/liveGame.css`), syntax-checked, visually verified against real + lightly-mutated MLB feed data via a local test harness (see Verification). Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-02

**Trigger:** owner request to rebuild the live game viewer around baseball's actual information hierarchy (GAME → INNING → HALF-INNING → PLATE APPEARANCE → PITCH → RESULT) instead of the D-117-era component order, with an explicit "understand game state in under two seconds" priority list (score → inning/half → outs → bases → count → batter → pitcher → last pitch → last play → on-deck) and a hard split between live-following info, traditional stats, and advanced analytics.

**Shipped:**
- **Scorebug header** — score/linescore unchanged, but the half-inning state (`▲ TOP 10TH` / `▼ BOT 10TH`) is now a real prominent badge, not a small mono tag buried in the meta row.
- **New default tab, LIVE**, replacing the old always-visible "Now card": Last Play (new, standalone), Current Situation (new composed readout — "BOT 10TH · 1 OUT · RUNNERS ON 1ST AND 2ND [RISP] · 2-1 COUNT"), a promoted ~150px interactive Base Diamond (click an occupied base to open the runner's player card via the existing delegated `[data-player-id]` listener), a batter/pitcher matchup card with the count now directly under the batter, a shrunk (120px) strike zone paired with a new chronological Pitch Sequence table, pitch-mix bars (replacing the old donut wheel), Due Up with real ON DECK / IN THE HOLE labels, and win probability last.
- **New STATCAST tab** — live this-game pitch velocity/spin bars and a batted-ball table (`hitData.launchAngle` read for the first time), plus a season-percentile block reusing `mlb.js`'s existing `fetchStatcast()`/`_renderStatcastCard()` verbatim, explicitly captioned "not live, updated daily" (Savant's own cache TTL is DAILY).
- Terminology tables (`_LG_CALL_TERMS`, `_LG_EVENT_TERMS`) standardize CALLED STRIKE/SWINGING STRIKE/FOUL/BALL/BALL IN PLAY and WALK/HBP/ERROR/DOUBLE PLAY/HOME RUN etc. everywhere a pitch or play result is shown (tooltip, Pitch Sequence, Last Play, Play-by-Play).
- Polling was restructured around one dispatcher, `_renderActiveLgTab`, called both per-poll and on tab click — mirrors `js/nflLiveGame.js`'s D-080 mount-shell-once/update-active-tab-only split, so a poll tick never disturbs a tab the viewer isn't looking at, and tab-content scroll position now survives a poll (it didn't before).
- Zone honesty caption added ("Dot position is tracked pitch location; dot color is the umpire's call — not an automated strike-zone determination"). ABS challenge state and a live pitch-timer countdown were deliberately **not** built — neither is confirmed present on `feed/live` in this codebase's own field inventory, matching the same live-verify-before-shipping discipline D-080/D-105/D-106 established for NFL.
- Desktop-specific fix, found via screenshot review after initial ship: the Pitch Zone/Sequence/Mix block was centered in a narrow (420px) column, leaving a wide dead margin on either side of it inside the ~830px page-mode panel. At `min-width: 640px`, scoped to `.lg-live-page` only (never the inline accordion, which sits in a narrower game-card column regardless of viewport), the small zone now sits beside the sequence table instead of stacked above it.

**Two real, pre-existing bugs found and fixed along the way (not introduced by this redesign, confirmed against live API data via the test harness below):**
1. **Base occupancy** was derived from `currentPlay.runners[].movement.end`, which came back empty or unresolved mid-at-bat in 2 of 3 real live games checked (DET@MIN, NYY@LAA, STL@LAD, all in progress 2026-09-02). Switched `_lgOccupiedBases` (shared by the base diamond, the Current Situation phrase, and the D-117 Phase 6 win-expectancy lookup) to read `linescore.offense.first/second/third` instead — a direct per-base `{id, fullName}` object that was populated correctly in all three games. `_buildDueUp`'s ON DECK/IN THE HOLE were similarly upgraded to read `linescore.offense.onDeck`/`.inHole` directly rather than walking `battingOrder` from the current batter's index.
2. **Pitch velocity** was read from a top-level `playEvents[].startSpeed`, which does not exist on real pitch events — confirmed via a real captured payload that the field is `playEvents[].pitchData.startSpeed`. This means the pitch tooltip (`_buildPitchZone`) and the hero's "last pitch" velocity (`_buildMatchupWithCount`) have likely shown "—" in production since they shipped (spin rate, on the adjacent `pitchData.breaks.spinRate` path, was separately live-verified 2026-08-20 and has been correct). Fixed via one new `_lgPitchVelo(pitchEvent)` helper (checks `pitchData.startSpeed` first, falls back to the old top-level path) applied at all four call sites.

**Verification:** `node --check` after each phase; a CSS brace-balance sanity check. No `wrangler pages dev` local server could be reached during this session — every port tried (8792, 8793) had a stray, unrelated process (confirmed via `netstat`, a different project's dev server) intercepting it ahead of wrangler's own listener, and killing those processes didn't resolve it. Rather than keep fighting the environment, verification used a static test harness (`_test_harness.html`, deleted before this entry was written, not committed) that loads the real script chain (`config.js` through `liveGame.js`, matching `index.html`'s order) against a real captured `feed/live` payload (DET@MIN, gamePk 823660) with a handful of fields edited (base runners, count, a synthetic 5-pitch at-bat) to exercise every visual state in one pass — RISP, multi-runner occupancy, a full pitch sequence, a scoring Last Play. `showMLBLiveGame`'s own network calls were stubbed via a `window.fetch` Proxy returning the same fixture, so the real poll loop, tab-switch dispatch, and async season-stat/H2H fetches all ran unmodified. Confirmed at both a single-column width and the real desktop `.lg-live-page` (sidebar) layout, and in both themes. Narrow-viewport (<500px) automated screenshot verification was attempted but abandoned: both CDP `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` and the `--window-size` launch flag reported/rendered `window.innerWidth` as ~500-980px regardless of the width requested (a Chrome-152/environment issue, not a site bug) — a CSS-constrained-container test (a 390px-wide div with a visible border) showed no internal overflow, which is reassuring but not a substitute for a true narrow-viewport check; a real mobile check is still owed before this is called fully verified.

`sw.js` CACHE_NAME bumped v233 → v234 (`js/liveGame.js`/`css/liveGame.css` are precached static assets).

**Implications:** this is the single largest single-session change to the live game viewer since D-117 shipped its six phases — worth a careful read-through diff before commit, not a rubber stamp, given the scope. The two bugs fixed (base occupancy, pitch velocity) were latent in shipped, live production code and are corrections independent of whether the redesign itself is kept as-is. `js/liveGame.js`'s CLAUDE.md Key Files entry needs updating in the same commit as this ships (doc-sync rule) — the old "P3-025 ... pitch zone, box score" description no longer reflects the file's actual shape (6 tabs, not the old 4; a Statcast tab; the terminology layer). A true narrow-viewport screenshot check (via a working local server, not this session's harness workaround) is the one open item before calling mobile fully verified.

## D-125 — NCAAF injury report / depth chart parity investigated and ruled out; shipped a keyword-tagged NCAAF news feed instead

**Status:** shipped (`js/news.js`, `functions/api/news.js`), syntax-checked, `check-manifest.cjs`/`check-themes.cjs --strict` both clean (2 known pre-existing theme warnings only), live-verified against real ESPN data via `wrangler pages dev`. Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-03

**Trigger:** owner asked to bring NCAAF up to parity with NFL on injuries and depth charts, while a separate project (BotChase) is mid-development.

**Investigated and ruled out — no free path to real parity exists, live-checked against four sources before writing any feature code:**
1. NCAAF's own live-game `/summary` has no `injuries` key at all — already established by D-120, re-confirmed here.
2. ESPN's per-team `site.api.espn.com/.../teams/{id}/injuries` and `/depthchart` both return empty `{}` — checked against a confirmed-valid team id (Florida, 57). Re-checked the identical NFL paths (Chiefs, 12) for comparison: also empty. Not a college-specific gap — these paths simply don't work for football at all, which is exactly why NFL's own Injury Report doesn't use them.
3. ESPN's league-wide `site.api.espn.com/.../injuries` feed: for NFL, 32 teams / 800 entries, dates through the day of the check. The identical endpoint for college football has **3 entries total, the newest dated 2022** — a dead, abandoned feed, not a smaller-but-live one.
4. `sports.core.api.espn.com/.../teams/{id}/depthchart` 404s for both NFL and NCAAF — not a real endpoint at all.
5. CollegeFootballData.com (the actively-maintained, dedicated free CFB stats API) — pulled its full OpenAPI spec (`api-docs.json`, 84 endpoints) and grepped for anything injury/depth/roster-related. Only match: a plain `/roster` list (jersey/position/height/weight), no starter order, no injury status.

NFL's real Injury Report (`nfl.js`, N-17) works because Sleeper's player pool carries `depth_chart_order`/`injury_status` fields directly — and Sleeper is an NFL-only fantasy platform with zero college football coverage. This is the same "CFB player data too sparse" wall D-042 already hit deferring player leaders/detail, showing up again for a different feature — not a new or surprising gap once traced to its root.

**Shipped instead — an honest substitute, not a fake structured report:**
- **NCAAF now has a real News feed at all** (it had none before this — the `news` view's `loadNews()` hard-coded `sport !== 'mlb' && sport !== 'nfl'` to fall back to `mlb`, so clicking "News" while browsing NCAAF silently showed MLB headlines under an "MLB — Latest" title the whole time NCAAF has been a live surface since D-042. Found as a side effect of this investigation, fixed as part of the same change since the fix is the same one-line `NEWS_SPORTS` array edit this feature needed anyway).
- `functions/api/news.js`'s `LEAGUES` map gained `ncaaf: 'football/college-football'` — confirmed live current (today's dated articles, real headlines, real `links.web.href`) before wiring in, same discipline as the injury-endpoint checks above.
- `js/news.js`: `NEWS_INJURY_RE` (a keyword regex — "injury", "questionable", "torn ACL", "ruled out", "placed on IR", etc.) tags matching headlines with the existing `.roster-il-badge` pill (reused verbatim, not a new component) and powers an All/Injury-Related filter-pill pair (same inline-chip pattern `displayNFLInjuries` already established, not a new pattern). The empty state and the page's own caption are explicit that this is a keyword match over real headlines, not an official report — so the feature can never be mistaken for the structured data investigated and ruled out above. Applied to all three news sports (mlb/nfl/ncaaf), not gated to ncaaf-only, since it's genuinely useful anywhere and keeping one code path is simpler than forking one.

**Bug found and fixed along the way:** the CSP `img-src` directive only allowed `a.espncdn.com`, but CFB news article thumbnails load from `s.espncdn.com`/`s.secure.espncdn.com` — ESPN's CDN host isn't stable across requests. Rather than chase specific subdomains, switched to `https://*.espncdn.com`, matching the wildcard pattern this same CSP line already used for `*.mlbstatic.com`. Live-verified in a real browser (not assumed from the CSP spec text) that the wildcard does match a 3-level host like `s.secure.espncdn.com`. Fixed in both `index.html`'s meta tag and `_headers` (this CSP is duplicated in both places, per the existing D-024 comment on that duplication).

`sw.js` CACHE_NAME bumped v234 → v235 (`js/news.js` is a precached static asset).

**Implications:** the owner's actual ask (injury/depth-chart parity) turns out to be structurally unbuildable for free, not merely un-built — worth remembering before this gets re-requested. The side-effect bug fix (NCAAF News silently showing MLB content) was a real, live, user-facing mislabeling issue on a nav tab that's been present since NCAAF shipped, caught only because this investigation touched the exact code path.

## D-127 — Game-start alert (D-079) discoverability nudge on first follow; real bug found and fixed in `bot/digest.py`'s highlight detection

**Status:** shipped (`js/auth.js`), syntax-checked, live-verified via headless Chrome (CDP) against the real toast/sign-in-sheet flow, both signed-in and signed-out paths. Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-03

**Trigger:** owner asked for user-retention/acquisition work while an unrelated background task (season history seeding, below) ran.

**Retention finding:** D-079's game-start push alerts shipped and are deployed, but the only way to discover the feature is the toggle on the Account page (`renderAccountView`) — nothing on the site ever points a visitor there. A user who follows their first team via the star icon (which works fully signed-out, per D-031/D-034) gets no signal that alerts exist at all. A real capability was sitting unused for lack of a discovery path, not lack of demand.

**Shipped:** `_maybeNudgePushAlerts(sport, entityType, entityId)`, called from `toggleFollow()` right after a follow turns on. Fires once ever (`localStorage` `zs_push_nudge_shown`), only on a visitor's first-ever follow (`AuthState.follows.size === 1`), only where `_pushSupported()` is true, and only if not already subscribed. Renders via the existing `ErrorHandler.toast()` component (`info` type, 12s duration, not the default 5s — this one has an action, not just a message) with an inline "Enable game alerts" button. Signed in → calls `enablePushAlerts()` directly. Signed out → `openAuthSheet({type:'push_alert',...})`, replayed by `_onSignedIn()` once sign-in completes (same detour pattern `save_draft`/`reload_view` intents already use — no new mechanism). Deliberately does not gate anything currently free (D-079's own comment already established push itself requires sign-in, since a subscription row is meaningless without an account to attach it to).

`sw.js` CACHE_NAME bumped v235 → v236 (`js/auth.js` is a precached static asset).

**Separately: a real, unrelated bug found and fixed while generating content for the acquisition side of this same request.** `bot/digest.py` (the tool that drafts social posts, including rare/"unprecedented" stat-line highlights) has produced zero highlights on every single day since it was built — `bot/data_fetcher.py`'s `extract_batting_lines`/`extract_pitching_lines` read a `playerStats[side][personId]` key that does not exist in `statsapi.boxscore_data()`'s real response shape. Live-checked against a real completed game (2026-09-02, Padres @ Reds): the actual shape is a flat `{side}Batters`/`{side}Pitchers` row list (string-typed stat fields, a header row with `personId: 0`). Fixed in `data_fetcher.py`; re-running the last 5 days immediately surfaced a real highlight (Pete Crow-Armstrong, 2026-08-29: 4-for-6, 3 HR, 6 RBI, 4 R, 13 TB) that the tool had silently missed before. This bug also affected `main.py`'s `mode_seed` (same extraction functions), so the one-time historical-database seed used for `db.is_unprecedented()`'s rarity check had never actually populated anything either — a re-seed of 2024/2025 was kicked off same-day to make future "unprecedented" claims credible rather than defaulting-true against an empty table.

**Implications:** both findings are the same shape — real, deployed capability, silently inert due to a wiring gap rather than a missing feature. Worth checking for more of these (a feature that's "shipped" per a past decision entry isn't the same claim as "a user can actually reach it" or "it actually returns data") before assuming the product needs new features rather than exposure of what already exists.

## D-128 — Dynamic per-entity `og:image` (fully edge-generated), first template live on MLB player pages

**Status:** shipped for one template (`functions/mlb/player/[id]/[[slug]].js`), other sports/templates not yet wired (see Next below). Syntax-checked, dependency-installed, live-verified end-to-end via `wrangler pages dev` against real MLB Stats API data (Aaron Judge, id 592450) — real PNG returned, real stat line, real team name. Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-03

**Trigger:** the og:image gap flagged four separate times across D-056/D-057/D-062/docs-archive without ever being actioned (always parked as "owner call" between a cheap static-per-type fix and a full dynamic per-entity build). Owner picked the full dynamic build this time, explicitly accepting the tradeoff of a new runtime dependency.

**What shipped:**
- `functions/_og.js` (underscore-prefixed — not a route, imported only) — `renderOgPng({env, url, eyebrow, title, subtitle, statLine, accentColor})` renders a 1200×630 PNG via **satori** (JSX-like tree → self-contained SVG, no external font resolution needed downstream) piped into **@resvg/resvg-wasm** (SVG → PNG). Layout: colored left identity bar (this codebase's existing "border=identity" house pattern, not a new visual language) + eyebrow/title/subtitle/stat-line stack + a `SPORTSTRATA.CC` watermark, all in Barlow Semi Condensed (the site's own `--font-display` token font) at 3 weights.
- `functions/api/og.js` — `GET /api/og?eyebrow=&title=&subtitle=&stat=&accent=` — the routed endpoint, covered by the existing `/api/*` rate limiter (`functions/api/_middleware.js`, no new route needed in `_routes.json`). Query values are length-capped (60–90 chars) before rendering — bounded cost per request, real callers never approach the caps. Fails safe: any render error redirects to the existing static `/assets/og-default.png` rather than surfacing a broken image or a 500 to a crawler.
- Fonts (`assets/fonts/BarlowSemiCondensed-{Bold,SemiBold,Regular}.ttf`, from the google/fonts OFL repo) are real static-site assets fetched via `env.ASSETS.fetch()` at request time — **not** a JS/wasm import. This site's live deploy is the Cloudflare Pages dashboard's git-integrated build (per `wrangler.toml`'s own comment, this repo's `wrangler.toml` doesn't drive the actual deploy), so a custom `wrangler.toml [[rules]]` module rule for `.ttf` would do nothing in production even though it might work under local `wrangler pages dev` — `env.ASSETS.fetch()` is the one font-loading approach guaranteed to behave identically in both. Cached at isolate scope (module-level `let`) after first fetch, same pattern as every other warm-request cache in this codebase.
- New dependencies: `satori` (pinned to **0.32.0**, deliberately not latest) and `@resvg/resvg-wasm`, both MPL-2.0 (verified before adding — not a copyleft risk for a consuming application, only for modifications to the licensed files themselves). `package.json` also gained an `overrides.fflate: ^0.8.3` to patch a transitive moderate DoS advisory in satori's own `@shuding/opentype.js` dependency.
- `.wasm` (resvg's `index_bg.wasm`) is imported directly as a JS module (`import RESVG_WASM from '@resvg/resvg-wasm/index_bg.wasm'`) — unlike the font-file case above, this **is** safe under the dashboard build: Cloudflare's Pages Functions bundler has native, build-path-independent support for `.wasm` imports (produces a `WebAssembly.Module` directly), so it doesn't depend on this repo's `wrangler.toml` at all.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to the OG-image work itself but caught because building it required reading every field this Function touches:** `functions/mlb/player/[id]/[[slug]].js` has fetched `/people/{id}` with **no `currentTeam` hydrate** since it shipped (D-041 Phase 1) — live-confirmed directly against the MLB Stats API that `currentTeam` is simply absent from the response without that hydrate flag, hydrate or no other hydrate. This means **every MLB player page's team name has been silently blank** the entire time: the meta description's `· {team}` clause, the JSON-LD `Person.affiliation`, and — worse — the crawlable snapshot's team link that D-114 specifically believed it had added ("previously rendered the team as plain text with zero outbound links... now links to /mlb/team/:abbr") never actually rendered anything at all, text or link, because `team` was always the empty string. Fixed by adding `currentTeam` to the existing hydrate string (now `currentTeam,stats(group=[hitting,pitching],type=season,season={year})` — combined with the new stats hydrate this template needed anyway, one request, not two). Live-verified: team name, team link, and JSON-LD affiliation all populate correctly now.

**Satori version pinned deliberately, not "latest":** `satori@0.33.0` added HarfBuzz text-shaping support, bundled as a top-level `import` of `harfbuzzjs` with a Node-`fs`-relative WASM auto-loader baked into the *import itself* (not just a function call) — this throws inside Cloudflare Pages Functions' bundled Worker at module-eval time (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'href')`, confirmed live via `wrangler pages dev`), in both the regular and `/standalone` builds (the standalone build's README section describing it as the Workers-safe path predates 0.33.0 and is now stale for this exact reason). Matches an open, unresolved upstream report (`github.com/vercel/satori/issues/794`, filed as a Cloudflare Workers build failure). **0.32.0** is the last version before HarfBuzz landed and has none of this — plain Latin text (player names, team names, stat lines) needs no complex-script shaping anyway, so nothing is lost by staying on it. Revisit only if upstream resolves #794 and a specific feature (ligatures, RTL/complex scripts) is actually needed.

**Not yet done — explicitly scoped as a fast-follow, not forgotten:**
- Only one template (MLB player) is wired. `functions/mlb/team/[abbr].js`, `functions/mlb/game/[pk].js`, `functions/mlb/leaders.js`, and the NFL/NCAAF/NCAAB/WNBA equivalents all still serve the static `og-default.png`. The renderer (`_og.js`) is generic (`eyebrow`/`title`/`subtitle`/`statLine`/`accentColor`) and asks nothing MLB-specific, so wiring another template is a query-string-construction change in that template's own Function, not new renderer work.
- `accentColor` is wired end-to-end (team-color-aware cards are one param away) but no caller passes one yet — every card today renders in brand orange. A per-team color map would need adding (client-side `getMLBTeamColors`/`getTeamColors` equivalents exist in `js/mlb.js`/`js/config.js` but aren't available server-side; either duplicate a small map the way `MLB_TEAM_ABBR` is already duplicated across Functions, or leave it default-orange, which is itself a legitimate, deliberate brand-consistency choice, not obviously wrong).
- No live-crawler verification yet (paste a real `/mlb/player/...` URL into the X/Slack/Facebook card debuggers) — only local `wrangler pages dev` was checked. Do this once deployed, same discipline as the D-041/D-050 rollouts' own "live verify after push" step.

**Implications:** this closes the actual, four-times-deferred SEO/share-CTR gap, but only proves the pipeline works — the growth value scales with how many templates get wired, not with this one page existing. The `currentTeam` bug is a reminder that "shipped" Functions can have silently-empty fields for a long time without anyone noticing, since a blank string never throws or fails a health check — the same shape as D-127's two findings just above.

## D-129 — NCAAF `/scoreboard` `situation` live-verified; found and fixed a real `isLive` bug affecting both NCAAF and NFL

**Status:** shipped (`js/ncaaf.js`, `js/nfl.js`, `js/ncaafLiveGame.js` comment, `CLAUDE.md`). NCAAF fix live-verified against a real in-progress game via direct simulation of the exact updated code against captured live API responses. NFL's identical fix is **not** live-verified (no NFL game in progress — season starts 2026-09-10) — applied preemptively on the strength of the identical upstream shape and a strictly-more-inclusive match, per its own code comment. Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-03

**Trigger:** owner reported live NCAAF games were underway and asked to find documentation on what was planned for live games — this was exactly the pending task recorded in memory (`project_ncaaf_live_verification.md`, written by a prior session): verify ESPN's `/scoreboard` `situation` field shape once a real NCAAF game went live, to unblock the deferred field-position graphic and confirm the home-hero live-detail read D-126 shipped defensively.

**What the live check found (via `sportstrata.cc/api/ncaaf?path=/scoreboard`, event 401856663, UAPB @ MIZ, in progress at check time):**
1. `comp.situation` on `/scoreboard` carries the same shape NFL's D-096 already confirmed: numeric `down`/`yardLine`/`distance`, `possession` (a team id), `isRedZone`, `downDistanceText`/`shortDownDistanceText`/`possessionText`, and a nested `lastPlay`. Live-verified real values: `{down:1, yardLine:84, distance:10, possession:"142", isRedZone:true, downDistanceText:"1st & 10 at UAPB 16"}` against `competitors` containing id `142` = MIZ.
2. Cross-checked `/summary?event=401856663`: `header.competitions[0]` has **no** `situation` key at all — confirms `js/ncaafLiveGame.js`'s existing assumption (previously unverified, now verified) that this data only exists on `/scoreboard`, not `/summary`.
3. `drives.current`/`drives.previous` in the same `/summary` response: `current.id` (`4018566638`) genuinely appears again inside `previous[]` — live-confirms the exact overlap scenario `_nclgRenderPbp`'s dedupe guard (`js/ncaafLiveGame.js`) was written defensively for. Checked the guard's actual filter logic against this real data: it correctly excludes the duplicate. No code change needed here — this only upgrades the guard from "untested, ported preemptively" to "confirmed necessary and correct."
4. **A real, separate bug, found because building the fix above required reading `comp.situation.text` and discovering it doesn't exist:** `fetchNCAAFScoreboard` (`js/ncaaf.js`) was passing `comp.situation` straight through untouched. `js/app.js`'s `_heroNCAAFLiveDetail` reads `g.situation.text` — a field ESPN's raw payload never provides. NFL's own `fetchNFLScoreboard` (`js/nfl.js`, D-096) never had this problem because it **synthesizes** `text` itself (`${teamAbbr} · ${shortDownDistanceText}`) rather than assuming ESPN supplies a ready-made string — NCAAF's version, written by analogy to NFL's, copied the *shape* of that comment without copying the *synthesis logic* it depended on. Net effect: the NCAAF home-hero live-detail line has rendered nothing since D-126 shipped it, for every live NCAAF game since. Fixed by porting NFL's exact synthesis logic into `fetchNCAAFScoreboard`.
5. **A second, more consequential bug, found by simulating the fix against the live capture:** the synthesized `situation` never appeared even after the fix above, because `isLive` itself was `false` for the live game being checked. Both `js/ncaaf.js` and `js/nfl.js` derive `isLive` from `status?.type?.name === 'STATUS_IN_PROGRESS' || status?.type?.name === 'STATUS_HALFTIME'` — an enum that does not include `STATUS_END_PERIOD`, which the live-captured game was sitting in (end of 1st quarter) at check time, confirmed via ESPN's own `status.type.state`, which reported `"in"` for the exact same moment. This means `isLive` has been silently `false` — dropping the LIVE badge, hero eligibility, and the situation line — for some real span of time at every quarter break of every NCAAF (and, unverified but same-pattern, NFL) game. Fixed by switching both to `status?.type?.state === 'in'`, ESPN's own canonical pre/in/post classification, confirmed against three real states in the same check (a live `'in'` game, a `'post'`-final game, and a `'pre'`-scheduled game) — a strict superset of the old name-enum, so the fix can only recover previously-missed live states, not un-match a real one.

**Not done — explicitly out of scope for this check:** the live field-position graphic in `js/ncaafLiveGame.js` itself remains unbuilt. This check resolved the data-shape uncertainty that was blocking it (per that file's own comment, now updated), but building the graphic is separate, larger work, not started here.

**Implications:** this is the third instance this session of the same failure shape — a field or condition assumed to exist/match based on a sibling implementation, never actually checked against the real payload, silently degrading rather than erroring.
