The `isLive` bug in particular is the highest-consequence of the three found today: it doesn't just leave a UI element blank (D-127, D-128's `currentTeam`), it flips a boolean that gates which badge renders and whether a game is even eligible for the home hero, for a real fraction of every single live game's runtime.

## D-130 — NCAAF live field-position graphic shipped; live game viewer now at full NFL parity

**Status:** shipped (`js/ncaaf.js`, `js/ncaafLiveGame.js`). Live-verified against a real in-progress game (UAPB @ MIZ, event 401856663) by injecting real captured `/summary` + `/scoreboard` data into the actual render functions in a real browser (headless Chrome via CDP) and screenshotting the result — not just simulated in isolation. Tested a possession-by-away-team case specifically (UAPB, not the home team MIZ), exercising the field math's less-common branch. Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-03

**Trigger:** owner asked to build NCAAF live-game-viewer parity with NFL — field viewer, stats, in-depth info — as a direct follow-on to D-129's live verification.

**What shipped:**
- `fetchNCAAFLiveSituation(eventId)` in `js/ncaaf.js` — raw situation + home/away team-id pair from `/scoreboard`, mirroring `js/nfl.js`'s `fetchNFLLiveSituation` exactly, including bypassing `espnNCAAFFetch`'s `ApiCache` layer with a plain `fetch()` (the field viewer polls every 20s; going through the 5-minute-TTL cached helper would have re-served the same stale situation for 5 minutes at a time — caught before shipping, not after).
- `_nclgFieldViewerHtml`/`_nclgPlayArrowHtml` in `js/ncaafLiveGame.js` — a verbatim logic port of `js/nflLiveGame.js`'s D-105 field viewer (ball marker, first-down line, red zone shading, home-anchored yardLine math, play-arrow trajectory by play type, timeout dots with a flash-on-change animation). Deliberately a port, not a shared cross-file function: the source reads/writes NFL's own `_nlg.lastPlayArrowId`/`_nlg.lastTimeouts` module state, and calling it directly from NCAAF's file would let switching between an NFL game and an NCAAF game in the same session corrupt each other's animation-trigger state. `_nclg` gained the same three fields (`situation`, `lastPlayArrowId`, `lastTimeouts`) NFL's `_nlg` already has. Team color comes from `_nclgTeamColor(team)` (reads `competitor.team.color` directly) rather than NFL's static abbr→hex map — NCAAF has never needed that map (130+ FBS teams vs. NFL's 32), so this wasn't a new choice, just following the file's existing convention.
- Reuses `css/nflLiveGame.css`'s `.field-viewer`/`.fv-*` classes verbatim — that file loads site-wide in `index.html` regardless of active sport (same discovery this file's own header comment already notes for `.nlg-*`/`.gv-*`), so zero new CSS was needed.
- `_nclgRenderHeader` now builds the same `.nlg-situation` plain-text line + `.field-viewer` graphic NFL's header does, gated by the same "absent degrades to nothing" rule (only renders when `_nclg.situation` resolved, the game is live, `down`/`yardLine` are real numbers, and `possession` resolves to one of the two competing teams).

**Live verification method, since this sandbox cannot reach ESPN directly (Akamai WAF blocks its outbound IP regardless of headers — same constraint noted elsewhere in this codebase) but D-129 already proved `sportstrata.cc/api/ncaaf` works:** fetched real `/summary` and `/scoreboard` data for the live game via the production proxy, loaded the actual site in headless Chrome, and used the DevTools protocol to inject that real data directly into `_nclgRender`/`_nclg.situation` and call the real render pipeline — not a mock or a hand-written approximation of the DOM. The resulting screenshot showed a correct field (green turf, yard numbers, away-left/home-right endzones with real team logos/colors), a correctly-positioned ball marker and scrimmage/first-down lines, a "Golden Lions ball" possession banner, working timeout dot rows, and the last-play situation card, all sourced from the live game's actual numbers at check time (3rd & 8 at UAPB 27, UAPB in possession).

**Live game viewer sports parity audit (the "stats, in depth info" half of the ask):** diffed every top-level render function between `js/nflLiveGame.js` and `js/ncaafLiveGame.js`. Only two NFL functions have no NCAAF counterpart, and both are intentional, already-decided exclusions, not gaps: an injuries card (D-125 already investigated and ruled out — no free NCAAF injury data source exists) and the entire Fantasy tab (no NCAAF fantasy feature exists). Everything else — Summary/Play-by-Play/Box Score/Team Stats/Analytics tabs, win probability chart, game leaders, game flow, conference standings sidebar — was already at parity as of the Phase 2 build (2026-08-29); this session's field viewer was the one real remaining gap.

**Implications:** the NCAAF live game viewer is now feature-complete relative to NFL's, modulo the two deliberate, documented exclusions above. Combined with D-129's `isLive`/situation-synthesis fixes, tonight's live NCAAF games are the first ones where the home hero, the Scores grid's LIVE badge, and this field viewer all function correctly rather than silently degrading.

## D-131 — CLAUDE.md corrected: NCAAF player leaders/detail has been live since D-044, not "deferred" as it claimed for two months

**Status:** doc-only fix (`CLAUDE.md`). Re-verified live end to end in production before correcting the claim: `sportstrata.cc/api/ncaafstats` (real leader, Riley Warzynski, 535 passing yards), `sportstrata.cc/api/ncaafathlete?id=5404620` (full bio + season stats), `sportstrata.cc/ncaaf/player/5404620/riley-warzynski` (real SEO title, 200), and the actual client-rendered page via `wrangler pages dev` + headless Chrome (hero, Player Profile table, Passing/Rushing stat blocks, and a working Season Profile radar chart). Not yet committed at the time this entry was written.
**Contributors:** Claude
**Date:** 2026-09-03

**Trigger:** continuing the NCAAF parity work from D-129/D-130, checked `js/ncaaf.js` for other NFL-parity functions and found `showNCAAFPlayer`/`displayNCAAFPlayerDetail`, fully routed (`ncaaf-player-{id}`) and backed by real Functions (`functions/api/ncaafstats.js`, `functions/api/ncaafathlete.js`) and a real SEO template (`functions/ncaaf/player/[id]/[[slug]].js`) — directly contradicting CLAUDE.md's own "Player leaders/detail were deferred (CFB player data too sparse)" claim, repeated in two places.

**What actually happened, per git history and DECISIONS.md's own record:** D-042 (2026-07-06) deferred player leaders/detail at first, assuming CFB player data was too sparse. The same day, D-044 checked that assumption directly, found ESPN's core API has full leader and per-athlete data by id, and built the feature — hero, season stat groups by position, a game log, a game-trend chart, and a "Season Profile" radar chart normalized as % of the FBS leader (an honest-precision choice: the radar was "deliberately deferred" one commit earlier specifically until a defensible normalization baseline existed, then shipped once it did). None of this ever made it back into CLAUDE.md's "Sport Focus" summary, which kept citing D-042's original, since-reversed assumption for two months — including in this session's own opening context. DECISIONS.md itself had already flagged the exact shape of this problem once, in the NCAAB scoping entry: *"worth being honest that NCAAF's own 'too sparse' assumption turned out wrong once actually checked... so NCAAB's deferral should get the same live check early rather than being assumed permanent"* — but that self-aware note never got folded back into the summary doc it was correcting.

**Fixed:** both CLAUDE.md mentions (the Sport Focus intro paragraph and the NCAAF Data Sources line) now state the real, current, live-verified status, and the Key Files row for `js/ncaaf.js` gained a line naming the actual functions. The injuries/depth-chart deferral (D-125) is now explicitly distinguished in the same paragraph from this one — that gap really is "no free data exists," this one was "an untested two-month-old assumption," and conflating them was part of what let this drift.

**Implications:** this is the fourth instance this session of "the docs/assumption said X, live data said otherwise" (D-127's push-alert, D-128's `currentTeam`, D-129's `isLive`/situation), but the first that's purely a documentation bug rather than a code bug — the feature itself was built correctly the same day it was scoped, and has presumably been working in production the whole two months; only the summary doc future sessions read first was wrong. Worth treating CLAUDE.md's "Sport Focus" claims as a hypothesis to spot-check against real code/data when a decision hinges on them, same discipline this session applied everywhere else, rather than as ground truth by default.
